# lonewrt: OpenWrt firmware template for the ZTE MF289F, spec

Revision 2026-11-25 · code bb1ea7509111cf739036a5c763580e068cf9a6aa · format spec-writing 0.8

## Problem

An LTE router often stands where nobody is around to look after it.
The modem can hang, the data package expires, the SMS store fills up, and a button reset erases the whole configuration.
Without help this ends in long outages that the owner learns about from the people on site.
The image gives a router that comes back configured after a flash and after a button reset, repairs the usual link failures by itself and leaves a trail for diagnosis.
The same code, built from a profile without secrets, gives an image anyone with the same router model can use.
Building it needs a Linux machine, which many people with this router do not have, so they also get a way to build in their own GitHub account from a browser.

## User

- **The owner** builds the image, flashes it and manages the router remotely over a WireGuard tunnel or Tailscale, and optionally over Telegram and SMS.
- **People on site** use the WiFi only; when something fails they may press the reset button.
- **Someone who gets a router with the generic image** has the same router model and their own SIM; they change settings in LuCI (the router's web interface).
- **Someone without a Linux machine**, for example a reader of an OpenWrt forum, has an MF289F that runs OpenWrt and wants an image of their own profile; they have a web browser, a GitHub account and LuCI.
- **A coding agent** that works for an owner builds and checks the image, following the project's agent skill.

## Goal

The owner flashes an image built from a chosen profile onto an MF289F.
The router then comes up by itself, after the flash and after every button reset, with working LTE, WiFi and, when the profile enables it, the tunnel.
An image from a private profile is accepted by the checklist after a flash and a reset test at the router; the generic image by a trial on a router with another operator's SIM (see Acceptance).
Someone without a Linux machine gets the same kind of image from a GitHub repository of their own. The build on GitHub, the configurator and the agent skill are accepted by trials the owner runs on a second GitHub account (see Acceptance).
With Tailscale on, the owner reaches LuCI and SSH by name from their own devices after one sign-in, accepted by point 10 of Acceptance.
Auto is the default for a clean flash or reset. The remaining acceptance and hardware questions are under Open.
Its usefulness shows in the first long period without a visit: did link failures end without a person.

## What it does

- **S1.** The owner builds an image from a profile; the generic image holds no secrets and no private data, and private profiles and images never reach git. When passwords are left empty, the build writes a card with the passwords and hints for the person who gets the router. (R1 to R6, R33, R33a)
- **S2.** After a flash or a button reset the router comes up with the settings of its profile, without anyone logging in. (R7 to R9)
- **S3.** At every start the router puts the modem back to LTE-only mode, the profile's bands and the right SMS store, whatever an earlier test left behind; when the profile gives no APN, the router finds one by itself. (R10 to R13)
- **S4.** The watcher tries to restore a dropped native IPv4 call in place. If IPv6 still works and IPv4 cannot be restored in place, the router preserves IPv6; IPv4 can remain unavailable until a restart. A full outage follows the interface restart and router restart ladder. (session repair and escalation, R49 to R49i; link repair, R14 to R21)
- **S5.** When a restart does not bring LTE back, the router switches to slow 2G (EDGE) between restarts so the owner keeps remote access; after the restarts are used up it stays on EDGE and tries LTE again from time to time. (R22 to R25)
- **S6.** Operator SMS (top-ups, package expiry) keep arriving, because the router cleans the SMS store by itself and archives the whole store before it deletes anything. (R26)
- **S7.** The owner sees in the logs and in LuCI what happened: the signal every few minutes, the course of a failure, the modem state, the data usage. A profile can turn on a debug log level for a test period: a longer persistent log, the signal samples in it and every step of the data session repair. (R28 to R30, R29c)
- **S8.** The owner reaches the router over a WireGuard tunnel; in the generic image the tunnel is prepared but disabled. (R31 to R32)
- **S9.** The Telegram bot relays SMS, checks the balance, announces recovery and answers questions about the router, WiFi and connected devices. The owner can name devices, run a speed test and request a restart. Approved numbers can ask for status or restart by SMS. (notifications and control, R34 to R48, R50, R51)
- **S10.** Someone without a Linux machine puts their profile into a private GitHub repository of their own as one secret, starts the build there for the lonewrt release they choose, and downloads the image to flash in LuCI or with a coding agent; the profile sets both passwords, so every build gives the same ones, and it can hold SSH keys for login; a public repository stops the build before anything is uploaded, except a test run with made-up values. (R52 to R55, R61)
- **S11.** The offline configurator shows the profile keys relevant to the chosen options and fills in new keys when loading an older profile. It checks values, draws passwords, previews operator balance replies and gives the profile as text or a file. (profile editor, R56 to R58a)
- **S12.** A coding agent with the agent skill leads its person from no profile to a checked image, built on their machine or on GitHub, without showing a secret of the profile in the chat, and flashes it only after the person says yes, with a backup first. (R59 to R59g)
- **S13.** With Tailscale turned on in the profile, the owner adds the router to their own Tailscale account with one sign-in link: the bot sends it after the flash, and an SMS command or SSH gives it too; a key in the profile adds the router without a click instead. The owner then reaches LuCI and SSH by the router's name from their own devices, with no server of their own. The bot says how to connect, when the sign-in ends and when a reset left an old entry, and the router's LAN can be made reachable as well. (R63 to R71)
- **S14.** In Auto the router uses what the operator gives, including IPv4 through translation on IPv6-only connections. WiFi devices get IPv6 when it is available, and the owner can force either version in LuCI or keep the selected mode through an upgrade. (R7c, R73 to R73p, R74 to R76k, R77 to R77e)

## What it does not do

The image does not have these functions and does not cross these lines:

- It does not update the modem firmware.
- It does not recognise the data package, count used GB or raise an alarm during a link failure, because without internet nothing reaches Telegram; the alarm comes from a separate heartbeat on a server (R42), and the router sends a message only once the internet is back (R40).
- It changes nothing on the WireGuard server.
- It does not update itself from LuCI; a new OpenWrt version is a new image build.
- It sends no SMS other than the balance query of the operator preset (with the shipped preset `29702`: `RACUN` to 1466) and the replies to SMS commands, and no USSD codes; the bot changes no state of the router other than the 2G message switch, a restart confirmed with a button, device names and starting a Tailscale sign-in. (R27, R37a)
- The generic image holds no secrets and no data of a private profile. (R4)
- The image's own scripts use AT for modem control, with the session watcher as the QMI exception (modem access, R12c; watcher gate, R49a).
- Over IPv6 it lets no new connection from the internet in to the router or the WiFi devices. (R75, R75a)
- It does not remember that an operator refused IPv6; it asks again at every start. (R73c)
- On a SIM that gives IPv6 only, the devices get IPv4 for their own connections only: nothing from the internet reaches them over IPv4 there.
- Loss of one IP version alone does not schedule a full restart while the other still works (working-family protection, D200).
- A button reset keeps no logs, snapshot, SMS archive or failure state; after a reset the link repair counts from zero. (R7b)
- It shows people on site nothing about a failure.
- Releases hold no built image: everyone builds an image from their own profile. (D96)
- The configurator loads nothing from outside its file, sends nothing anywhere and keeps nothing in the browser. (R58, R56c)
- It never builds a profile in a public GitHub repository, except a run the person marks as a test. (R54)
- The build on GitHub only leaves a download (R53); the agent skill flashes only after the person's yes to that flash (R59b).
- It builds for the MF289F only. Another router is a port in a fork, which `docs/porting.md` guides (R62).
- With Tailscale it is no exit node, has no Tailscale SSH and no Tailscale page in LuCI, and cannot be signed out from the chat; a button reset and a flash that does not keep settings make it a new entry in the account. (D144)

## How it works

**Build.** The official OpenWrt ImageBuilder puts the image together from the system packages, a few modem packages and the project's own files.
The settings of one router (address, WiFi, tunnel, passwords, notifications) live in a **profile**: a private profile may hold secrets, the generic one holds none.
Before the build a script fills the project's files with the values of the profile, so the same code gives either image.

**First start.** A flash and a button reset erase changes made on the router, but the files of the image stay.
At the first start a one-time script sets the network, WiFi, tunnel, firewall and passwords from them, so a reset brings back the configured router, not bare OpenWrt.

**Modem.** The network mode, the bands and the SMS store live in the modem's own memory, and an OpenWrt reset does not touch them.
So a start script checks and fixes them at every boot.
OpenWrt sets up the data connection over QMI (the modem control protocol), and the project's scripts use only AT commands (the modem's text commands), one at a time, under a shared lock; only the data session watcher (below) also speaks QMI, to restart a call that netifd does not know is dead.
The build adapts the OpenWrt QMI handler so netifd can set up the IP versions below (QMI handler derivation, R12d).

**IP versions.** The internet comes in two versions, IPv4 and IPv6, and an operator may give either or both.
Auto asks for both IP versions and uses what the operator grants.
On IPv6-only mobile service, 464XLAT provides IPv4 access: CLAT runs on the router and the operator runs the other half. The LAN gets IPv6 behind the router's firewall when a usable prefix is available.
The router withdraws its LAN IPv6 default after a confirmed loss and resumes advertising when a usable prefix returns (LAN withdrawal and return, R74c, R74f).

**Data session watcher.** When the mobile network detaches the modem and the modem attaches again by itself, netifd keeps showing `wwan` as up, but the call of the modem is dead and nothing in OpenWrt notices.
The watcher checks the native IPv4 call and tries to restore it in place. When IPv4 is healthy it also checks a published IPv6 call so the LAN can follow a confirmed loss or return (session repair and IPv6 observation, R49b, R49c, R49i).
A failed IPv4 restore can escalate to an interface restart. With a published IPv6 sibling the watcher needs a current-session check confirming IPv6 silence before escalating (escalation guard, R49d).

**Link repair.** Watchcat checks cellular reachability and can restart the interface. The recovery ladder handles longer outages and counts answers over either IP version (interface restart and family checks, R14, R77).
The ladder recognises a used-up package or a SIM locked with a PIN and stops restarting sooner.
If restarting fails to recover LTE, the ladder can use EDGE until it can try LTE again (EDGE fallback, R22, R23).

**Ways to build.** Every way starts from the same profile, and `build.sh` decides whether a profile is valid. A build on GitHub also needs both passwords set in the profile.
On a Linux machine the owner runs `build.sh` directly.
Without one, a person fills in the configurator, a web page in a single file that runs in the browser and sends nothing, and stores the resulting text as a secret of a private GitHub repository of their own, made from a small template the project offers.
A GitHub Actions workflow in that repository fetches the lonewrt release the person names, builds the image on GitHub's machines (runners), runs the tests, and hands back the image as a download (an artifact); the person flashes it in LuCI, or lets a coding agent do it.
A coding agent can lead through the same steps with the agent skill, uses the profile without showing its secrets in the chat, and flashes only when the person says yes.

**Tailscale.** Tailscale is a VPN service whose free plan joins a person's devices into one private network, their account. The devices find each other through Tailscale's servers, so it works even where the operator shares one public address among many SIMs (CGNAT) and nothing from outside can connect to the router.
With `TS_ENABLED='1'` the build adds the official OpenWrt package, which roughly halves the free flash.
A small service of the image sets Tailscale up at every start, and the router's firewall lets the owner's devices in but lets the router start nothing towards them; `docs/tailscale.md` shows how to make the account itself enforce the same, so even a stolen router reaches nothing.
A router with no Tailscale state (after a flash or a reset) asks Tailscale for a sign-in link, and the bot posts it once; whoever signs in with it adds the router to their account.
The bot then says how to connect and reminds the owner to turn off the end of the sign-in for the router, which Tailscale otherwise applies after some months.

## Example

The last line of the signal log and the end of a short failure, as the logs show them (illustrative values):

```
$ ssh root@<router> tail -n 1 /tmp/lw-signal.log
2025-01-09_12:30:03 zsnt=6 net=LTE-A_B3_(1800_MHz)_/_B1_(2100_MHz) pb=B3(1800MHz) s1=B1(2100MHz) rsrp=-85dBm rsrq=-14dB sinr=5.4dB rssi=-53dBm pci=157 loss=0% rtt=36.459 wg_age=3s
$ ssh root@<router> 'grep "wwan-recover: " /root/openwrt.log | tail -n 3'
Thu Jan  9 11:30:00 2025 daemon.notice wwan-recover: offline (tx+0 rx+0, operator DNS silent or skipped, watchdog this boot=0) cycle=3 reboots=0 class=hang mode=lte uptime=86100
Thu Jan  9 11:35:00 2025 daemon.notice wwan-recover: offline (tx+0 rx+0, operator DNS silent or skipped, watchdog this boot=0) cycle=4 reboots=0 class=hang mode=lte uptime=86400
Thu Jan  9 11:40:01 2025 daemon.notice wwan-recover: online on LTE, failure over after 4 cycles and 0 reboots cycle=4 reboots=0 class=hang mode=lte uptime=86701
```

## Key decisions

- **Configuration in the image:** a button reset brings back the configured router; in exchange, a lasting change of settings needs a new build and a flash. (D2)
- **LTE only, no automatic mode:** the router does not fall to 2G by itself and get stuck there, and with dead LTE it gets EDGE right after the first failed restart, at the cost of short breaks in access at the following restarts. This holds for the generic image too, with no switch in LuCI. (D3, D30)
- **Session watcher:** a data session dropped by the network is started again in place within seconds instead of after minutes of watchcat and a restart of the interface; in exchange the image has one resident script that asks the modem over QMI every 5 s. (D80)
- **Secrets in the private image:** the image is ready to flash with no further steps, but the image file and its profile must be guarded like a password and kept out of git. (D15, D20)
- **Building without Linux in your own private GitHub repository, no ready images:** a person gets an image of their own profile without a Linux machine, at the cost of a GitHub account that holds the profile's secrets; releases ship no image, because an image without a profile has no bot and gives everyone the same passwords. The configurator that writes the profile sends nothing, since its form holds those secrets. (D96, D97, D104)
- **Tailscale, joined by a link from the bot:** the owner reaches the router from their own devices without running a server, and the link needs no secret in the profile (a key for joining without a click is optional); in exchange it depends on Tailscale's servers and has about half the free flash, and every reset needs a new sign-in that leaves an old entry in the account. (D133, D134, D140, D145)
- **Automatic IP versions:** Auto asks for IPv6 at every start so operators can enable it without a profile change. The release relies on bench evidence for networks the owner cannot try (retry and default choices, D179, D211).
- **Automatic IPv4 translation:** IPv6-only operators can offer IPv4 through OpenWrt’s translator. Existing interface events and the recovery cycle drive it without another job or setting (translation choices, D209, D212, D213).
- **Existing IPv4 path:** translation adds no process or call on a router that uses native IPv4 and has no previous translation to clean up, preserving the owner's usual path (D210).

## Open

The following questions need an owner decision for the release. The remaining items track follow-up work.

- **O11 Release acceptance scope** (after the read, revision 2026-11-25): the Auto release rule requires all of Acceptance 12. The individual VM scenarios for routed and delegated LAN IPv6, IPv6 during NAT64 failure, LAN configuration isolation, IPv6 firewall traffic, kept upgrades, watchcat entry isolation, IPv6 traffic-based repair and early IPv4 publication are absent; early publication is a conditional design (LAN and firewall rules, R74, R74d, R74e, R75; upgrade, R7e; link checks, R14b, R77d; early publication, R73p). Proposal: gate release on the named VM scenarios and scripted withdrawal checks that exist, and keep the missing scenarios as explicit evidence gaps. Build any missing check the owner still wants to gate release.
- **O12 Auto start time** (after the read, revision 2026-11-25): does release with Auto require the hardware start-time comparison and a count of profile writes before it ships? One live IPv4-only start does not establish the timing bound. Proposal: require five alternating starts per mode on the IPv4-only MF289F and record profile writes in the same run. Keep early publication conditional on missing the limit (timing and fallback rules, R73i, R73p; default and release, R73k; Acceptance 13).
- **O13 Modem profile writes on repeated failure** (after the read, revision 2026-11-25): a SIM or APN that gives no call can cause up to four profile writes per failed start, for the three PDP types and rollback, while netifd keeps retrying (profile writes and rollback, R73f, R73n; from: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/P2a-review.md, risk 3). Proposal: count writes on a bounded all-refused run before accepting the risk. If limiting writes is necessary, retain the last type after a bounded number of consecutive failures and test recovery after changing SIM or APN.
- **O14 IPv4 loss with working IPv6** (after the read, revision 2026-11-25): if in-place restore fails, IPv4 can stay unavailable until a person restarts the session while IPv6 is still working. Proposal: keep the working IPv6 session for 0.7.0 and document that the owner may have to restart it. Any timed escalation needs its own limit and a test that measures interruption of IPv6 (recovery and working-family protection, R49d, R77b, D200).
- **O15 IPv6 traffic in the repair ladder** (after the read, revision 2026-11-25): the implemented traffic rule has review evidence and awaits owner approval. Proposal: approve keeping ongoing IPv6 traffic as evidence against a silence restart while allowing scheduled EDGE restarts and LTE probes (traffic rule and proposal, R77d, D214).
- **O16 Public evidence and personal details** (after the read, revision 2026-11-25): private report paths and personal SIM, APN or clock details make some evidence inaccessible and expose details readers do not need. Proposal: name the router and test setup in the spec, cite public or repository-local evidence where available, and keep private source paths and personal details in the project notes.

### Follow-up questions

- **O6 IPv6-only operators:** Some networks may offer translation prefixes the image cannot use or may give different settings through the modem and the network. Proposal: retain the current /96 limit for this release, verify it with a real IPv6-only operator and document the IPv6-only result if its prefix is unsupported. Confirm the link-only settings path for IPv6-only and modem report preference for dual stack using the checks under Unverified. This would replace the modem-first choice (D194).
- **O7 DNS discovery and shared routes:** A temporary route to the operator's DNS server can affect other traffic or survive an interrupted lookup. Proposal: retain existing routes and the current temporary route for discovery, then test gateway changes, interruption and concurrent routing before changing that behaviour.
- **O8 Repair files:** The recovery lock and traffic sample use files in the shared temporary directory. Proposal: move both under the root-owned runtime directory after checking takeover of a stuck lock and how traffic samples are read.
- **O9 Translation ownership:** The router currently has one creator for the translation interface and cleans up only its own resources. Proposal: retain that arrangement and check interrupted setup and coexistence with another translation before extending it. Retain discovery from the operator's DNS and the IPv6-only eligibility rule from the DNS64-only and native-IPv4 eligibility decision (D186).
- **O10 WireGuard packet size over translation:** The largest packet that fits the real operator path is unknown. Proposal: keep the image setting at 1400, measure the path with an IPv6-only SIM and both tunnel endpoints, then have the owner decide whether to change either endpoint.

---

Part 2 is for the builder and the maintainer.

## Requirements

### Terms

Paths starting with `/` are on the router; the others are relative to the folder of this file.

- **profile**: the file `profiles/<name>.conf` with variables and an optional folder `profiles/<name>/` with files (SSH keys); `generic.conf` holds no secrets, a private profile may. The generic image (profile `generic`, `build.sh generic`) and an image from a private profile are `sysupgrade` images built from these profiles.
- **cycle**: one run of `wwan-recover` from cron, every 5 minutes.
- **CLAT helper**, **translation check** and **CLAT session identity**: the helper is the live process identified by the stock PID file or the saved PID used for cleanup (R76i). The translation check is `lw-clat-check`, which manages `wwan_6_4`. Its IPv6 session identity is the call client and handle plus the setup uptime from the identity record (R12d).
- **session**, **family**, **auto** and **forced**: the session is what one start of the interface `wwan` sets up with the network, until the interface goes down; its families are the IP versions it carries, IPv4 and IPv6. Auto, forced IPv4 and forced IPv6 are the values of `network.wwan.pdptype` of the IP family rule (R73).
- **native IPv4**, **translated IPv4** and **IPv4 service**: native IPv4 is an IPv4 call granted at the start, with its address on the parent or through the lease of `wwan_4`. The session retains that classification if the call is lost (R76b). Translated IPv4 carries IPv4 over the session's IPv6 on its own device (R76). For family checks, IPv4 service exists with native IPv4 or while netifd reports `wwan_6_4` up. That report alone does not show that the operator's translator answers.
- **cellular answer**: a reply to a ping that went out through the session's device for the address's family: from 8.8.8.8 or 1.1.1.1 through the native IPv4 device or the translation device, from 2001:4860:4860::8888 or 2606:4700:4700::1111 (the IPv6 addresses of the same two public DNS services, from: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/E-astra-report.md, section 2 "Probes", 2026-11-23) through the session's IPv6 device. A reply that came over another interface (the WireGuard tunnel, Tailscale, the LAN) is none.
- **family check** and **online**: the family check is one round of pings that, for each family the session has, pings that family's two addresses in turn and stops for that family at its first answer; it gives a result per family and a combined one. Each check that runs it keeps the packet count and time limit it has for its IPv4 addresses (watchcat by the watchcat restart rule (R14), `wwan-recover` 2 packets with a 3 s limit, and so on), and its IPv6 addresses get the same. Online is a cellular answer in one family check.
- **family lost**: a family of the session that got no cellular answer in 3 scheduled family checks of watchcat (R14) in a row while another family answered; family checks of other parts of the image do not count. It is a state the log reports (R77b), not a proof that the call is gone.
- **Confirmed in-modem IPv6 call loss** is the watcher's status and settings confirmation for the published IPv6 call while native IPv4 is healthy. It changes the session's loss flag (watcher confirmation, R49b, R49i).
- **Loss of usable LAN IPv6** is a confirmed absence of a usable IPv6 call, LAN assignment or source-specific cellular route for a previously served managed LAN. This triggers withdrawal even though the modem might still report a call (LAN withdrawal, R74c, R74f).
- **Served managed LAN** is the image-managed LAN while odhcpd is serving its usable IPv6 assignment. **Reconciliation** evaluates the current session and committed settings and requests any necessary change to the running advertisements. An **eligible event** is one of the reconciliation triggers in the LAN event rule (R74f). **Phase** is the last applied configuration, and **pending replay** re-evaluates and retries an owed change (RAM state and apply rules, R14h, R74g).
- **Evidence names**: C1 is initial CLAT support, C2 is its VM verification and prefix recovery, and L1 is LAN IPv6 withdrawal. F1 is guarded repair state, F2 adds the IPv6 traffic measure, and F3 fixes the final repair review findings. Reviews 2a and 2b cover modem negotiation and the family-aware watcher respectively; review 3a covers initial LAN IPv6. The third expert review covers repair state (`../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/E3-astra-report.md`).
- **expert review**: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/E-astra-report.md, the design review of the IP family work of 2026-11-23, which decision rows D171 to D192 cite by finding and section; its recommendations are taken into the spec on the owner's go for the overnight work of that day. The **second expert review** is ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/E2-astra-report.md, 2026-11-23, of this spec after its first read; decision rows D195 to D203 cite it by item, and its changes are taken in as rulings of the session that runs the work.
- **LTE-only mode** and **failure**: the router is in LTE-only mode when there is no EDGE marker in `/tmp` and `network.wwan.modes=lte`; a failure is the run of cycles from the first cycle that is not online to the first online cycle in LTE-only mode, so a cycle on EDGE belongs to the failure.
- **code** and **work folder**: `build.sh`, `src/` (scripts and templates), `profiles/` and `image/` are in this folder; the ImageBuilder and the downloaded packages are in the work folder, which `build.sh` takes from the variable `LW_WORK`, by default `work/` in this folder (ignored by git). `fetch-deps.sh` downloads everything there with pinned sha256 sums (D35).
- **limit** and **limit cycle**: the limit is the number of restarts from the limit rule (R19); the limit cycle is the first cycle that is not online after the restart that reached this number.
- **operator DNS** and **AT port**: the operator DNS is the first server from the native IPv4 lease on `wwan_4`, else the first IPv4 server on an up parent with a static native IPv4 address when the child is absent, else a server from the session's IPv6 settings. The AT port is `/dev/ttyUSB2` for the image's scripts and `/dev/ttyUSB1` for LuCI and manual work, both of the same modem.
- **`<r>`** and **`<ladder>`**: `<r>` is `ssh root@<router LAN address>` (the cable connection is in `README.md`); `<ladder>` is the recovery ladder tests `tests/ladder/`, run by `tests/run-all.sh ladder`.
- **`<tests>`** and **`<vm>`**: `<tests>` is `tests/run-all.sh`, which runs every test `tests/<area>/*.sh` in the official OpenWrt 25.12.6 x86_64 rootfs (the same busybox, `uci`, `jsonfilter` and coreutils `timeout` as on the router) under `bwrap`, with stand-ins for the hardware from `tests/stubs/`: an AT modem with a state model, `ping` and `nslookup` separately for LTE and EDGE, `logger`, `reboot`, `ifup`, the uptime. `<vm>` is `tests/vm/`: an image with the same files built for x86_64 and run in QEMU, with a stand-in AT modem on a serial port and a fake `wwan`. Neither of them imitates the ipq40xx hardware, QMI (apart from two `uqmi` stand-ins in `<tests>`: one for the watcher, and a stateful one with modem profiles, clients and calls per family, against which `<tests>` runs the image's `qmi.sh` derived from the cached `uqmi` package, R73) or the operator network.
- **operator preset**: a data file `/usr/share/lw/operators/<MCC-MNC>` with everything that belongs to one mobile operator (R47); the format is in `docs/operators.md`. The image ships `29702` (Telekom Montenegro); requirement texts quote it as the example. A profile can carry one more in its `OP_*` keys (R47d).
- **user's repository**, **workflow**, **run** and **artifact**: the user's repository is a GitHub repository that a person makes from the project's template repository (R53h); the workflow is its GitHub Actions workflow that builds an image, a run is one execution of it on a GitHub-hosted runner, and the artifact is the download a run leaves. `LW_PROFILE` is a repository secret of the user's repository that holds the text of one profile file.
- **release**: a lonewrt version published as a GitHub Release of the public repository with the tag `v<VERSION>` (R3b), for example `v0.1.0`.
- **configurator** and **agent skill**: the configurator is the HTML file of the configurator rule (R56); the agent skill is the instruction file for a coding agent of the skill rule (R59), at `skills/lonewrt/SKILL.md` (R59d).
- **secret values** and **run fixture**: the secret values of a profile are the values of `WIFI_KEY`, `ROOT_PASSWORD`, `ROOT_PASSWORD_HASH`, `WWAN_PASSWORD`, `WWAN_PIN`, `WG_PRIVATE_KEY`, `TG_BOT_TOKEN`, `HC_PING_URL`, `SMS_ADMINS` and `TS_AUTHKEY`, the keys `docs/profile.md` names as secrets or private data. The run fixture is `tests/build/fixtures/fake-full.conf` with its `@FAKE_...@` placeholders filled the way `tests/build/lib.bash` fills them, so `build.sh` accepts it.
- **tailscale image**, **account**, **fresh Tailscale state** and **added**: a tailscale image is built from a profile with `TS_ENABLED='1'`; the account is the private Tailscale network (tailnet) of the person who signed the router in; the router has fresh Tailscale state when `/etc/tailscale/` holds no state of a node, which is the case after every flash that does not keep settings (`sysupgrade -n`, LuCI without "Keep settings") and after every button reset, and stays so across restarts until a sign-in; the router is added while Tailscale reports it signed in to an account (backend state `Running`, see the interface facts of the Tailscale section).
- **sign-in**: a change of the router from not added to added; a restart of the router or of Tailscale is not one.
- **auth key** and **tag**: an auth key is a key made on Tailscale's admin page (`tskey-auth-…`) that signs a device in without a click; in a profile it is `TS_AUTHKEY`. A tag is a label such as `tag:lonewrt` that a device gets in the admin page or from a tagged auth key, the key that adds it; the account's access policy can name a tag in its rules (R71b).

### Build and profiles

- **R1:** The image is built with the official OpenWrt 25.12.6 ImageBuilder for `ipq40xx/generic`, device profile `zte_mf289f`, with the kernel and system packages of the official release (D1).
- **R2:** The image holds the packages of the "Included" table in `packages.md` and none of the "Left out" table; that table is binding (D14, D26). A row marked "only with `TS_ENABLED='1'`" is in a tailscale image only (R63). The packages of the table "Removed from the default set" are not in any image (D54, D91, D167).
  - **R2a:** The `.apk` files from outside the official feeds (group `extra` of `deps.lst`) go into the ImageBuilder only as `deps.lst` pins them: each listed file must be in `dl/extra` with its pinned sha256, checked by `build.sh` right before the copy, and `dl/extra` holds no other `.apk`. A missing, altered or unlisted file stops the build with the file's name. Before the copy, `build.sh` removes from the ImageBuilder's `packages/` the other versions of those same packages, matched by the exact package name (`modemband` is not `modemband-5g`), and nothing else; a package pinned twice in `deps.lst` stops the build with both file names (D77).
  - *scenario* given a pinned `.apk` changed by one byte, or an extra `.apk` in `dl/extra`, when the owner runs `build.sh`, then the build ends with an error naming the file, before the ImageBuilder starts
- **R3:** `build.sh <name>` builds the image from `profiles/<name>.conf`. A key of `generic.conf` missing from the profile, a key not in `generic.conf`, a key given twice, a malformed value, or an empty value of a key that may not be empty stops the build with the key's name (never its value) and no new image. Keys that may be empty: `WIFI_KEY`, `ROOT_PASSWORD` and `ROOT_PASSWORD_HASH` (R33, R33b), `WWAN_APN`, `WWAN_AUTH`, `WWAN_USERNAME`, `WWAN_PASSWORD`, `WWAN_PIN` (R13e), `LTE_BANDS`, the `WG_*` keys except `WG_ENABLED`, `TG_BOT_TOKEN`, `TG_CHAT_ID`, `LOW_BALANCE`, `HC_PING_URL`, `SMS_ADMINS`, `SSH_KEYS`, `TIMEZONE` (R3c), the `OP_*` keys (R47d) and `TS_AUTHKEY` (R66) (D20, D35, D43, D57, D61, D114, D127, D131, D132). `TS_ENABLED` and `TS_ADVERTISE_LAN` must be exactly `0` or `1`. With `WG_ENABLED='1'`, `WG_PRIVATE_KEY`, `WG_ADDRESS`, `WG_PEER_PUBLIC_KEY` and `WG_ENDPOINT_HOST` must be set. `SMS_ADMINS` outside its format (a comma list, each number with an optional `+` and 6 to 15 digits, no spaces) stops the build, and so does `LINK_RESTART_AFTER_S` outside its range (R14a). `LOG_LEVEL` must be exactly `info` or `debug` (lower case); any other value, `DEBUG` and an empty one included, stops the build with the key's name and the two values (R29c, D81).
  - *scenario* given a profile without a LAN address, when the owner runs `build.sh` with it, then the build ends with an error naming the key and there is no new file in `image/`
  - **R3a:** When the profile file holds a non-empty secret key (`WIFI_KEY`, `ROOT_PASSWORD`, `ROOT_PASSWORD_HASH`, `WWAN_PASSWORD`, `WWAN_PIN`, `WG_PRIVATE_KEY`, `TG_BOT_TOKEN`, `HC_PING_URL`, `TS_AUTHKEY`) or the profile's folder holds a private SSH host key (`dropbear_ed25519_host_key`, `dropbear_rsa_host_key`; not `authorized_keys`), and group or others can read that file, `build.sh` prints one warning that names the files and suggests `chmod 600`; the build goes on, and the warning never shows a value (D78).
  - **R3b:** The release version is the single line of the file `VERSION` at the repository root: `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` with an optional `-prerelease` suffix (`0.1.0`, `0.2.0-rc.1`), numbers without leading zeros. `build.sh` reads it for every build, with `--render-only` too; a missing file or any other content stops the build with the message `VERSION is missing` or `VERSION is not MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`, before ImageBuilder runs, and no new image. The value goes into `/etc/lw-build` (R7b) and into the summary line of the build (D94).
  - *scenario* given a `VERSION` file that holds `1.2`, when the owner runs `build.sh` with any profile, then the build ends with an error naming `VERSION` and there is no new file in `image/`
  - **R3c:** An empty `TIMEZONE` takes the POSIX string of `ZONENAME` from the zone table of `configurator/index.html` (the table of the time-zone picker rule (R56f)), so the image gets the string the configurator would fill in; a non-empty `TIMEZONE` is used as written. An empty `TIMEZONE` with a `ZONENAME` the table lacks stops the build with the message `key TIMEZONE is empty and ZONENAME is not in the configurator's list of time zones; set TIMEZONE`, and a missing page or table stops it with `no zone list` (D127).
    - *scenario* given a profile with `TIMEZONE=''` and `ZONENAME='America/New_York'`, when the owner runs `build.sh --render-only`, then the overlay's `zz-lw-config` sets the time zone `EST5EDT,M3.2.0,M11.1.0`; given `TIMEZONE=''` and `ZONENAME='Mars/Olympus'`, then the build ends with the message naming `TIMEZONE`
- **R4:** The generic image holds no WireGuard private key, WiFi password, root password hash, SSH host keys, `authorized_keys` or bot token of a private profile (D13).
  - *scenario* given the unpacked rootfs of the generic image (`unsquashfs`), when it is searched for each value of `tests/build/fixtures/fake-full.conf` and of `tests/build/private-values.txt` (when that file exists), then there is no hit
  - **R4a:** The generic image holds no public data of a private profile either: WiFi name, WireGuard server address or tunnel address (D13).
- **R5:** Profiles other than `generic.conf`, their folders and the folder `image/` are in this folder and ignored by git (D15, D20, D35).
  - *cmd* `git check-ignore image/x.bin profiles/mine.conf profiles/mine/` prints all three paths in order
- **R6:** A syntax error in any script of the image stops the build before the ImageBuilder starts and leaves no partial file in `image/`.
  - *scenario* given an image script with an unclosed `if`, when the owner runs the build, then the build ends with an error naming the file and there is no new image
- **R61:** The profile key `SSH_KEYS` holds public SSH keys for root's key login, so a profile carries them without its folder. Its value is empty or a list of keys separated by `;`, each `<type> <base64 data> [comment]`, with the type `ssh-ed25519`, `ssh-rsa` or `ecdsa-sha2-nistp256` and a comment without `;`. A malformed entry stops the build with the key's name and the entry's number, never its value. The image's `/etc/dropbear/authorized_keys` holds the keys of `SSH_KEYS` and of `profiles/<name>/authorized_keys`, each key once (D114).
  - *scenario* given `SSH_KEYS` with two made-up keys and a profile folder whose `authorized_keys` holds one of them and a third, when the owner renders the image, then its `authorized_keys` has the three keys once each; given an entry `ssh-ed25519 not-base64`, then the build stops naming `SSH_KEYS` and entry 1
- **R62:** `docs/porting.md` tells a coding agent how to move lonewrt to another OpenWrt router with an LTE modem, in a fork: what stays, what changes (target, modem protocol, ports and names, vendor AT commands, Wi-Fi radios, per-modem packages, recovery, the test models) and which measurements of this spec to take again on the new router; `README.md` and `AGENTS.md` link to it (D128).
  - *scenario* given the repository, when `tests/skill/run.sh` runs, then `docs/porting.md` exists and `README.md` and `AGENTS.md` link to it
- **R33:** When the profile leaves the WiFi or root password empty, the build draws it, writes it into the image (the root password as a `$6$` hash from `openssl passwd -6`) and writes it in clear text to `image/<name>-passwords.txt` (mode 0600) in the lines `ssid=`, `wifi=` and `root=`; when that file already exists, the build takes the passwords from it instead of drawing new ones (D21, D36).
  - *scenario* given `generic.conf` with empty passwords, when the owner builds the image twice, then `image/generic-passwords.txt` has the WiFi name and two passwords, the second build does not change it, and after flashing either image both passwords work
  - **R33b:** The root password can also be given in clear text as `ROOT_PASSWORD` (8 to 63 printable ASCII characters, as `WIFI_KEY`); the build hashes it the way it hashes a drawn one and writes only the hash into the image. Exactly one of `ROOT_PASSWORD` and `ROOT_PASSWORD_HASH` may be set: both set stops the build with an error naming the two keys; both empty keeps R33. When a card for the profile already exists, every build rewrites it from what the image holds, so it never names a password the image does not have: drawn values keep coming from the old card, a value the profile sets replaces the card's (`wifi=`, and `root=` with the profile's `ROOT_PASSWORD`), and for a profile that gives only `ROOT_PASSWORD_HASH` the card has no `root=` line and says that root uses the hash from the profile. When no card exists and nothing is drawn, no card is written. A rebuild keeps the password, not the hash: the hash gets a new salt each time, as for a drawn password. An image from a profile with `ROOT_PASSWORD` is private by the rule that holds for `ROOT_PASSWORD_HASH` (R4, D15). Neither the build output nor the image holds the clear text (D73).
  - *scenario* given a profile with `ROOT_PASSWORD='fake-root-pw-q7x9'`, when the owner builds the image, then the root password on the router is that password, and neither the build output nor the overlay holds it in clear text
  - **R33a:** The same file has hints below the passwords for the person who gets the router (in English): the LuCI address over HTTPS and the browser's certificate warning; that a button reset brings back the passwords of the card; that a SIM with a PIN needs the PIN removed or put into the profile (`WWAN_PIN`, R13e), because one entered in LuCI is lost at a button reset; that without internet 5 minutes after the start the APN is entered in LuCI; that the router works on LTE and sets it again at every start, switches to 2G (EDGE) only while LTE stays dead after a restart (R22, R24), and that a mode set in LuCI lasts until the next start; that the router restarts itself on a failure, and how to turn that off in LuCI's scheduled tasks; that above 30 SMS the router archives the whole store to `/root/sms-archive/` daily at 04:10 and deletes the oldest down to 20; that the WiFi country is changed in LuCI (D36); and that SSH warns of a changed host key after a flash or a button reset in which the router made a new key (R55), with the command that drops the old key from `known_hosts`.

### Ways to build

Someone without a Linux machine builds in the user's repository, with a profile written in the configurator; a coding agent follows the agent skill. The build itself is the one of the rules above.

- **R52:** A profile is the only input of every way to build: the workflow (R53), the configurator (R56) and the agent skill (R59) read and write profile files in the format of `docs/profile.md`, add no key that `generic.conf` lacks, and leave the decision whether a profile is valid to `build.sh` (R3), so a profile is accepted or refused alike on every way (D95). The one exception is the password rule of a run (R53i), which refuses a profile that `build.sh` accepts.
- **R53:** A run takes the whole profile from `LW_PROFILE`, builds the image from it with `build.sh`, runs the tests of the test rule (R53d), and only when all of them pass uploads one artifact with `<name>-sysupgrade.bin` and `<name>.manifest`; a run draws no password, so it writes no card (R53i). Which code a run builds and where `<name>` comes from is the run fields rule (R53j); how long the artifact stays is the artifact lifetime rule (R53g) (D97, D102, D113).
  - *scenario* given a user's repository whose `LW_PROFILE` holds the run fixture, when the person starts a run, then the run passes and its artifact holds the image and the manifest of that profile and nothing else
  - **R53e:** The artifact holds only the files of the run rule above (R53): never the profile, the rendered overlay, the ImageBuilder's output folder or a log.
  - **R53a:** A missing or empty `LW_PROFILE` stops the run with a message that names the secret and says where to set it; a profile that `build.sh` refuses stops the run with the message of `build.sh`, which names the key and never its value (R3). A stopped run uploads no artifact.
    - *scenario* given `LW_PROFILE` with the line of `LAN_IP` removed, when a run starts, then it stops with an error naming `LAN_IP` and leaves no artifact
  - **R53b:** The log, the summary and the annotations of a run hold none of the secret values of the profile: the workflow never prints the secret, the profile file or the rendered overlay, a failed test or build shows no such value, and GitHub's hiding of secret values in the log is not counted on.
    - *scenario* given the run of the run fixture, when its whole log, summary and annotations are searched for each secret value of the fixture, then there is no hit
  - **R53c:** On the runner the profile is a file readable by its owner only (0600) for the length of the run; it is never committed, pushed or uploaded.
    - *scenario* given a run of the run fixture, when it ends, then the user's repository has no new commit and the artifact has no profile file
  - **R53d:** The tests of a run are `tests/build/run.sh --quick` and `tests/run-all.sh`, and one that fails stops the run with no artifact. A test that cannot run on the runner, because its sandbox cannot start or a tool is missing (a `SKIP` line), counts as failed. The run lifts Ubuntu's block on unprivileged user namespaces for its own job, so the `bwrap` sandbox of the rootfs tests starts; the VM bench `tests/vm/` is not part of a run (D105).
    - *scenario* given a test that fails or prints `SKIP` on the runner, when a run reaches the tests, then the run fails, names the test and leaves no artifact
  - **R53g:** The workflow keeps the artifact 7 days, and the run's summary gives the date when the download goes away (D106).
  - **R53h:** The user's repository is made from a separate template repository of the project that holds only the workflow and a short README; lonewrt itself is not the template (D111).
  - **R53k:** The workflow of the template only checks out the release of the run's version field and runs the script `ci/github-run.sh` of that release; every step of a run (the user-namespace setting, the dependencies, the password rule, the build, the tests and the upload) comes from that script, so a fix of a run reaches every user's repository with the next release (D118).
    - *scenario* given a release whose `ci/github-run.sh` adds a step, when a user's repository made from the template a release earlier runs that version, then the run takes the new step without a change of its workflow file
  - **R53j:** A run starts by hand with two fields: the lonewrt release version (the newest release by default) and the profile name (`router` by default), which names the files of the artifact and the `profile=` line of `/etc/lw-build` (R7b). The run builds the code of that release's tag from the public lonewrt repository and writes the tag's commit into its summary; a version with no release, and a release older than `ci/github-run.sh` (`0.1.0`), stop the run with a message that names the version and leave no artifact. Moving to a newer release is a new run with the new version (D111).
    - *scenario* given the version of a release that has `ci/github-run.sh` and the name `router`, when a run passes, then the artifact holds `router-sysupgrade.bin` and `router.manifest` and the summary names the commit of that release's tag; given the version `9.9.9` or `0.1.0`, then the run stops naming it
  - **R53i:** A run stops before the build when `WIFI_KEY` is empty, or `ROOT_PASSWORD` and `ROOT_PASSWORD_HASH` are both empty, with a message that names those keys and says that both passwords must be set in the profile so that every run gives the same ones; it leaves no artifact (D112).
    - *scenario* given the run fixture with `WIFI_KEY` emptied, when a run starts, then it stops naming `WIFI_KEY` before the build and leaves no artifact
- **R54:** In a public user's repository a run stops before the build and uploads nothing; its message says that the repository must be private, because every signed-in GitHub user can download an artifact of a public repository (from: GitHub docs, "Downloading workflow artifacts", read 2026-11-16), and how to make it private. The one exception is a run that the person starts marked as a test: it ignores `LW_PROFILE` and builds the run fixture, and its summary starts with a warning that the image is open to every signed-in GitHub user. A private repository gets neither (D110, D119).
  - *scenario* given a public user's repository, when a run starts unmarked, then it stops before the build with that message and leaves no artifact; marked as a test with any `LW_PROFILE`, then it builds the run fixture and its summary starts with the warning; given a private repository, then neither
- **R55:** `README.md` describes the way without Linux from start to end: make the user's repository from the template as private (R53h); make the profile in the configurator with both passwords set (R53i) and keep the saved file, which holds the passwords and which the configurator of a later release loads (R56d); put its text into `LW_PROFILE`, start a run, download the artifact and take a backup in LuCI. Then it gives two ways to flash, LuCI first: in LuCI with the settings not kept, which is what `sysupgrade -n` does, naming the OpenWrt version the LuCI flash was tried from, or with a coding agent and the agent skill (R59b) (D117). It also says that a flash and a button reset give the router a new SSH host key, and how to drop the old one from `known_hosts`; this holds for an image without host keys in the profile's folder, which every image from a run is, and the text need not name that condition (D129).
  - **R55a:** `README.md` holds the hints of the password card (R33a) for the person who builds on GitHub, and the summary of a run links to them (D122).
- **R56:** The configurator is one HTML file in the repository that works when opened from disk in a browser. It has a field for every key of `profiles/generic.conf`, filled with that file's value and explained as in `docs/profile.md` (D99).
  - *scenario* given `generic.conf` with a new key that the page does not have, when the tests run, then a test fails naming the key
  - **R56c:** The same file also works published on GitHub Pages of the project. It keeps nothing in the browser (no browser storage, no cookies), so a reload or a closed tab empties the form (D107).
  - **R56e:** GitHub Pages carries the configurator of the newest release, the page shows the release version it belongs to next to its title, and at the profile text the page and `README.md` say to start the run with that version (D123).
    - *scenario* given a filled form, when the person reloads the page, then every field shows the value of `generic.conf` again and the browser's storage for the page is empty
  - **R56d:** The person can load an existing profile text into the form. The form takes its values, fills each key the text lacks with the value of `generic.conf` and marks it as new, and leaves out each key `generic.conf` no longer has, naming it, so the text it makes passes `build.sh` of the release the page belongs to (D109).
    - *scenario* given a profile of the previous release that lacks one key of `generic.conf` and has one key that was removed, when the person loads it, then the new key shows its default marked as new, the removed key is named as left out, and the other values are the loaded ones
  - **R56b:** From the form the configurator makes the text of a profile: each key once, in the order of `generic.conf`, as `KEY='value'` lines, which the person copies (for `LW_PROFILE`) or saves as `<name>.conf`.
    - *scenario* given the form filled with the values of the run fixture, when the person copies the text, then it equals the run fixture key by key and in order
  - **R56a:** The text of a form left unchanged has the value of `generic.conf` for every key, and `build.sh --render-only <name> <dir>` accepts it saved as `profiles/<name>.conf`.
    - *scenario* given the unchanged form, when its text is saved as `profiles/cfg.conf` and `build.sh --render-only cfg <scratch dir>` runs, then it exits 0
  - **R56f:** `ZONENAME` is picked from a list of the time zones that LuCI of the image's OpenWrt offers, plus `UTC`, and the pick sets `TIMEZONE` to that zone's POSIX string from the same table. `TIMEZONE` is shown only while it holds another string than the picked zone's, or the zone is not on the list (a loaded profile), with a note; a loaded value of either key is kept as loaded (D124). A loaded empty `TIMEZONE` counts as the picked zone's string, since the build fills it from the same table (R3c); with a zone not on the list it is shown with a note that the build cannot fill it.
    - *scenario* given the unchanged form, when the person picks `America/New_York`, then the text has `TIMEZONE='EST5EDT,M3.2.0,M11.1.0'` and no `TIMEZONE` field is shown; given a loaded profile with `ZONENAME='Mars/Olympus'`, then both values stay and `TIMEZONE` is shown with its note
  - **R56g:** A field that matters only with another value set is shown only then, unless it holds another value than its default: the `WG_*` keys with `WG_ENABLED` at `1`, `WWAN_AUTH`, `WWAN_USERNAME` and `WWAN_PASSWORD` with an APN, `LOW_BALANCE` with both Telegram keys, `SMS_REBOOT_MAX_AGE_MIN`, `SMS_REBOOT_MAX_PER_DAY` and `ROUTER_NAME` with `SMS_ADMINS`, the other `OP_*` keys with `OP_MCCMNC`; `ROOT_PASSWORD` and `ROOT_PASSWORD_HASH` each hide while the other is set. A hidden field keeps its value in the text (D125).
    - *scenario* given the unchanged form, when the person sets `WG_ENABLED` to `1`, then the seven other `WG_*` fields appear; when they type a root password, then the hash field goes
  - **R56h:** The form takes `SSH_KEYS` one key per line and writes them into the text separated by `;`, without blank lines; a loaded profile shows its keys one per line (D126).
  - **R56i:** Below the `OP_*` fields the form has a box for a balance SMS of the operator and shows what the router would read from it with `OP_BALANCE_REPLY_PATTERN` (R47c): the amount with `OP_CURRENCY` and the date, or that the reply does not fit, or that the pattern is one the router rejects. It is a preview: the pasted SMS is not part of the profile, and it marks no key and stops no text (R57) (D132).
    - *cmd* `tests/configurator/20-browser.sh` puts 25 pairs of pattern and reply through the page and through `operators.sh` and `notify.sh` run by the image's shell, and expects the same answer from both: the amount and date, no fit, or a rejected pattern (expected)
- **R57:** Before it makes the text, the form marks at its key every value that cannot be a profile value at all: an empty value of a key that may not be empty (R3), and a `'` or a line break, which the profile format cannot hold (the line breaks between the keys of `SSH_KEYS` are separators, R56h). It never refuses a value that `build.sh` accepts, and every other check is left to `build.sh`, except the Tailscale marks and warnings (R70a to R70d), which do not stop the text either (D100).
  - *scenario* given the values of `fake-full.conf` typed into the form, when the person asks for the text, then nothing is marked; given an empty `LAN_IP` or a Wi-Fi name with `'`, then that key is marked and no text is made
  - **R57a:** When `WIFI_KEY` is empty, or `ROOT_PASSWORD` and `ROOT_PASSWORD_HASH` are both empty, the form shows a note at `WIFI_KEY`, or once at `ROOT_PASSWORD`, that a run on GitHub refuses such a profile (R53i). Unlike the marks of the rough-check rule above (R57), the note does not stop the text, which `build.sh` accepts on Linux (D112).
  - **R57b:** While `WIFI_KEY` or `ROOT_PASSWORD` is empty, its field has a button that draws a password with the browser's random source and puts it into the field: three groups of four characters joined by `-`, from the 32 characters the build draws from (R33). The button is not there while the field holds a value, so it never replaces a password the person typed or loaded, and a draw sends nothing (R58) (D130).
    - *cmd* `tests/configurator/20-browser.sh` expects a button at both fields of the unchanged form, a drawn value of that form in the field and in the text, two draws that differ, no button at a field with a value, and `build.sh --render-only` accepting the text (expected)
- **R58:** The configurator makes no network request: the file loads nothing from outside itself (no scripts, styles, fonts, images or analytics), and filling, copying and saving send nothing (D104).
  - *scenario* given a headless browser with its request log on, when it opens the file from disk, fills a value, copies the text and saves the file, then the log holds only the file itself
  - **R58a:** The page explains how to get the Telegram keys of a profile, the bot token from @BotFather and the ID of the chat, as steps the person takes in Telegram or in their own browser, never as a request from the page.
- **R59:** The agent skill leads a person and their coding agent from no profile to a checked image. It takes a profile file made in the configurator, asking in the chat only for values that are not secret values (R59c), writes `profiles/<name>.conf` with mode 0600 (git ignores it, R5), and builds the image on the person's Linux machine (`./fetch-deps.sh`, `./build.sh <name>`) or in the user's repository (R53), as the person chooses (D101). Example: the person says "I have a profile from the configurator, build on my laptop", and the agent saves it as `profiles/<name>.conf` with mode 0600, runs the two commands and reports; with "build it on GitHub" it starts a run in the user's repository and reads its artifact.
  - **R59a:** A build ends with a report: whether the build and the tests passed, the release version and profile name of the image (R7b), the sha256 of the image file and, when the build wrote one, where the password card is. A failed build or test is reported as failed, with the message of `build.sh` or of the test, and the skill does not go on to a flash.
    - *scenario* given a profile with an empty `LAN_IP`, when the agent builds with the skill, then the report says the build failed on `LAN_IP` and the agent offers no flash
  - **R59b:** The skill flashes only after the person's explicit yes to that flash, every time. Then it first saves a backup (`sysupgrade -b` and `/root`) on the person's machine, checks that the image copied to the router has the sha256 of the report and that `sysupgrade -T` passes, and only then runs `sysupgrade -n`, as `README.md` shows; a failed backup, check or copy stops it before `sysupgrade -n`, and a lost connection during the flash is reported with the README's recovery note, not retried (D101). When the new image is a tailscale image and the router holds Tailscale state (`/etc/tailscale/tailscaled.state`), the skill packs `/etc/tailscale/` and any existing root shell history (R32a) into an archive on the router and runs `sysupgrade -f <archive>` in place of `sysupgrade -n`, so the router stays in the account with its name, tag and key expiry, and everything else comes from the profile as after `-n` (R7); for Tailscale (R65, R66a, R72b, R72c) this counts as a flash that keeps settings, and the person can ask for a fresh start, which is `-n` (D164, D219). A flash over the tunnel has the limit of the remote flash rule (R59e). Example: the person says "build the image for my router", and the agent builds and reports but does not flash; the person then says "flash it", and the agent backs up, checks and flashes.
    - *scenario* given a built image and no yes from the person, when the session ends, then no `sysupgrade` ran on the router; given "flash it" and a router where `sysupgrade -T` fails, then the agent reports the failure and does not run `sysupgrade -n`; given an added router and a tailscale image, when the agent flashes it, then the router is added again within 5 min of the start with the same name and no link message comes
  - **R59c:** The skill's report and the agent's session transcript, the output of the commands it runs included, name the keys of the profile and never its secret values: the agent asks for no secret value in the chat, runs no command that prints the profile file, the password card or a secret value (no `cat`, no diff of secret keys), and when the person asks for a password it says which file holds it (D115).
    - *scenario* given a profile file from the configurator, when the agent builds with the skill and the person asks for the WiFi password, then the chat holds no secret value of the profile and the agent names the file that holds the password
  - **R59d:** The agent skill is the folder `skills/lonewrt/` with its instruction file `SKILL.md`, and `README.md` and `AGENTS.md` link to it (D108).
  - **R59e:** Over the WireGuard tunnel the skill flashes an image whose profile changes a `WG_*` key, a `WWAN_*` key or `LTE_BANDS` against the router's current settings only after the person says that someone is at the router; otherwise it stops before the backup and says why, naming the changed keys and never their values (D121).
    - *scenario* given an image with a changed `WG_ENDPOINT_HOST` and "flash it" over the tunnel, when the person has not said that someone is at the router, then no `sysupgrade` runs and the agent names the changed key
  - **R59g:** On the GitHub way the agent may make the user's repository from the template, with the person's own `gh` login and after their yes, and starts runs and downloads artifacts there. The secret value never reaches the agent: either the person sets `LW_PROFILE` in GitHub's settings, or the agent sends the saved profile file to it with a command whose arguments and output hold no value (`gh secret set LW_PROFILE < <file>`), without reading the file (D120).
    - *scenario* given a profile file and "build it on GitHub", when the agent makes the repository and sets the secret, then its transcript holds the repository name and the secret's name and none of the file's values
  - **R59f:** The project's `AGENTS.md` keeps its rule against reading or printing a private profile and a password card for work on the code, and names the agent skill, run at the person's request, as its one exception (D115).
- **R60:** A release whose `generic.conf` gains or loses a key names each such key in its `CHANGELOG.md` entry, so a person knows that a saved profile must go through the configurator's loading rule (R56d) (D109).

### First start and reset

- **R7:** After a flash with `sysupgrade -n` and after a button reset, at its first start the image sets everything in the column "From the profile" of the table below; the table is binding (D2, D74, D82, D193).
  - **R7a:** After the same flash or reset, the generic image ends with the values in the column "With `generic.conf`" (D21, D25).
    - *scenario* given the generic image from `generic.conf`, when the router comes up after the flash, then the LAN has `192.168.1.1`, the WiFi is called `OpenWrt-LTE` with the password from `image/generic-passwords.txt`, and `wg0` is disabled

| Setting | From the profile | With `generic.conf` |
|---|---|---|
| LAN | `LAN_IP`, mask /24; IPv6 from the session's usable prefix (R74); steady IPv4-only service sends no router advertisements or DHCPv6, and the LAN has no ULA prefix (R74b, D193) | `192.168.1.1/24`, IPv6 as from the profile |
| WiFi | both bands, one name and password (`WIFI_SSID`, `WIFI_KEY`), `psk2` (WPA2 only, D74), 802.11r fast roaming, country, channels and HT modes from the profile | as from the profile: name `OpenWrt-LTE`, password drawn, country `PL`, which the owner changes to the router's country before the build |
| WiFi schedule | installed, off | the same |
| LTE | interface `wwan` over QMI, APN from the profile by the APN rule (R13), mode by the LTE-only rule (R10), registration wait `network.wwan.timeout` 30 (D82), IP families auto (`network.wwan.pdptype` `ipv4v6`) by the default rule (R7c, R73k) | the same, with an empty APN and the operator table as fallback (R13a, R13b) |
| WireGuard | `wg0` and zone `vpn` always exist; with `WG_ENABLED='1'` the tunnel is on by the tunnel rule (R31), otherwise off by the generic tunnel rule (R32) | tunnel off (R32) |
| firewall | `wwan` and the interfaces its session adds in zone `wan` (R75), `wg0` in zone `vpn`; in a tailscale image also `tailscale0` in zone `tailscale` (R64a); the stock IPsec rules from `wan` to `lan` for IPv4 only (R75a) | the same |
| Tailscale | in a tailscale image the service on, with the settings rule (R64) and the profile's auth key (R66a); otherwise not there | not there |
| system | host name `HOSTNAME`, time zone `TIMEZONE` and `ZONENAME`, persistent log by the log rule (R29) | the same, host name `lte-router` |
| log level | `LOG_LEVEL` as uci `lonewrt.log.level`; `debug` also sets the persistent log to 2048 KB, in a tailscale image 1024 KB (R29c) | `info`, 1024 KB |
| session watcher | service `lw-linkwatch`, enabled by the image, no options (R49) | the same |
| root password | hash `ROOT_PASSWORD_HASH`, the hash of `ROOT_PASSWORD` (R33b), or drawn (R33) | drawn |
| SSH | host keys and `authorized_keys` from `profiles/<name>/` when present, and the keys of `SSH_KEYS` (R61), the same after every reset; missing host keys are generated at the first start | host keys generated at the first start, no `authorized_keys` |
| watchcat | restart of the interface `wwan` after `LINK_RESTART_AFTER_S` seconds without a cellular answer (R14, R14a) | after 120 s |
| cron | `wwan-recover` every 5 min, `lw-siglog` every 5 min shifted by 2 min, `lw-sms-clean` at 04:10; `lw-notify balance` (the preset's balance query) on Monday at 10:00 by the balance rule (R35) when `TG_BOT_TOKEN`, `TG_CHAT_ID` and `LOW_BALANCE` are set; `lw-heartbeat` every 5 min when `HC_PING_URL` is set (R42) | the first three only |
| notifications | service `lw-notify` with token, chat, threshold, router name and SMS admins from the profile (R34 to R38, R46) | none, keys empty |

- **R7b:** The image has a file `/etc/lw-build` with the release version (R3b), the profile name, the build date and a hash of the files in `src/`, one `key=value` line each (`version=`, `profile=`, `built=`, `src_sha256=`), and the first start, once syslog and the persistent log (R29) run, writes an entry with the tag `lw-firstboot` with the same content, so after a reset the log shows that there was a reset and which image runs (D37, D94).
  - **R7c:** A clean flash and a button reset set `network.wwan.pdptype` to the image's default (R73k). A flash that keeps settings preserves an explicit restored value, including `ipv4` from an older image. If the restored configuration has no value, it stays absent and the IPv4 fallback applies (R73) (D181, D201, D211).
    - *scenario* given a router on 0.6.1, when an image with auto as its default is flashed keeping settings, then `uci get network.wwan.pdptype` gives `ipv4`; after a button reset it gives `ipv4v6`
  - **R7d:** After a flash that keeps settings, the watchcat entry `wwan_soft` checks the link as the watchcat rule says (R14), with the `period` it had; other watchcat entries stay as they were (D181).
    - *scenario* given a kept 0.6.1 watchcat file with `period` 300 and one more entry of the owner, when the image's first start runs, then `wwan_soft` runs the family check with `period` 300 and the other entry is unchanged
  - **R7e:** After a flash that keeps settings, the LAN `lan` is moved to the rules of IPv6 on the LAN (R74 to R74g) only when `dhcp.lan` serves the interface `lan`, `network.lan` has `ip6assign` 60 and no other IPv6 option, and the IPv6 options of `dhcp.lan` are exactly one stock set: of 0.6.1, router advertisements (`ra`) and DHCPv6 (`dhcpv6`) `disabled`, alone or with the stock `ra_slaac` 1 and `ra_flags` `managed-config other-config`; of 0.6.0, `ra` and `dhcpv6` `server` with `ra_slaac` 1 and `ra_flags` `managed-config other-config` (measured: the `dhcp.lan` and `network.lan` settings of the owner's 0.6.0 router, 2026-11-24). A `lan` with any other value, any further option the matcher knows, or a `dhcp.lan` on another interface stays as it was, and so does every other LAN. The matcher knows, on `network.lan`, `ip6*`, `ipv6*`, `delegate` and `lw_ipv6`, and on `dhcp.lan`, `ra`, `ra_*`, `dhcpv6`, `dhcpv6_*`, `ndp`, `ndp_*`, `ndproxy_*`, `dns`, `dns_service`, `domain`, `master`, `upstream`, `ignore`, `prefix_filter`, `preferred_lifetime`, `max_preferred_lifetime`, `max_valid_lifetime`, `ifname` and `networkid`; an option outside these, or IPv6 policy in another UCI section, is not seen. A value the owner chose that equals an old default is moved as well, because the two cannot be told apart. The profile's LAN address is set at every flash, a flash that keeps settings included, as in 0.6.x (D201).
    - *scenario* given `<vm>` with a kept 0.6.1 configuration and one more LAN of the owner, when the image's first start runs with forced IPv4, then the LAN is IPv4 only and quiet (R74b); after auto is set in LuCI and the WAN peer gives a routed /64, then `lan` gets IPv6 by the LAN prefix rule (R74), UDP port 500 and ESP from the peer do not reach a LAN client, and the other LAN is unchanged; given a kept 0.6.0 configuration with the stock flags, then the first start gives `dhcp.lan.ra` and `dhcpv6` `disabled`, `ip6assign` 64 and the profile's LAN address, and keeps `pdptype` `ipv4`; given a kept 0.6.0 or 0.6.1 `lan` with one more option such as `ra_maxinterval`, `dns` or `ip6hint`, or a single `ra_flags` value, then `lan` stays as it was
    - *cmd* `tests/firstboot/r7e-r75a.sh`
- **R8:** The first start does not wait for the modem: LAN, WiFi and SSH come up even when the modem does not answer.
  - **R8a:** A first start cut by a power loss finishes the configuration at the next start, with the same result.
- **R9:** Changes made in LuCI survive a restart; the exceptions are the LTE mode and the bands, which the modem state rule puts back (R10, R11).

### Modem

- **R10:** At every start, before LTE first comes up, the interface `wwan` asks for LTE-only mode (`network.wwan.modes=lte`), also after the EDGE fallback (D3). The request is the mode set that `qmi.sh` makes after its `--network-register`, which can otherwise move the modem to LTE despite stored GSM-only mode (from: modem mode observations, commit `f9ca2f8`, `SPEC.md`, decision D82); `AT+ZSNT?` shows the stored mode, not the one in use after an interface start, so the reads of R11 and R23 confirm the stored mode (D82).
- **R11:** At every start, in the background and not earlier than one minute after the AT port appears, the router brings the modem to this state: LTE-only mode (`+ZSNT: 6,0,0`), the bands of the profile, SMS store `ME` for reading, writing and receiving (D3).
  - *scenario* given a profile with `LTE_BANDS='1,3,8,20,28,32'` and a modem left in auto mode with other bands by a test, when the router restarts, then within 5 min `AT+ZSNT?` gives `6,0,0`, `AT+ZNLOCKBAND?` gives the mask `88080085`, and syslog has an `lw-modem-mode` entry with `modem OK`
  - **R11a:** When the mode and bands already match, the modem gets no command to change the mode or bands; the SMS store command goes at every attempt.
  - **R11b:** When the state still differs after 3 attempts 30 s apart, or the AT port does not appear within 120 s, the router writes that to syslog (tag `lw-modem-mode`) and does nothing more until the next start.
  - **R11c:** A profile with `LTE_BANDS` locks the modem to those bands (D4); a profile without bands does not change the modem's bands (D23).
- **R12:** The image's scripts send AT commands only through `/dev/ttyUSB2`, including when reading and setting the bands and when reading the IMSI (D28). The bands are set with `AT+ZNLOCKBAND=1,0,<mask>,0` and read with `AT+ZNLOCKBAND?`, where bit n-1 of the hexadecimal mask is band n; for example bands 1, 3, 8, 20, 28, 32 give `88080085`.
  - **R12a:** The image's scripts send at most one AT command at a time on `/dev/ttyUSB2`, because they all take the shared lock `/tmp/lw-at.lock`; LuCI on `/dev/ttyUSB1` works without this lock (D5).
  - **R12b:** Every AT command, the wait for the lock included, has a time limit of 10 to 30 s (5 s for the watcher's snapshot, R49g), and at most 20 s in the APN script; when the limit runs out, no process of that command is left (D29).
    - *scenario* given the AT lock is held on a router, when an `AT+ZSNT?` read takes the image's lock with a 20 s limit, then it exits with code 124 and leaves no orphan process
  - **R12c:** No script of the image runs `uqmi` except `lw-linkwatch` (R49a, D80); QMI otherwise serves only netifd's `qmi.sh` (R12d) for the data connection (D5).
    - *cmd* `rg -l '^[^#]*\buqmi\b' src/files/` prints only `src/files/usr/sbin/lw-linkwatch`; `tests/linkwatch/service.sh` checks it
  - **R12d:** The image ships the netifd protocol handler of the `uqmi` package, `/lib/netifd/proto/qmi.sh`, with the listed edits and no other change: (1) the `--network-scan` call that follows the mode set when `network.wwan.modes` is set is removed, and the comment line above it stays; (2) the call setup follows the IP family rules (R73 to R73p), including protection through the end of serial setup (R14f, R49a); early publication is a conditional fallback for a missed startup timing bound (R73i, R73p); (3) the native IPv4 child `wwan_4` is made only for a native IPv4 lease, only the translation check creates `wwan_6_4`, and the IPv6 child of `wwan` leaves a nonempty `/var/run/lw-clat/v6only` record only for IPv6-only, with the IPv6 client, handle and setup uptime as `<cid_6>/<pdh_6>/<uptime>`; a dual-stack DHCPv6 child leaves any earlier record empty (R76b, R76d, O9); (4) when the modem supplies static IPv6 settings (R73j), they are read once, and the set published is the set checked (R73j). The build takes the file from the package that goes into the image (the `uqmi` of the ImageBuilder's own package feeds, resolved by its own `apk`, so it needs the network), and stops with the file's name and no new image when a line an edit anchors on is not there exactly as often as the edit expects. The upstream notices of the file stay; the repository holds no copy of the stock file, only the anchors and lonewrt's own text. Every uqmi call the edits add has uqmi's own `-t` and a time limit around it. The derived file passes the same `sh -n` check as the image's other scripts (R6), and the VM image gets the same derivation (D82, D172, D199).
    - *cmd* `tests/build/45-qmi-handler.sh` runs the derivation against a stand-in package: the reviewed file gives the file with the edits; a file with another sha256, an anchor missing or there once too often (the anchor of edit (4) included) stop it with the package's file name and write nothing; a derived file that fails `sh -n` and a lost network stop it; `tests/build/15-pinned-packages.sh` expects `build.sh` to stop with no image for such a package file and for an image file that is not that build's package file with the edits; `tests/build/60-images.sh` expects the image's `qmi.sh` to be the package's file of the same build with the edits; `tests/vm/10-firstboot.sh` expects no scan line, the mode set, and `sh -n` passing in the guest (expected)
    - *scenario* given the generic image with forced IPv4 and a registered modem, when `ifup wwan` runs, then the first good ping through `wwan0` comes after 14 to 15 s, not after 40 to 46 s, and `grep -c -e --network-scan /lib/netifd/proto/qmi.sh` gives `0`
  - **R12e:** The build stops with the file's name and no new image when the package's `qmi.sh` has another sha256 than the one the edits were reviewed against (now `073b855cb15d6cdffb0c1de2f3fec9785796d6399c9f8ce5fcd5c2fbbe6fa6d4`, from: expert review, 2026-11-23) (D192).
  - **R12f:** After the build it checks that the image's `qmi.sh`, mode 0755 included, is the package's file from that build with the same edits, and stops with no new image when it is not (D82, D172).
  - **R12g:** The build's pins of stock files (R12e, R14c, R12h) can be replaced only by `<tests>`, through the variables `LW_QMI_SHA256`, `LW_WATCHCAT_SHA256`, `LW_WATCHCAT_INIT_SHA256` and `LW_ODHCPD_INIT_SHA256`; a build run any other way with one of them set, also to an empty value, stops, naming the variable, with no new image (D205).
    - *cmd* `tests/build/15-pinned-packages.sh` expects `build.sh` run outside `<tests>` with each of the four variables set to stop with its name and no image
  - **R12h:** The build stops with no new image when the stock `/etc/init.d/odhcpd` of the `odhcpd-ipv6only` package that goes into the image is missing or has another sha256 than the pinned one, which the image's own init script was written against (R74g) (D206).
    - *cmd* `tests/build/15-pinned-packages.sh` expects a matching pin to pass and a changed stock init to stop the build; `tests/build/60-images.sh` expects the image's `/etc/init.d/odhcpd` to be the image's own file
- **R13:** An APN given in the profile or entered in LuCI is used unchanged and never overwritten (D27).
  - **R13a:** When no APN is given (the generic image), the router connects with an empty APN and the network assigns its own; syslog has an `Auto-APN` entry that the empty APN works (D27).
  - **R13b:** At every failed connection attempt (`ifup-failed`) from the second in a row, when the APN is empty or carries the "from table" marker, the router reads the SIM's IMSI, writes APN, authentication and login from the operator table (26001, 26002, 26003, 26006, 29701, 29702 and 29703) with the "from table" marker, and reconnects at once; an unknown operator stays with the empty APN, and syslog has an `Auto-APN` entry with the MCC-MNC and a note that the APN must be entered in LuCI (D27, D34).
    - *scenario* given the generic image and a SIM of an operator from the table whose network rejects an empty APN, when two attempts in a row fail, then the third attempt goes with the APN from the table, and syslog has an `Auto-APN` entry with the operator's name
  - **R13d:** The "from table" marker remembers the APN it wrote and holds only while the current APN equals it, so an APN changed in LuCI falls under the profile APN rule (R13); the router replaces a marked APN with the table APN of a new SIM when, at a failed attempt, the IMSI points to another operator. The APN script never holds up other hotplug events, because it runs in the background, and at most one copy of it runs (D34).
  - **R13c:** When `AT+CPIN?` does not give `READY`, or the IMSI cannot be read in 6 tries, the settings stay as they are and syslog has an entry with the tag `Auto-APN`; an attempt cut by a busy lock is not repeated in that run, because the next failed connection attempt starts the script again, and the whole APN script ends within 150 s (D29).
