  - **R13e:** `WWAN_PIN` from the profile (empty, or 4 to 8 digits; a secret value) is written as the `pincode` of the interface `wwan`, so netifd's QMI handler unlocks a SIM that asks for its PIN at every start of the interface, also after a button reset; an empty key leaves the option unset, and a SIM without a PIN ignores the option. A value outside the format stops the build with the key's name (R3). The handler tries a PIN only while the SIM reports all 3 tries left and stops the interface after a wrong one, so a wrong `WWAN_PIN` costs at most 2 tries (the handler makes two calls with it) and the router alone never blocks the SIM; the failure then falls into the class `sim` (R18d). A SIM that has fewer than 3 tries left is not tried at all, also with the right PIN (D131).
    - *cmd* `tests/build/10-profile.sh` expects a stop naming the key for a missing `WWAN_PIN` and for 3 or 9 digits, a letter, a sign, a space and fullwidth digits, with no value in the message, and `P_WWAN_PIN` as written for 4 and 8 digits and for an empty value (expected)
    - *cmd* `tests/firstboot/full.sh` expects `network.wwan.pincode` with the fixture's PIN after the first start, `tests/firstboot/generic.sh` expects it unset (expected)

### IP families of the LTE session

A start of `wwan` asks the network for a session and uses the families the operator grants.
These rules hold for every start: after a boot, an `ifup`, a watchcat restart, the EDGE switch (R22) and the LTE probe (R23).
The stand-in modem of Terms models each operator below; the hardware each case was tried on is in Acceptance.

- **R73:** `network.wwan.pdptype` decides which families a start asks for: `ipv4v6` is auto, `ipv4` or `ip` forces IPv4, `ipv6` forces IPv6, in any letter case; a missing or other value forces IPv4, as the stock handler does. A value set in LuCI takes effect at the next start of the interface, with no new image (D170, D171, D215).
  - **R73a:** Auto runs the steps of the table below in order and stops at the first step that gives a session; forced IPv4 runs only step 2, forced IPv6 only step 3. A call counts as granted only when it started with a numeric handle and reads connected (R73e); any other outcome (a refusal, an error text, no answer in time, a failed family selection) counts as refused; the MF289F accepts the family selection for both families (measured: `uqmi --set-client-id wds,<cid> --set-ip-family ipv4` and `ipv6` on a spare client, rc 0, owner's router, 2026-11-23). The table binds (D169, D171, D178).

| Step | Modem profile type | Calls, each on its own client | Session |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `IPV4V6` | IPv4, then IPv6 | both granted: dual stack; only IPv4: IPv4 alone; only IPv6: IPv6 alone with translated IPv4 (R76) |
| 2, when both calls of step 1 were refused | `IP` | IPv4 | IPv4 alone |
| 3, when step 2 was refused | `IPV6` | IPv6 | IPv6 alone with translated IPv4 (R76) |
| all refused | | | none: the start fails as before, with the interface restart and recovery ladder (R14 to R21) and Auto-APN (R13b) |

    - *scenario* given the stand-in modem of each operator model (both granted, only IPv4, only IPv6, `IPV4V6` refused with `IP` granted, only `IPV6` granted, all refused), when `wwan` starts in auto, then the session has the families of the table's row; with forced IPv4 and forced IPv6 only step 2 or step 3 runs
  - **R73b:** A family refused while the other family of the same step is up never ends the start: the interface comes up with the survivor, the start counts as a good one (no failed start, nothing counted by Auto-APN, R13b), and netifd starts nothing again. In step 1 the IPv6 call follows the IPv4 call, so on a SIM that grants only IPv4 the IPv4 call is up before IPv6 is asked for (D169).
    - *scenario* given a SIM that grants IPv4 and refuses IPv6 in every session, when `wwan` starts in auto, then it comes up on IPv4 after one refused IPv6 call and no further start follows; at the debug level (R29c) syslog has one line per start that IPv6 was refused, and at the info level the log is the one of forced IPv4
  - **R73c:** A start remembers no refusal of an earlier start: every start runs the order from step 1, so an operator that grants IPv6 later is used from the next start on (D179).
    - *scenario* given the stand-in modem refusing IPv6 at one start and granting it at the next, when `wwan` starts twice in auto, then the second session is dual stack
  - **R73d:** The errors before the calls (SIM, PIN, registration, device) end the start as they do in the stock handler, before any call. Repeated failure to get a QMI client ends the steps of that start. A start whose steps all failed gives one failed start (`ifup-failed`); refusals inside the steps give none. Every step uses the same APN, authentication and login, so the APN rules (R13, R13b) decide the APN as before (D182).
    - *scenario* given the stand-in modem refusing every call with an empty APN, when two starts fail in a row, then each gives one `ifup-failed` and the third start goes with the APN from the operator table (R13b); given the stand-in refusing only IPv6, then no `ifup-failed` comes
  - **R73e:** A start publishes only calls that started with a handle and read connected. A failed call with a known handle is stopped by that handle before its client is released. After a lost start reply, status is read on that client with the IPv6 family selected for an IPv6 call. A confirmed disconnected client is released. A connected or unreadable client beside a confirmed sibling is left for the next start's sweep. Without a sibling, a connected call with no handle gets the client-scoped `0xffffffff` stop before release, and an unreadable client is released. The sweep of all old calls runs once before the first call of a start (D182, D196).
    - *scenario* given the stand-in modem with one fault at a time (a negative handle, a text or empty reply, no client, a failed family selection, a handle followed by a bad status, a hung call, a failed profile write, missing IPv6 settings, a lease that never comes), when `wwan` starts, then only connected calls are published, the other family stays up and each taken client is published, released or left for the next sweep as the status and sibling rules specify
  - **R73f:** Profile 1 of the modem holds the APN and the PDP type, as the stock handler writes them (an empty APN included); the authentication and the login go on each call, so a change of them alone takes effect at the next call and no credential is stored in the profile. A start skips the write only when a current, verified read shows that the profile's APN and type match what the step needs and the profile is enabled; otherwise it writes, so a change of the APN (by Auto-APN, R13b, in LuCI, or with a new SIM) reaches the modem as before (D183, D196). The possibility of repeated writes while all calls are refused is open under the modem write limit question (O13).
    - *scenario* given the stand-in modem with profile 1 at `IPV4V6` and the APN `a`, when the APN in `network.wwan` becomes `b` and `wwan` starts, then profile 1 holds `b`; when it starts again unchanged, then profile 1 gets no write; when only the password changes, then the next call carries it and profile 1 gets no write; given a profile with the right APN and type but disabled, then the start writes it
  - **R73g:** The IPv4 call of every step starts with the modem's autoconnect on unless `network.wwan.autoconnect` is `0`, as the stock handler does for `ip`; the IPv6 call has it only when the option is `1` (D183).
    - *scenario* given auto and no `autoconnect` option, when step 1 runs, then the stand-in sees `--autoconnect` on the IPv4 call and not on the IPv6 call
  - **R73h:** A start has a negotiation deadline of 60 s on a clock that does not jump, from the preparation of profile 1 through every family attempt to the end of its cleanup; the SIM, PIN and registration waits before it are not part of it. Every QMI call and other subprocess gets at most the time left before the deadline, and time is kept back for the cleanup and recording a survivor, including its SIM identity when readable. At the deadline the start publishes a confirmed surviving call (R73b), else it fails once (R73d); a cleanup or a rollback it cannot check by then is done at the next start in which the modem answers (R73e, R73m, R73n) (D182, D197).
    - *scenario* given the stand-in modem that stops answering before any call is granted, when `wwan` starts in auto, then the start fails once at its deadline
    - *scenario* given the stand-in modem that grants IPv4 and then stops answering on the IPv6 attempt, when `wwan` starts in auto, then IPv4 is published by the deadline, no failed start follows, and the next responsive start finds no call of the earlier one
  - **R73i:** In auto, on a SIM that grants IPv4 and refuses IPv6, the slowest of five starts is at most 3 s behind the slowest of five starts with forced IPv4. Each start is timed from `ifup wwan` to the first IPv4 reply bound to the address of the new lease, the two modes take turns on the same router and radio, the profile operations are timed apart, and both sets of times are reported against the 14.2 to 14.8 s of forced IPv4 (from: modem startup measurements, commit `f9ca2f8`, `SPEC.md`, decision D82, five runs). The start first publishes the session after both attempts; only when that misses the limit does it publish the IPv4 call before it asks for IPv6 (R73p), and the limit is measured again. Whether the release needs that hardware comparison before it ships is the Auto start-time question (O12) (D184, D199).
    - *scenario* given an MF289F with a SIM that grants only IPv4, when five starts in auto and five in forced IPv4 run in turn, then the slowest auto start is at most 3 s behind the slowest forced IPv4 start, each auto start shows one refused IPv6 call, and the report holds all ten times and the profile times
  - **R73j:** IPv6-only sessions get IPv6 settings from router advertisements and DHCPv6 through `wwan_6`. Dual stack uses one checked modem report when `network.wwan.dhcpv6` is unset or `0`, the WAN address is in 2000::/3, the prefix length is 1 through 64, and the gateway and first DNS address are usable. Usable addresses exclude unspecified and loopback forms in every spelling, and uncompressed addresses have all eight groups. An unusable second DNS address is dropped. With an unusable report or `dhcpv6=1`, `wwan_6` supplies the settings instead. Only the selected source publishes settings, using the checked report on the modem path (from: `lw_v6_ok` and `QMI_N_V6`, code baseline in the header; operator settings question (O6)) (D194).
    - *scenario* given step 1 grants both families with a usable report, when the session starts, then the one checked modem report is published and no DHCPv6 child takes its place
    - *scenario* given step 1 grants only IPv6, or dual stack gets a prefix above /64 or an unspecified, loopback or malformed required address, when the session starts, then `wwan_6` supplies the settings
    - *cmd* `tests/qmi/r73j-ipv6-settings.sh` and `tests/qmi/r73j-static-settings.sh`
  - **R73k:** A clean flash and a button reset default to Auto (`ipv4v6`). Release with that default requires Acceptance 12 in full, including the CLAT VM checks and both LAN withdrawal scenarios (R74c, R74f). The acceptance scope and hardware timing gate are open questions (O11, O12) (D211, D216).
    - *scenario* given a clean flash or button reset after a forced setting, when first boot runs, then `network.wwan.pdptype` is `ipv4v6`; with settings kept, the previous explicit value or its absence survives (R7c)
  - **R73l:** The release `README.md` IP-versions bullet gives the Auto default, the LuCI PDP Type choices, the operator's DNS64 and NAT64 requirement for IPv6-only access, and the effects of keeping settings (R7c, R7e, R73k, R75a). `docs/network.md` lists both families' probe addresses and the cellular AAAA lookup of `ipv4only.arpa` to the operator's DNS64 server (R76a). The LAN section of `docs/network.md` describes the withdrawal, event sources and pending replay (R74c, R74f, R74g) (D181, D201, D211).

  - **R73m:** After the interface goes down, after a killed start and after a power cut, the next start leaves no client of an earlier start allocated and no call of it in the modem (D182).
    - *scenario* given the stand-in modem, when an `ifdown` comes during a start, a start is killed, the power is cut, or a teardown runs twice, then the next start begins with no client or call of the earlier one
  - **R73n:** After a start whose steps all failed, profile 1 gets back the type it had before that start only when this start changed it; the APN the start wrote stays, and the write is checked once the modem answers. The profile type with which a start came up (which may be `IPV4V6` on a SIM that granted only IPv4) is kept on flash, written at once and only when it changes, so that a cut write leaves the old or the new value. Before registration a start repairs profile 1 once, and only for a change of its type that the flash record shows a cut-off start began, with the same SIM and APN; a type that differs from the record is no reason alone, and a first start or a new SIM takes the normal path. The MF289F does not tell its SIM (measured: `uqmi --get-iccid` and `uqmi --get-imsi` answer `Not supported`, owner's router, 2026-11-23), an unknown SIM never matches the record, and so this repair never runs on it (D183, D203).
    - *scenario* given all steps refused, when the start ends, then profile 1 holds the type it had before the start if this start changed it, and the new APN in either case
    - *scenario* given a start cut off after it changed the type of profile 1, when the next start runs with the same SIM and APN, then profile 1 gets the recorded type once before registration; with a new SIM, then no repair runs
  - **R73o:** When the modem does not let the start read profile 1 back, the start writes and starts as the stock handler does, within the deadline (R73h); such a write counts as unverified, which the log tells apart from a refused call, and no log line holds the raw profile. A failed write readback invalidates the cached profile, so the next attempt must confirm or write the needed settings (D196). The MF289F lets the start read profile 1 (measured: `uqmi --get-profile-settings 3gpp,1` gives the keys `apn`, `pdp-type`, `username`, `password`, `auth`, `no-roaming` and `apn-disabled`, owner's router, 2026-11-23), so this path is for other modems and failed reads.
    - *scenario* given the stand-in modem with a failing profile read, when `wwan` starts with forced IPv4, then the IPv4 call comes up as with the stock handler and the debug log has one line for an unverified write; given a write that does not stick, then the log says so and the call is not counted as refused
  - **R73p:** If the startup timing bound is missed and early publication is needed (startup timing rule, R73i; measurement gate question, O12), a start that publishes IPv4 early starts the `wwan_4` child (or sets the static IPv4 when `dhcp` is `0`) while IPv6 is still pending, and holds the modem alone until it ends, also when `wwan` already shows up (R49a): its early update puts a mark of a running start into netifd's data of `wwan` (a field such as `lw_setup`), and its last update leaves the mark out, since netifd itself gives no sign that a start still runs; a start that publishes once, as forced IPv4 and the serial order of the startup timing rule (R73i) do, needs no netifd field; its negotiation mark follows the timed guard (R14f). Its second update keeps the lease, `cid_4`, `pdh_4` and the DNS, makes no second child, and gives no second start and no Auto-APN failure; a start killed between taking the IPv6 client and publishing it leaves no call of it (R73m) (D199).
    - *scenario* given the conditional early-publication path (expected) in `<vm>` with real netifd and an IPv6 attempt held on purpose, when the start publishes IPv4 early, then IPv4 traffic flows during the wait, the IPv6 result adds to the session without a second `wwan_4`, and a kill during the wait leaves no IPv6 call

### IPv6 on the LAN

The LAN is `lan`, the one LAN of the image. A global prefix is an IPv6 prefix of the operator that the network routes to the router.

- **R74:** While the session has IPv6 with a global prefix, the LAN gets an IPv6 prefix from it: a prefix the operator delegates when it gives one, otherwise the session's own /64 extended to the LAN (RFC 7278), with `network.lan.ip6assign` 64. The LAN has no ULA prefix (a local-only IPv6 prefix), with IPv6 or without (D174, D175).
  - *scenario* given `<vm>` with a WAN peer that gives a routed /64, when the session starts, then a LAN client gets an address in that /64 and a default route and reaches a native IPv6 service; when the peer withdraws the /64, then the client gets a default-router lifetime of zero
  - **R74a:** The LAN's router advertisements carry the prefix for addresses the devices make themselves (SLAAC), a default-router lifetime above zero and the router's DNS address (RDNSS), and they start once a global prefix is assigned to the LAN and the router has a usable default route over the session for traffic from that prefix; no ping answer is needed. Before that the LAN gets no advertisement. DHCPv6 stays off, and the advertisements do not tell devices to use it (D175, D198).
    - *scenario* given a session with a /64 and its default route, when the LAN gets the prefix, then an advertisement goes out that carries the prefix, a default-router lifetime above zero and the router's DNS address, also while the probe addresses give no echo
  - **R74b:** In steady IPv4-only service the managed LAN sends no router advertisements or DHCPv6, has no ULA prefix, and odhcpd emits no recurring warning about a missing IPv6 default or public prefix. An ordinary IPv6 withdrawal can produce up to two route warnings, one per phase (from: `tests/vm/96-lan6.sh`, code baseline in the header) (D193).
    - *cmd* `tests/rootfs/run.sh tests/lan6/r74-session.sh` passes the quiet IPv4-only case in the LAN suite
  - **R74c:** When reconciliation confirms that a served managed LAN lost usable IPv6 (R74f), the router attempts an advertisement carrying the old prefix: its preferred lifetime is zero if netifd has deprecated it and at most 60 s otherwise. If the loss persists, it turns managed router advertisements off and sends a final advertisement with router lifetime zero and no Prefix Information Option (PIO), even with a second IPv6 default. On return of a usable assigned prefix and route, normal advertisements resume with the 1800 s preferred-lifetime cap. Missing probes alone do not withdraw the advertisements (R77b) (from: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/L1-lan-ipv6-loss-report.md, Rework, 2026-11-25) (D175, D198, D216).
    - *scenario* given `<vm>` with a served cellular prefix and a second IPv6 default, when an eligible event confirms the loss, then the final advertisement has router lifetime zero and no PIO; the preceding withdrawal attempt carries the old prefix as in the LAN withdrawal rule (R74c)
    - *scenario* given `<vm>` with dual stack, when the peer drops only probe echoes, then LAN IPv6 TCP and advertisements continue; when the route is then removed and a listed event triggers reconciliation, then the two-phase withdrawal applies (R74f)
  - **R74d:** IPv6 on the LAN works while translated IPv4 is down (R76a), and the LAN's IPv4 addresses and DHCPv4 never change with the IPv6 state (D175).
    - *scenario* given `<vm>` with an IPv6-only WAN peer, when its NAT64 stops, then a LAN client keeps its IPv6 connection, its IPv4 address and its DHCPv4 lease
  - **R74e:** The managed LAN's RAM configuration uses committed settings. Staged LuCI edits stay pending and do not enter either withdrawal phase or the resumed configuration. LAN IPv6 reconciliation writes nothing to flash, adds no pending UCI change and leaves other LANs alone (D175, D206).
    - *scenario* given `<vm>` with a second LAN in a kept configuration, when the session gains and loses IPv6 three times, then `uci changes` prints nothing, the overlay gets no write and the second LAN's settings are unchanged
  - **R74f:** The LAN is evaluated at boot and reconciled on every interface hotplug event for `wwan`, `wwan_*` and `lan`, on procd reload triggers for `dhcp`, `network` and `system`, on explicit odhcpd reload, and on the watcher's confirmed in-modem IPv6 loss or return (R49b, R49i). An event starts withdrawal only for a previously served managed LAN with confirmed loss of the call, usable LAN assignment or source-specific cellular route. An unreadable required status or timed-out required route lookup preserves the running configuration; a usable parent result can serve the LAN even if the optional child is absent or unreadable. Pending replay evaluates the current session before completing the owed change (R74g) (D175, D198, D216).
    - *scenario* given the stateful `uqmi` stand-in with both calls, when healthy IPv4 accompanies a confirmed in-modem IPv6 disconnect, then the watcher requests LAN withdrawal without disturbing IPv4; unreadable replies leave the state alone, and a connected IPv6 call with a usable assignment resumes advertisements
  - **R74g:** The image's odhcpd init uses the RAM configuration (R14h), falling back to the persistent quiet configuration if the copy cannot be made at boot. An unchanged interface event with no pending apply sends no signal, and steady IPv4-only `ifup wwan` uses the same external commands as before L1 (measured: stock versus L1 hotplug trace, ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/L1-review.md, round 2, 2026-11-25). A change remains pending until procd successfully accepts the reload signal for `odhcpd` instance `instance1`; matching configuration bytes do not clear that obligation. During a withdrawal the successful first signal leaves the final apply pending through the wait and any interruption. An eligible reload retries the change, including the existing watcher at roughly 30 s intervals and the next non-dry-run recovery cycle for an odhcpd pending change (from: `lw-linkwatch` and `wwan-recover`, code baseline in the header).

    The boot path recomputes the configuration from the current session. A replay does not start a stopped daemon and adds no background job. After `apk upgrade odhcpd-ipv6only` the stock init returns and managed LAN IPv6 is unavailable until the next flash (D206, D216).
    - *scenario* given a pending LAN change after a failed signal or interrupted reload, when an eligible reconciliation succeeds, then odhcpd applies the configuration for the current session and clears the pending work after its final signal
- **R75:** Over IPv6 no new connection from the LTE side reaches a LAN device or the router: TCP, UDP and any other traffic that does not answer a connection from inside is dropped. Allowed in are only the ICMPv6 messages of the stock rules (errors such as Packet Too Big, Destination Unreachable and Time Exceeded, forwarded echo requests, and to the router the neighbour and router messages) and DHCPv6 answers to the router. Every interface the session adds (an IPv6 child, the translation interface) is in the firewall zone `wan` (D176, D190).
  - *scenario* given `<vm>` with a LAN client that holds a global address, when the WAN peer opens a TCP connection, sends UDP to port 500 and sends ESP to it, then nothing reaches the client, while a Packet Too Big message and an echo request do
  - **R75a:** The stock rules that let ESP and UDP port 500 (IPsec) from `wan` to `lan` (`Allow-IPSec-ESP` and `Allow-ISAKMP`, from: stock `/etc/config/firewall` of 25.12.6, via ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/E-astra-report.md, finding 5) hold for IPv4 only in the image's defaults, so they let nothing in over IPv6, and for IPv4 they stay as they are. After a flash that keeps settings, each of the two rules that is still as stock gets the same IPv4 limit, while a rule the owner changed or deleted stays changed or absent (D189, D201).
    - *scenario* given a clean flash or a kept configuration with the two unchanged IPsec rules, when first boot applies the LAN migration, then both rules have `family` `ipv4`; an edited or deleted rule stays edited or absent (firewall migration, R7e; fixture `tests/firstboot/r7e-r75a.sh`)

### IPv4 over an IPv6-only session

464XLAT gives IPv4 over IPv6. The router's translator (CLAT) sends IPv6 packets toward the operator's translator (PLAT), which turns them back into IPv4 (from: RFC 6877, April 2013). DNS64 is the operator's DNS that answers with an IPv6 address for a name that has only IPv4 addresses.

- **R76:** With an IPv6-only session, a usable DNS64 answer and a working operator NAT64 (the PLAT), the router and LAN devices reach IPv4 addresses and IPv4-only names through CLAT (D169, D200, D209).
  - *scenario* given `<vm>` with a WAN peer that gives IPv6 only, DNS64 and a test NAT64, when the session starts, then the router and a LAN client open a TCP connection to an IPv4 address and to a name with only an IPv4 address
  - **R76a:** The translation takes the first usable AAAA answer to `ipv4only.arpa` from the IPv6 child's DNS servers in order (from: RFC 7050, November 2013). Discovery gives each server up to 4 s (from: `LC_DNS_LIMIT` in `src/files/usr/sbin/lw-clat-check`, code baseline in the header), uses the child's cellular device and skips link-local servers. This image accepts only a `/96` embedding `192.0.0.170` or `192.0.0.171` with the zero u octet, excluding `::/16`, link-local and multicast, and refuses the other RFC 6052 lengths (from: RFC 6052 §2.2, October 2010, and `lc_p96`, code baseline in the header; operator scope O6). A changed session or DNS path invalidates the answer. With no usable answer the router stays IPv6-only and records one notice-level `lw-clat` syslog line for DNS64 per CLAT session identity without restarting `wwan` (DNS discovery choice O7) (D186).

    A cycle re-asks DNS64 to check for PLAT movement only for a locally up translation of the current session whose IPv4 probe in that cycle failed (`lw-clat-check tick v4fail`). It uses that probe result without another ping; a cycle that skips probing does not run this check. A different usable answer and a known previous prefix allow replacement under the rebuild rule (R76c). The same prefix, no answer or an unusable answer keeps the existing translation in this check and gives at most one DNS64 notice per session identity. Renumbering can separately ask DNS64 when it needs a prefix for replacement (R76c).
    - *scenario* given an IPv6-only bench without a DNS64 answer, when the answer returns and the next translation check runs, then IPv6 works throughout and IPv4 comes up without a restart of `wwan`
    - *scenario* given locally up CLAT with a saved PLAT prefix, when translated IPv4 stops answering the current recovery cycle's probe and DNS64 supplies a different usable prefix, then IPv4 is restored through that prefix without restarting `wwan`; with the same prefix or no usable answer, the existing translation stays
  - **R76b:** Only a confirmed IPv6-only session can start a translation. An IPv4 call granted at setup keeps the session ineligible even if that call stops answering. With no earlier translation to clean up, the native IPv4 path, including a dual-stack session with a DHCPv6 child, adds no CLAT process or call (D186, D200, D210).
    - *scenario* given a dual-stack session with `wwan_6` and no old translation, when that child comes up, then no translation check starts; given a later IPv6-only session, when its child comes up with usable DNS64, then `wwan_6_4` comes up
  - **R76c:** A failed local setup or stopped helper is retried on IPv6-only child up, on parent up if a translation remains active, or at the next eligible cycle. A cycle checks translation for an IPv6-only session or when cleanup is due. A translation retry leaves `wwan` up while IPv6 is online. The interface is locally up when netifd reports the expected device up and its own CLAT helper is alive. A local setup failure gets one notice per unchanged failure state, and a setup ended by loss of its session gives no setup-failure line. The check uses no modem calls or standing job, and times out after 90 s on interface events or 60 s in a cycle, with 2 s of kill grace (from: `src/files/etc/hotplug.d/iface/90-lw-lan6` and `src/files/usr/sbin/wwan-recover`, code baseline in the header) (D186, D212).

    A locally up translation of the same session follows a changed cellular /64 on `ifupdate wwan_6` (`lw-clat-check update` when a translation is active) or an eligible cycle. The update can rebuild only when the translation's valid IPv6 address is outside the complete, validated set of the child's /64s, and performs no ordinary setup or dead-helper recovery. Missing or invalid address data keeps the translation, and reordering, repeats or changes to host bits within the same set do not trigger another rebuild.

    A PLAT move or /64 renumbering takes the current translation down only once a usable replacement prefix is known. Renumbering can use the validated saved prefix or a fresh DNS64 answer, with preference for the fresh answer on `tick v4fail`; without either, the existing translation stays. After teardown the new interface is created only if the IPv6-only identity and the network path, including the /64 set, still match.
    - *scenario* given an active translation whose address is no longer in any validated /64 from `wwan_6`, when the child sends an update and a usable replacement prefix is known, then the new translation uses the new /64 without restarting `wwan`; if the prefix is unavailable or the address list cannot be validated, the existing translation stays
    - *scenario* given the IPv6-only bench, when the translation's setup fails three times and then works, then IPv4 works through one translation device with one route and rule set, and nothing restarted `wwan`
  - **R76d:** The translation is the dynamic interface `wwan_6_4`, protocol `464xlat`, attached to `wwan_6` as `@wwan_6` with `tunlink wwan_6`, the discovered `ip6prefix` and the firewall zone of `wwan`. The device is `464-wwan_6_4`; `wwan_4` holds the native IPv4 lease (D191).
  - **R76e:** Every image holds the packages `464xlat` and `kmod-nat46`, listed under Included in `packages.md` (R2). They total 21 327 bytes of package files (from: `apk fetch` from the 25.12.6 feeds, `packages.md`, 2026-11-24) (D169, D209).
  - **R76f:** At a confirmed session end, including an `ifdown` without a following `ifup`, or on return to native IPv4, the old translation and its owned resources are removed. An unreadable or incomplete parent status leaves the translation alone (translation ownership, O9).
  - **R76g:** Discovery uses the cellular device for DNS64 and preserves existing host routes. If it needs a route, its temporary `/128` uses the child's device and gateway when known. If inspection fails or the path leaves on another device, that server is skipped. A route installed for discovery is removed when the query finishes or an interruption is handled. A failed deletion prevents setup in that attempt and gets a retry before the worker exits. A later check retries interrupted route cleanup using the recorded DNS server, device and gateway with the fixed metric of 1 (from: `lc_pin_recover`, code baseline in the header); it preserves entries with a different device, gateway or metric (shared DNS discovery routes, O7).
  - **R76h:** CLAT comes up without enabling a CLAT option in LuCI (D213).
  - **R76i:** An unreadable translation status preserves its state and resources. Only confirmed removal permits deleting the remaining owned resources or adding another interface of the same name (`ubus -S list network.interface.wwan_6_4` exit 4; measured: one absent-object query on a live ubus in ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/C1-review.md, round 3, 2026-11-24) (translation ownership, O9).

    Before lonewrt requests stock teardown, `/var/run/464-wwan_6_4.pid` is cleared unless it names the live helper with a numeric PID, the `464xlatcfg` executable basename, `464-wwan_6_4` as its first argument and no newline in the command line. Lonewrt retains the verified helper PID after successful setup and through teardown, and verifies ownership again before signaling it. A replacement cannot open the same device while its verified old helper is still exiting.
  - **R76j:** When `ifdown` cannot finish cleanup, cleanup remains due for the current worker or a later cycle. Confirmed removal satisfies it; a confirmed IPv6-only session transfers the decision to that session's translation check (translation ownership, O9). Teardown retains the old address and verified PID until cleanup completes; an unreadable rule list, a remaining owned rule or a helper that has not exited leaves evidence for another attempt.
  - **R76k:** A ready IPv6-only session with a changed CLAT session identity replaces the previous translation before starting the new one, even when the modem reuses both handles (translation ownership, O9).

### Link repair

- **R14:** Watchcat runs the family check every 60 s, one packet per address, and keeps one silence timer fed by the check's combined result: when no check is online for `LINK_RESTART_AFTER_S` seconds, 120 s by default, it restarts the interface `wwan` (the logical interface, not one of its children), and does so again after every such period of silence, even after the restart limit. The wait after boot and the reset of the timer after a restart stay as in stock watchcat. The image adds the family check to watchcat's own round as a guarded hook, whose exact form is the builder's (hook form under Builder decides) (D6, D79, D173, D185, D195).
  - **R14b:** The hook acts only for the watchcat entry `wwan_soft`, which the image selects by an explicit option passed only for that entry; any other entry, also a second one of the owner for `wwan`, checks and restarts as stock watchcat does. For `wwan_soft` the hook pings the addresses of Terms for the session's families, so its `pinghosts` and `addressfamily` options, which LuCI shows, have no effect (D195).
    - *scenario* given `<vm>` with a second watchcat entry of the owner for `wwan`, when the period of `wwan_soft` is changed in LuCI and the service restarts, then `wwan_soft` runs the family check with the new period, the second entry pings as stock, and the wait after boot and the timer reset after a restart are those of stock watchcat
  - **R14c:** The build derives the edited watchcat files from the files of the package that goes into the image, with the same guards as for `qmi.sh` (an anchor not there exactly as often as expected stops the build), keeps their upstream notices, and the repository holds no copy of them (D195).
  - **R14d:** A family check of watchcat ends within 40 s with its device lookups included: each lookup of netifd ends within 2 s, and the check, lookups included, is cut at 37 s; a device that netifd does not name is skipped for that check and never pinged without a device; only the scheduled checks of watchcat count towards a family lost (Terms) (D195).
  - **R14e:** Watchcat stays under its existing service controls: its LuCI page (R30a) and the card's instructions for it and for the separate scheduled router restart (R33a) work as before (D195).
  - **R14f:** When the `wwan_soft` silence timer expires with a valid negotiation mark, watchcat holds its restart until the mark disappears or its deadline expires. The handler publishes the mark at negotiation start and keeps it through settings publication and the launch of children, removing it on normal setup exit or failed negotiation. If the mark disappears early, watchcat repeats the family check until an answer or the earlier of 20 s and the clamped deadline, with a 1 s wait between failed attempts (from: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/F3-gemini-fixes-report.md and ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/F3-review.md, 2026-11-24). At the deadline the normal restart decision applies without another check, and an absent, expired or invalid mark leaves normal timing (D197, D207).
    - *scenario* given a start whose IPv6 attempt hangs and a silence timer that runs out 10 s into it, when the start publishes IPv4 before its deadline, then watchcat runs no restart; given a start that hangs past its deadline, then watchcat restarts `wwan` once after the guard; given a mark far in the future, then watchcat restarts at most 60 s after the timer ran out
    - *cmd* `tests/qmi/r14f-mark.sh` and `tests/watchcat/r14f-start-wait.sh`
  - **R14g:** After each scheduled family check of `wwan_soft` in a session with IPv6, watchcat's hook writes that check's IPv6 result for the session watcher (R49d), through one function of `probe.sh` that no other check calls. The file is `/var/run/lw-family/ipv6`, in the family state directory (R14h), replaced at once (written beside it and renamed), with one line of four fields separated by single spaces: `<cid_4>/<pdh_4> <birth> <second> <result>`. `cid_4` and `pdh_4` are the strings of the status read the check used; birth is the whole second of `/proc/uptime` minus netifd's `uptime` of `wwan` in that read, or `-` without one; second is the whole `/proc/uptime` second at which the check's IPv6 pings started; result is `answer` (a cellular IPv6 answer, or traffic by the measure of the IPv6 traffic measure (R77e)), `none` (pinged on a ready IPv6 device, no answer, and the measure quiet), `unknown` (pinged on a ready IPv6 device, no answer, and no valid sample for the measure) or `nodev` (no ready IPv6 device, nothing pinged; it comes before every other word). In a session without IPv6 nothing is written and nothing removed. Only the reader judges a result: the watcher counts an exact `none` of its own session (same `cid_4/pdh_4`, birth within 6 s, second at or after the loss), and anything else, `unknown` and a file in a directory that fails the check of the RAM state rule (R14h) included, keeps IPv6 (D204, D207, D214).
    - *scenario* given a dual-stack session, when two scheduled family checks of `wwan_soft` get no IPv6 answer and the device carries no IPv6 traffic between them, then `/var/run/lw-family/ipv6` holds one line with the session's `cid_4/pdh_4`, its birth, the second the IPv6 pings of the second check started and `none`, and the directory has mode 0700; when the first check of the session gets no answer, then the line says `unknown`; when `wwan-recover` runs a family check, then the file does not change
  - **R14h:** The guarded repair state lives under `/var/run`: `/var/run/lw-family` holds the negotiation mark (R14f) and the family result (R14g), and `/var/run/lw-linkwatch` holds the session watcher's markers (`escalated`, `restores`, `pause_until`, `v6lost` and `v6reload`; R49d, R49e, R49i). The writer makes each with mode 0700, and its repair-state readers and writers check that it is a root-owned directory and not a link. A failed check writes nothing and believes nothing, which keeps the fail-safe: no mark gives watchcat's stock timing (R14f), no family result keeps IPv6 (R49d), and no watcher file means no pause, no count of restores and no held escalation, so every incident may escalate on its own (R49d, R49e). `/var/run` is `/tmp/run`, owned by root with mode 0755, made by `/etc/init.d/boot` before netifd and watchcat start (measured: `ls -ld /var/run` and `/etc/init.d/boot` on the owner's router, 2026-11-24) (D207).

    `/var/run/lw-odhcpd/dhcp` is the managed RAM configuration. Its `phase` records the applied state and `pending` records the owed state (`active`, `w` for withdrawal or `off`) (R74g). The watcher keeps the one-line loss identity `<cid_6>/<pdh_6> <birth>` in `/var/run/lw-linkwatch/v6lost` (`-` when the birth is unknown) and an existence marker for a pending LAN reload in `/var/run/lw-linkwatch/v6reload` (R49i); these flags survive a watcher respawn. Reboot clears the RAM phase and flags, and odhcpd recomputes the configuration at boot.

    CLAT uses a separate RAM directory, `/var/run/lw-clat`, for its lock, session and translation records, the PLAT prefix, the handled /64 set, the temporary route record, and deferred cleanup with the saved helper PID and teardown address (R12d, R76c, R76g, R76i, R76j). The directory checks above apply to `lw-family` and `lw-linkwatch`; the CLAT directory's protection is tracked under Unverified. The ladder's traffic sample and its lock remain `/tmp/wwan-recover.run` and `/tmp/wwan-recover.lock` (R21a, R77e, O8).
  - **R14i:** The negotiation mark is `/var/run/lw-family/negotiating`, a regular file with one whole uptime second giving the negotiation deadline (R73h). A reader rejects links and nonnumeric values or an invalid state directory, and caps the accepted deadline at 60 s ahead of the read (D207).
    - *scenario* given a regular numeric mark beyond the cap, an expired mark, and a mark reached through a linked state directory, when the watcher reads them, then only the first holds the poll and its deadline is capped
    - *scenario* given a link, or a file, in place of `/var/run/lw-family`, when a start and a family check run, then nothing is written behind it, watchcat keeps its stock timing, and a valid `none` line behind it keeps IPv6; given a link in place of `/var/run/lw-linkwatch`, when the watcher escalates, then the `ifup` goes out and nothing is written behind the link
    - *cmd* `tests/qmi/r14f-mark.sh`, `tests/watchcat/r14f-start-wait.sh`, `tests/watchcat/r14g-seam.sh`, `tests/linkwatch/r14g-writer-feed.sh`, `tests/linkwatch/r49d-sibling-escalation.sh` and `tests/linkwatch/escalate.sh`
  - **R14a:** `LINK_RESTART_AFTER_S` is a key of every profile: a whole number of seconds from 120 to 900, written as the `period` of the watchcat entry `wwan_soft`; nothing else of that entry (mode, interface, hosts, ping period of 60 s) depends on the profile. A value outside the range, not a number or empty stops the build with the key's name and the range (R3). The lower bound is two ping periods, so a restart needs at least two pings in a row without an answer. The upper bound keeps the interface restart ahead of the first router restart of `wwan-recover` (R16), which comes at the 6th cycle of a failure, in two cases. (a) Steady state: the drop is at D and the router has been up for more than 10 min (R17). The first failed cycle is in [D, D+300), so the 6th cycle is at D+1500 at the earliest. Watchcat restarts the interface about `60 * ceil(period / 60) + 20` s after its last answer (the checks run every 60 s, a failed ping waits 10 s per host; from: watchcat runs `ping -c 1` without `-W`, and busybox `ping` then waits 10 s), so with 900 s it comes by D+920 at the latest, and the link is back about 15 s later (the restart of the interface takes 14 to 15 s, measured on IPv4, D82), by D+935. (b) A failure already there at boot: watchcat waits until uptime `period` before it counts, so with 900 s its first restart is at about 1820 s of uptime and the link is back at about 1835 s. `wwan-recover` starts a failure only from uptime 600 (R17), so its first cycle is in [600, 900) and its 6th cycle, the router restart, is at 2100 s at the earliest. With 900 s the interface restart comes first in both cases, with 565 s to spare in (a) and 265 s in (b) (D79). A session with both families has four addresses, and a failed family check takes up to 40 s instead of 20 s (R77c), which moves both restarts 20 s later: the link is back by D+955 in (a) and at about 1855 s in (b), with 545 s and 245 s to spare. The order "interface restart, then router restart" of part 1 and of the restart ladder (R16) therefore holds for every value in the range.
    - *cmd* `tests/build/10-profile.sh` expects 120 and every value in range as the `period` of `wwan_soft` in the rendered `etc/config/watchcat`, and a stop naming the key and the range for 0, 119, 901, a leading zero, a sign, a fraction, text, a space, fullwidth digits and an empty value (expected)
    - *cmd* `tests/firstboot/generic.sh` and `tests/firstboot/full.sh` expect `watchcat.wwan_soft.period` 120 and 180 after the first start (expected)
    - *scenario* given the generic image and a failure the session watcher does not cover (R49b), such as a data path that stops while the session reads connected, or any drop with `/etc/init.d/lw-linkwatch stop`, when the pings fail, then watchcat restarts `wwan` about 80 to 140 s later (the 2nd failed ping 60 s after the 1st, each waiting 10 s per host) and the link is back about 15 s after that, with no router restart
- **R77:** Every check of the image that decides whether the internet works counts online as Terms defines it: watchcat (R14), the cycles and classes of `wwan-recover` (R15 to R23), the `/status` verdict and its ping (R39c), the 2G message (R40c), the internet-back message (R40), sending what waited for the internet (R34a, R72), the speed test's failure line (R41c), the SMS `status` reply (R46a) and Tailscale's internet test (R68b). A session is online while any family answers, including an IPv6-only session without IPv4 service, and no check counts an answer over the tunnel, Tailscale or another interface (D173).
  - *scenario* given an IPv6-only session with working IPv6 and the translation down, when watchcat's period and 10 cycles of `wwan-recover` pass, then nothing restarts `wwan` or the router, there is no EDGE and no failure, and `/status` says the internet works
  - *scenario* given an IPv4-only session that stops answering while a route over the WireGuard tunnel would answer, when the pings go, then watchcat and `wwan-recover` repair as before
  - **R77a:** A session without IPv6 gets no ping to an IPv6 address and, at the info level, no log line about a missing or refused IPv6 (R73b); a confirmed IPv6-only session gets IPv4 pings only while netifd reports `wwan_6_4` up (D185).
    - *scenario* given an IPv4-only session at the info level, when the router runs for a day, then the stand-in `ping` sees no IPv6 address and syslog has no line that names IPv6
  - **R77b:** When a family of the session becomes a family lost while another answers, syslog gets one line naming the family, and one more at its first answer after that; such a state is no failure (R15) and starts no repair other than that of the family itself (R49h, R49i, R76c), and that repair runs only where its result can be checked; nothing restarts `wwan` on a timer for a lost family, and a full restart is left to the owner. While netifd reports the translation up, the same lost-family rule applies to its IPv4 probes. When the translation interface stops being up, watchcat clears the IPv4 loss state without a loss or return line (D185, D200).
    - *scenario* given a dual-stack session, when IPv6 stops answering for 3 family checks of watchcat and then answers, then syslog has one line for the loss and one for the return, and `wwan-recover` counts no failure
  - **R77c:** Each family check reads the session's devices from netifd anew, so a new start or a translation that comes or goes is followed at the next check, and a family check ends within 40 s, the device lookups included. The IPv6 probe device comes from the first up interface among `wwan_6` and `wwan` with an IPv6 address or, if none is present on that interface, a prefix in 2000::/3 (D185, D195).
    - *scenario* given a session whose translation interface goes away between two checks, when the next family check runs, then it pings no address through the old device and ends within 40 s
  - **R77d:** In a session with IPv6, traffic measured on its IPv6 device (R77e) keeps watchcat's timer fed and counts as online for the ladder. Quiet and unknown leave those decisions to the probes. A due EDGE restart still follows its ladder threshold (R16), and the LTE probe and fallback follow their own thresholds (R23). An IPv4-only session reads no IPv6 traffic counters (D200, D214).
    - *scenario* given `<vm>` with an IPv6-only WAN peer that drops only the echoes to the probe addresses, when a LAN client keeps a TCP transfer over IPv6 running past watchcat's period and 10 cycles of `wwan-recover`, then nothing restarts `wwan` or the router
    - *scenario* given a dual-stack session whose IPv4 call is lost while TCP over IPv6 flows and the IPv6 probes get no answer, when a full watchcat period and six cycles of `wwan-recover` pass, then nothing restarts `wwan` or the router and the family result is `answer` (R14g); when the IPv6 traffic stops too, then watchcat restarts `wwan` under the watchcat restart rule (R14) and the ladder restarts the router at its sixth cycle without traffic
    - *scenario* given a session on EDGE with IPv6 traffic, when a restart becomes due, then the router restarts at the scheduled cycle and keeps the repair spacing of the restart ladder (R16); in passive mode, traffic leaves the LTE probe due at its scheduled cycle (R23)
    - *cmd* `tests/watchcat/r77d-traffic.sh`, `tests/watchcat/r49d-dual-traffic.sh` and `tests/ladder/r77d-traffic.sh`
  - **R77e:** The IPv6 traffic measure compares the per-device IPv6 counters of the kernel in `/proc/net/dev_snmp6/<dev>` with the sample the same check took last time: IPv6 in is the rise of `Ip6InReceives` less the rise of `Icmp6InMsgs`, IPv6 out the rise of `Ip6OutTransmits` less the rise of `Icmp6OutMsgs`, so the router's own ICMPv6 (the probes, router advertisements, neighbour discovery) and IPv4 never count (measured: these counter names in the file of `wwan0` on the owner's router, an IPv4-only session showing 14 sent, 12 of them ICMPv6, and nothing received, 2026-11-24). It has three results: traffic, at least 4 packets in and 4 out; quiet, a valid sample with less; unknown, no valid sample. A sample is valid only for the same device and the same session (the call numbers of the IPv4 call, else of the IPv6 call, and the birth from the family result record (R14g), within 6 s) and up to two periods of its check old (watchcat: two ping periods plus the 37 s bound of a check, R14d; the ladder: 600 s); no sample, a missing file or counter, a counter that is not a number or went back, and a sample of another device or session or an older one give unknown. The counters are compared as 64-bit numbers (measured: the image's busybox `ash` on the owner's router, armv7l, adds and compares values above 2^32 and up to 2^63 - 1 correctly, 2026-11-24) (D214).
    - *scenario* given a session whose device received router advertisements and neighbour discovery only, when two checks pass, then the measure is quiet; given the first check of a new session, then it is unknown; given counters that went back, then it is unknown
    - *cmd* `tests/watchcat/r77d-measure.sh`; `tests/ladder/ipv4-trace.sh` expects the ladder of a forced-IPv4 session byte for byte as the script before the measure
- **R15:** An online cycle in LTE-only mode ends the failure and resets its counter (in the LTE probe after the limit only with the mode confirmed, R23), and the first cycle that is not online starts a failure (D6, D30).
- **R16:** During a failure the router restarts after 6, 18, 42, 90, 162 and 234 cycles from its start, that is after about 30 min, 1.5 h, 3.5 h, 7.5 h, 13.5 h and 19.5 h, even when it is on EDGE (D7, D11, D30).
  - *cmd* `tests/ladder/hang.sh` (modem hang, 250 cycles) expects `REBOOT` entries at cycles 6, 18, 42, 90, 162, 234 (expected)
  - *cmd* `tests/ladder/edge-works.sh` (dead LTE, working EDGE) expects the same restarts and an `EDGE enter` entry in the first cycle after the boot grace after every restart (expected)
  - **R16a:** The restart happens in the first cycle in which the failure counter reached or passed the threshold, even when the threshold cycle was skipped (the first 10 min after a start, the lock).
- **R49:** The service `lw-linkwatch` (`/usr/sbin/lw-linkwatch run`, procd, respawned without limit, enabled in every image, started after the network) looks at the data session of `wwan` every 5 s. When the mobile network detaches the modem with a re-attach request and the modem attaches again by itself, the session stays dead for the host: the modem is registered, but the call is gone, while netifd still shows `wwan` as up with the old address. The watcher notices this within 5 s and restores the session in place in about 2 s, without `ifup`. watchcat (R14) and `wwan-recover` (R16) are unchanged and stay behind it as the later lines of defence (D80).
  - *scenario* given the generic image and a modem that is registered but out of call (made with `AT+CGATT=0` then `AT+CGATT=1` on `/dev/ttyUSB1`), when the next pass runs, then within about 7 s the interface `wwan` has a working ping again, with the new address, no `ifup` and no router restart, and syslog has one `lw-linkwatch` line with the outcome
  - *cmd* `tests/run-all.sh linkwatch` exits 0 with no failures or skips
  - **R49a:** The watcher makes no uqmi call unless `ifstatus wwan` shows the interface up, the control node of `network.wwan.device` exists, no valid negotiation mark is present (R14f, R14i), and, when early publication is used, netifd shows no early setup still running (R73p), and netifd's data of `wwan` holds a native IPv4 call (`cid_4` and a numeric `pdh_4`), whatever `network.wwan.pdptype` says; a session without native IPv4 gets no call at all (D177, D199, D207); with netifd showing it down it makes no call at all, and an interface that netifd takes down during a restore ends the restore at once, with no `ifup` of its own. Every uqmi call has `-t` and a `timeout` around it, and no other script of the image runs uqmi (R12c). The watcher talks to the modem over AT only through the helper and lock of the AT lock rule (R12a), and only for the snapshot of the watcher heartbeat rule (R49g) (D80).

    The IPv6 status and settings reads require a healthy native IPv4 status in the same pass, the published IPv6 fields `cid_6` and `pdh_6`, and a fresh check of the session, control node and negotiation mark (R49h). A healthy dual-stack pass adds one netifd read before the IPv6 status request (from: `lk_v6_watch` and `lk_v6_own`, code baseline in the header). A steady native IPv4-only pass without pending work adds no modem request or netifd read. An IPv6-only session still makes no modem request.
    - *scenario* given an already published native IPv4 call and a valid negotiation mark, when the watcher checks the session, then it makes no modem call while the mark is valid
    - *cmd* `tests/linkwatch/netifd.sh`, `tests/linkwatch/interface.sh` and `tests/linkwatch/service.sh` pass in the linkwatch suite
  - **R49b:** The session counts as lost only when the data status on netifd's IPv4 client (`uqmi -s -d <node> -t 3000 --set-client-id wds,<cid_4> --get-data-status`, which answers `"connected"` on a working call of the MF289F, measured: that command on the owner's router, 2026-11-23) is `"disconnected"` and the current settings on netifd's client (`--set-client-id wds,<cid_4 of the data object of wwan> --get-current-settings`) are `"Out of call"`. A data status of `"connected"` keeps IPv4 on its healthy path and permits the IPv6 check when the published sibling passes R49a. Any other answer, an empty one, garbage, a failing call, a timeout, settings with an address, or no `cid_4` in the data of netifd, leads to no action: a hung or confused uqmi must never cause a restart (D80, D177).

    On its own published IPv6 client, an eligible watcher pass reads `--get-data-status`. A new `"disconnected"` result needs `--get-current-settings` to return `"Out of call"` before recording loss. An unreadable, failing, ambiguous or timed-out reply leaves the IPv6 loss state alone. A later `"connected"` for the same flagged call clears the loss, and an already flagged disconnected call needs no repeat settings request (R49h, R49i).
    - *cmd* `tests/linkwatch/healthy.sh` and `tests/linkwatch/answers.sh` pass in the linkwatch suite
  - **R49c:** A lost IPv4 call is restored in place in every session that has one (R49a), with auto and forced IPv4 alike (D177). The restore, repeated every 2 s while it runs: when the modem is not `registered` (`--get-serving-system`), wait; when it is, start the call on netifd's client with the call that qmi.sh makes, `uqmi -s -d <node> -t 5000 --set-client-id wds,<cid_4> --start-network` followed by `--apn`, `--profile`, `--auth-type`, `--username` and `--password`, in the stock handler's order, each only when the option of `network.wwan` is set (`apn`, `profile`, `auth`, `username`, `password`), and `--autoconnect` unless `network.wwan.autoconnect` is `0`, as the IPv4 call of the start had it (R73g). In a session with static IPv4 (`dhcp` `0`, R73p) no `udhcpc` runs and no signal goes out: the restore is done only when the new call keeps the old address, and otherwise the incident ends by the 20 s rule of the watcher escalation rule (R49d). On a numeric handle the watcher sends USR2 to the `udhcpc` of the device (device name from netifd, `l3_device` of `wwan`, else of `wwan_4`; pid from `/var/run/udhcpc-<device>.pid`, only when that process is named `udhcpc`), waits until the release is done, and only then sends USR1: the release followed by the renew gives the new lease within the second, a renew alone takes about 13 s. The release is done when the device no longer has the address it had before USR2 (udhcpc runs its deconfig script inside the release and netifd removes the address); the watcher looks at the address up to 25 times in a row (about 0.2 s, no nap between the looks), and when the address is still there after that, or the device had none, it waits 1 s. USR1 never goes out with USR2 still pending, because two pending signals arrive in number order, USR1 first, and udhcpc would renew and then release and end without an address until the next request 6 s later. A non-numeric answer is retried; after `"No effect"` in a session with the IPv4 call alone, the client is cleared first with `--set-client-id wds,<cid_4> --stop-network 0xffffffff --autoconnect`, as qmi.sh does, while in a session where another call may exist (netifd's data holds an IPv6 call, or its state is unknown) no such stop goes out and the start is retried as it is (D196). The restore is done when the data status is `"connected"` and the device has the IPv4 address that `--get-current-settings` reports; a lease that does not arrive is asked for again every 6 s. A handle that differs from `pdh_4` of netifd goes into the outcome line (R49f); netifd's data is not changed (D80).
    - *cmd* `tests/linkwatch/restore.sh`, `restore-options.sh`, `retry.sh`, `handle.sh` and `escalate.sh` pass in the linkwatch suite
  - **R49d:** When the session is not restored 20 s after the loss was seen, the watcher runs `ifup wwan` once, logs it, and takes no further action for this incident, however many passes follow. When the session also has an IPv6 call, the watcher runs `ifup wwan` only when a family check of this session, taken after the loss on the session's IPv6 device, has the result `none` (R14g); with `answer`, `unknown` or `nodev`, or with no such result (none yet, an older one, one of an earlier session, or one in a directory that fails the check of the RAM state rule (R14h)), it runs no `ifup`, logs that IPv6 is kept, and takes no further action for this incident; IPv4 then comes back at the next start of the interface or a restart by hand (D177, D196, D200, D204, D214). A new incident can start only after a pass has seen the data status `"connected"`, or when the session changed (R49h): the escalation marker holds only for the session (`cid_4`, `pdh_4` and birth, R14g) in which it was set. A nonnumeric birth in the escalation marker is ignored. The marker of the escalation is in RAM (`/var/run/lw-linkwatch/escalated`, R14h), so a respawn of the service keeps it (D207). The trade-off when working IPv6 prevents escalation is the dual-stack recovery question (O14) (D80).
    - *cmd* `tests/linkwatch/escalate.sh` and `tests/linkwatch/loop.sh` pass in the linkwatch suite
  - **R49e:** Three restores within 10 minutes mean the restore does not hold (a data plan that ran out may look like this). After the third the watcher takes no action for 10 minutes, with no uqmi call at all, logs that once, and then resumes with the count at zero; the watchcat and `wwan-recover` repairs go on meanwhile. The times are in RAM (`/var/run/lw-linkwatch`, R14h) (D80, D207).
    - *cmd* `tests/linkwatch/brake.sh` pass in the linkwatch suite
  - **R49f:** At the default level the watcher writes to syslog (tag `lw-linkwatch`, so also to the persistent log of the persistent log rule (R29)) one line per IPv4 incident, with its outcome and duration: `data session lost with the modem registered, restored in place after 4 s, address 10.1.2.3 -> 10.4.5.6` (with `data session lost, modem was not registered at first,` in front of the same words when it had to wait for the registration, and `, new data handle <h> (netifd has <pdh_4>)` at the end when the handle differs), `data session lost, not restored in place after 20 s, ifup wwan`, `data session lost, not restored in place after 20 s, IPv6 kept, no ifup`, `data session lost, the session changed, nothing more to do`, `data session lost, no network device name from netifd, ifup wwan` (in a session that also has an IPv6 call, the rule of the watcher escalation rule (R49d) decides, with `IPv6 kept, no ifup` in place of `ifup wwan`), `data session lost, netifd took wwan down after 3 s, nothing more to do`, `3 restores in 10 min, pausing for 10 min`. A pass with no incident or IPv6 transition writes no routine notice or flash state; the heartbeat follows R49g and debug logging follows R29c. The lines carry no APN login (D80).

    For IPv6 the tag records one line at each confirmed transition: `IPv6 call lost, IPv4 kept, the LAN stops advertising IPv6` and `IPv6 call connected again, the LAN follows the session again` (R49b, R49i). A retry or another pass in the same loss does not repeat the transition line.
    - *cmd* `tests/linkwatch/healthy.sh`, `restore.sh` and `brake.sh` pass in the linkwatch suite
  - **R49g:** At the debug level (R29c) the watcher also logs, at `daemon.debug`: every step of an incident with its time since the loss (`+2s ...`); after the restore, never before it, a snapshot of the modem through `lw_at` (`AT+CEER`, `AT+CEREG?`, `AT+CGATT?`, `AT+CGACT?`, `AT+CGPADDR=1`, 5 s each with the lock wait, stopping at the first command that does not answer); every answer of the watcher status rule (R49b) that is neither healthy nor lost, and every uqmi call that failed, returned nothing or took 2 s or more; and every 5 minutes a heartbeat with the number of passes, the number of failed or slow uqmi calls and the slowest answer time since the last heartbeat. At the info level none of these lines is written (D80).
    - *cmd* `tests/linkwatch/debug.sh`, `heartbeat.sh` and `answers.sh` pass in the linkwatch suite
  - **R49h:** An incident belongs to the session in which it began: when `wwan` is no longer confirmed up, the IPv4 call identity is different or absent, or two known session births differ by more than 6 s (R14g), the watcher ends it with no further call, signal or `ifup`. A restore of the IPv4 call leaves the session's IPv6 call up, and no exit of an incident restarts `wwan` while that call works or its state is unknown (R49c, R49d, R49f) (D177, D196).

    Before each IPv6 request and before setting or clearing the IPv6 loss flag, a fresh read must still show the pass's IPv4 identity and a matching birth when known, the same IPv6 client and handle, the control node present and no valid negotiation mark. Failure of any check abandons that IPv6 action (R49a, R49i).

    - *cmd* `tests/linkwatch/escalate.sh` and `tests/linkwatch/restore.sh` with session fixtures: native IPv4 alone, both calls with IPv4 lost, an IPv6-only session (no call at all), a session that changes during the incident, IPv6 online at the escalation (no `ifup`), no IPv6 result or one of an earlier session at the escalation (no `ifup`), `"No effect"` and a missing device with an IPv6 call (no wildcard stop, no `ifup`), a start still running (no call), and IPv6 lost while IPv4 is online (IPv6 observation without an IPv4 action, R49i); the listed watcher fixtures passed on the review snapshot
  - **R49i:** A loss or return confirmed by the IPv6 read of the watcher status rule (R49b) while IPv4 is healthy requests LAN reconciliation only (R74f), leaving IPv4 running without a start, stop, signal, `ifup wwan` or reboot. The RAM loss flag identifies the IPv6 client and handle and the session birth when known; only a matching flag withdraws the LAN, with known births compared as in the call ownership rule (R49h). A confirmed return of that call clears the flag, and advertising resumes once the session has a usable assignment and route (R74c). The reload marker is recorded before changing the flag and stays until the reload succeeds (R14h, R74g) (D177, D200).
- **R17:** In the first 10 min after a start `wwan-recover` keeps counting the cycles of an ongoing failure, but does not start a new failure, give a class, take a snapshot, switch to EDGE or restart the router.
- **R18:** Only a cycle that is not online and is in LTE-only mode gets a class, checked in this order: `sim` when `AT+CPIN?` answers about the SIM with something other than `READY` or `SIM busy` (no answer, a bare `ERROR` and a short `SIM busy` after a modem reset are not `sim`); `account` when no new `NETDEV WATCHDOG` entry appeared in the log during this boot and the operator DNS answers; `hang` when a watchdog entry appeared or fewer than 2 packets went out in the cycle, the pings of this cycle included; otherwise `unknown`, treated as `hang`. `sim` and `account`, once given, stay until the end of the failure, because watchcat bringing `wwan` up and a briefly silent port give false `hang` in single cycles. Cycles on EDGE do not change the class (D31, D38, D40).
  - **R18a:** The class never holds a restart back before the limit (D10).
  - **R18b:** The last class from a cycle in LTE-only mode, also from a cycle of the LTE probe after the limit (R23), is saved in the failure state as the limit class, and it decides about EDGE after the limit and after a restart (D31, D41).
  - **R18c:** The operator DNS selected as Terms defines it answers when `timeout 5 nslookup <fixed name> <operator DNS>`, sent over the session, ends with exit code 0; no operator DNS means no answer, and an answer of the router's own resolver, the tunnel or Tailscale does not count; the query goes out only when the route to that server leaves through the cellular device of its family (`ip-tiny` prints `dev wwan0` for `ip -4 route get <server>` and exits with 2 for `ip -6 route get <server>` without an IPv6 route, measured: those commands on the owner's router, 2026-11-23). The number of sent packets is measured on `wwan0`, the modem's own device, before this query (D31, D173).
    - *cmd* `tests/ladder/classes.sh` and `tests/ladder/account.sh` expect the classes of the failure classification rule (R18) for an IPv4-only session, a dual-stack session and an IPv6-only session, with the operator DNS of `wwan_4` and of the IPv6 settings (expected)
  - **R18d:** The class `sim` acts in the limit and EDGE rules like `account` (R19, R22, R22a, R25), and its syslog entry gives the answer of `AT+CPIN?` and says that the PIN is entered in LuCI (D38).
  - **R18e:** A `NETDEV WATCHDOG` entry (R18, R21d) is a line of the kernel, in the persistent log and in `logread` alike: after the 24-character date the facility `kern`, then `kernel:` and the words. The same words in a line of a service (a failed login with a chosen user name, for example) do not count, so a LAN user cannot turn the class `account` into `hang`.
    - *cmd* `tests/ladder/classes.sh` with lines of `daemon`, `authpriv` and `user` that hold the words, and with the whole kernel prefix inside the message of a service, expects the class `account` (expected)
- **R19:** After 6 restarts in one failure (2 when the last class is `account` or `sim`) the router goes into passive mode: it does not restart until it is online again in LTE-only mode, and syslog has an entry with `LIMIT:` (D8).
  - *cmd* `tests/ladder/account.sh` (pings fail, operator DNS answers, traffic goes out) expects restarts only at cycles 6 and 18, no `EDGE` entry and class `account` in the state; `tests/ladder/no-dns.sh` (DNS silent too) expects the full ladder and EDGE (expected)
- **R20:** A diagnostic snapshot (traffic counters, address, modem readings over AT, latest watchdogs) goes to `/root/wwan-recover-snapshot.txt` at the start of a failure as a new file (the previous one becomes `.old`), and is appended to before every restart and at the limit; every entry has a header with the reason, the cycle and the uptime, and the last 12 lines of the signal log.
  - **R20a:** The modem readings in the snapshot take at most 30 s together: with a busy AT lock or after the first command that ran out of time, the snapshot skips the remaining readings and records that (D33).
    - *scenario* given the AT lock held by `flock /tmp/lw-at.lock sleep 600 &`, or an `sms_tool` that does not answer, when a cycle reaches a restart threshold, then the restart happens within 30 s and the snapshot has an entry about the skipped readings
- **R21:** The failure counter survives a router restart and counts cycles, not time (D9).
  - **R21a:** A second run while the first is still running ends at once; the next cycle takes over the lock of a process that is gone or has run for more than 20 min, first ending that process with its children, and every run ends after 270 s at the latest (D22).
    - *scenario* given a `wwan-recover` lock left by a process killed with `kill -9`, when the next cycle starts, then the cycle takes over the lock and works normally
  - **R21c:** An interrupted write of the failure state leaves no damaged file: the state is either the old or the new one; the state is written only when something other than the uptime changed in it.
  - **R21d:** The failure state is in `/etc/wwan-recover.state` as `key=value` lines with at least the cycle count, the restart count, the limit class, passive mode and the uptime at the write; the reference for new `NETDEV WATCHDOG` entries counts from the current boot, because after a restart the RAM log starts from zero (D39).
  - **R21e:** Every `wwan-recover` entry in syslog ends with the fields `cycle= reboots= class= mode= uptime=`, where `mode` is `lte` in LTE-only mode (also in the LTE probe after the limit) or `edge`, because during a failure the router's clock may have gone back (D9, D39).
  - **R21b:** With `DRYRUN=1` the script changes nothing (no restart, no state write, no EDGE), and every one of its syslog entries has the prefix `[DRY-RUN]`.

### EDGE fallback

The fallback puts the modem into automatic mode; on networks that run only 2G next to LTE, this means EDGE.

- **R22:** When the failure state has at least one restart from the ladder and is not passive, 10 min have passed since the start, the router is not online and the last class is not `account` or `sim`, the router switches the modem to automatic mode (`network.wwan.modes=all` and `AT+ZSNT=0,0,0`) and brings `wwan` up again, at most once per start. In the cycle in which the failure reaches the limit, the router first settles the limit (R19), then the switch to EDGE by the passive mode rule (R22a) (D30).
  - *scenario* given dead LTE (ping and operator DNS silent) and ping and operator DNS working on EDGE, when 250 cycles pass, then there are 6 restarts, the saved limit class is `hang`, and the router stays on EDGE
  - **R22a:** In passive mode, from the limit cycle on and after every start, the router switches to EDGE when 10 min have passed since the start and it is not online, at most once per start, not counting the return after a failed LTE probe (R23), unless the saved limit class is `account` or `sim` (D32).
    - *scenario* given a failure state after the limit with class `hang` and a fresh start of the router, when 10 min pass without being online, then the router switches to EDGE exactly once
- **R23:** Before the limit, EDGE lasts until the next restart from the ladder. After the limit the router, every 72 cycles on EDGE, online or not (6 h), tries LTE-only mode: it removes the EDGE marker, sets `network.wwan.modes=lte` and `AT+ZSNT=6,0,0` and brings `wwan` up, so the probe cycles are cycles in LTE-only mode. An online probe cycle ends the failure when a read of `AT+ZSNT?` confirms LTE-only mode; 2 probe cycles that are not online or lack this confirmation go back to EDGE, unless the class from the probe is `account` or `sim`. IPv6 traffic leaves a due EDGE restart and the LTE probe on schedule (R16, R77d) (D30, D41, D214).
  - **R23a:** After the limit the router on EDGE does not restart, even when EDGE goes away (D30).
    - *scenario* given the router on EDGE after the limit, when there is no online cycle for 100 cycles, then there is no restart
- **R24:** A router restart always ends the EDGE fallback, and the router comes up in LTE-only mode by the modem state rules (R10, R11); it goes back to EDGE only by the EDGE rules (R22, R22a) (D12).
- **R25:** When the saved limit class is `account`, the router never switches to EDGE, even after a restart; when the package runs out while the router is already on EDGE, the router leaves EDGE at the next restart from the ladder (D24).

### SMS store

The `ME` store of the modem holds 50 messages, and a full store blocks delivery until the modem registers with the network again (measured on the modem firmware named in Risks).

- **R26:** Every day at 04:10, when the `ME` store holds more than 30 messages, the router saves the whole store as JSON from `sms_tool` to `/root/sms-archive/<YYYYMMDD-hhmmss>.json` and deletes the oldest messages until 20 are left; with 30 or fewer it neither archives nor deletes anything (D16).
  - **R26a:** The age of a message comes from its time stamp, and a message without a time stamp counts as the oldest.
  - **R26b:** The archive keeps the 20 latest dumps.
  - **R26c:** When the modem does not answer, the router deletes nothing and writes a syslog entry (tag `lw-sms-clean`).
  - **R26d:** With `DRYRUN=1` the script prints the number of messages and the indices it would delete with the thresholds `MAX` and `KEEP` from the environment, and neither deletes nor archives anything.
  - **R26e:** An interruption during deleting leaves the archive of the whole store from before deleting, and the next run works by the thresholds of the cleaning rule (R26).
  - **R26f:** The cleaning reads the list with the repair of the sender field of the message conversion rule (R34h), so a message with a quote or a backslash in its sender does not stop it, and the dump holds the repaired list; a list that stays unreadable deletes and archives nothing, with the syslog entry of the SMS archive rule (R26c) (D71).
- **R27:** By itself the image sends only the balance query of the operator preset (R47; with the preset `29702` the SMS `RACUN` to 1466), by the balance and bot command rules (R35, R37), and replies to SMS commands from the numbers in `SMS_ADMINS` (R46); it never sends other SMS or USSD codes (D47, D61).
  - *cmd* `rg -n 'sms_tool[^|;]* (send|ussd)|AT\+CMGS|AT\+CUSD' src/files/` finds only the one send function of the notification scripts, which carries the balance query and the SMS replies

### Diagnostics and LuCI

- **R28:** Every 5 min the router appends to `/tmp/lw-signal.log` one line with the fields `zsnt net pb s1 rsrp rsrq sinr rssi pci loss rtt wg_age` in the order and format of the Example (D17). `zsnt` is the stored mode (`AT+ZSNT?`), not the one in use after an interface start (R10); on the EDGE fallback `qmi.sh` sets the mode `all`, after which `AT+ZSNT?` answers `NV item value incompatible with +ZSNT parameters` and the field reads `?` (D82). `loss` and `rtt` come from one ping of the session's IPv4 service when it has one, else of its IPv6; a session with both adds the fields `loss6` and `rtt6` for IPv6 at the end of the line, after `wg_age`, so the line of an IPv4-only session stays as it is (D185).
  - *scenario* given a completed signal sample on the router, when the log is read, then its last line uses the fields and order of the Example
  - **R28a:** The log keeps the last 5 000 lines, in RAM only, and is gone after a restart.
  - **R28b:** The line is written even without internet or an answer from the modem (a field without a reading is `?`), and a run ends within 240 s, before the next one.
- **R29:** Syslog also goes to `/root/openwrt.log` (1 MB, 2 MB at the debug level of the debug logging rule (R29c) outside a tailscale image (D147), then the file `.old`) and survives a restart.
  - **R29a:** Cron does not log the start of every job.
  - **R29b:** The syslog entries of the image's scripts, the snapshot, the state file and the signal log are in English and in ASCII (D42); a line that quotes what the modem or `uqmi` answered shows printable ASCII only, as one line of at most 300 bytes, the rest replaced by `?` and `|`.
    - *cmd* `tests/linkwatch/ascii.sh` pass in the linkwatch suite
  - **R29c:** The profile key `LOG_LEVEL` (`info` or `debug`, `info` in `generic.conf`, R3) is written at the first start as uci `lonewrt.log.level`. At `info` the image logs as it did before the key. At `debug` the persistent log rotates at 2048 KB (`system.log_size`, `log_buffer_size` unchanged), `lw-siglog` writes each 5-minute sample to syslog too (tag `lw-siglog`, priority `daemon.debug`, the same line as in `/tmp/lw-signal.log`), so the signal history survives a restart, and `lw-linkwatch` logs as the heartbeat rule (R49g) says. The helper `lw_debug TAG MESSAGE` of `/usr/lib/lw/common.sh` writes at `daemon.debug` only at this level, and a service reads the level once, at its start: on a running router `uci set lonewrt.log.level=debug` takes effect for `lw-linkwatch` after `/etc/init.d/lw-linkwatch restart` and for `lw-siglog` at its next run (D81). No other script changes what it logs. In a tailscale image the debug level keeps the persistent log at 1024 KB, like `info` (D147).
    - *cmd* `tests/firstboot/generic.sh` and `tests/firstboot/full.sh` expect `lonewrt.log.level` and `system.@system[0].log_size` to be `info` and 1024, and `debug` and 2048; `tests/build/10-profile.sh` expects a stop naming the key and `use info or debug` for `DEBUG`, `Debug`, an empty value and others (expected)
    - *cmd* `tests/modem/siglog-debug.sh` and `tests/smoke/loglevel.sh` expect the syslog line only at the debug level (expected)
- **R30:** LuCI runs over HTTPS, and plain HTTP on port 80 only redirects to it (`uhttpd.main.redirect_https=1`, set at the first start when the section exists) (D19).
  - **R30a:** LuCI shows the signal (modemdata, over AT on `/dev/ttyUSB1`), the bands (modemband), SMS and AT (sms-tool), data usage per device (nlbwmon, with its database on flash by the counter labels rule (R44a)), watchcat and the WiFi schedule (D14, D26).

### Remote access

The image does not change the server; these rules apply to the router only.

- **R31:** With `WG_ENABLED='1'` the image connects `wg0` to the server of the profile, with the profile's keepalive (`WG_KEEPALIVE`) (D2).
  - **R31a:** The router and the LAN are reachable from the tunnel, and nothing passes from the LAN into the tunnel (D2).
