- **R32:** The generic image has `wg0` and the zone `vpn` like any other image, but the interface is disabled and has no key, peer or endpoint; turning it on means entering them in LuCI (D25).
  - *scenario* given a router with the generic image, when the owner enters key, peer and endpoint in LuCI and enables `wg0`, then the tunnel comes up and still works after a restart
- **R32a:** The interactive root login shell keeps its history in `/root/.ash_history` on flash, using BusyBox's built-in length limit. The history survives a restart, a flash that keeps settings and the Tailscale update using `sysupgrade -f` (R59b, `docs/tailscale.md`). A flash with `sysupgrade -n` and a button reset start with an empty history. Non-interactive shells (scripts, `ssh router cmd`) keep no history (D219).
  - *cmd* `tests/smoke/shell-history.sh` expects the login shell's `HISTFILE` to be `/root/.ash_history`, the non-interactive shell's to be empty, and the keep list to name the file.

### Tailscale

This section holds only for a tailscale image; an image with `TS_ENABLED='0'` has no part of it (no package, zone, service, `/status` line or SMS command), and the other requirements pass unchanged; the one answer such an image gives about Tailscale is in the command list (R37). Tailscale's own rules used here (from: Tailscale docs "Key expiry", `tailscale.com/kb/1028`, and "Auth keys", `tailscale.com/kb/1085`, read 2026-11-22): a device's sign-in ends after 180 days unless key expiry is turned off for it; a device added with a tagged auth key has key expiry off; an auth key lives 1 to 90 days, and a device added with it stays added after the key expires.

The image relies on these fields of Tailscale's state as `tailscale status --json` gives it (guess; Unverified); which commands read the state and ask for a link is the builder's (hook form under Builder decides):

- `BackendState`: `NeedsLogin` is not added (after the router was added: the sign-in ended or the router was removed), `Running` is added, `Starting` is starting, and `Stopped` or no answer from the daemon is not running.
- `AuthURL`: the sign-in link.
- `Self.DNSName`: the router's name.
- `Self.KeyExpiry`: the date the sign-in ends, absent while key expiry is off for the router.
- `Self.PrimaryRoutes`: the approved route.
- `CurrentTailnet.Name`: the account.

#### Package, size and service

- **R63:** With `TS_ENABLED='1'` the image has the packages `tailscale` and `kmod-tun` of the OpenWrt 25.12.6 feeds, and the "Included" table of `packages.md` lists them with the note "only with `TS_ENABLED='1'`"; with `TS_ENABLED='0'` the image has neither (R2) (D133, D134).
  - *cmd* `tests/build/run.sh` checks the package list of a tailscale and a non-tailscale profile, the uci of network and firewall after the first start for each key combination, a stop for a value of `TS_ENABLED` or `TS_ADVERTISE_LAN` other than `0` and `1` (R3), the key formats (R66), the stop of the build size guard (R63f), the key file readable by root only (R64b), the LAN warning (R67b), the size limit (R63a) and the `tailscale=` line (R63b) (expected)
  - **R63a:** `build.sh` stops, with no new image, when the root file system of a tailscale image (the `root` file in the sysupgrade archive) is larger than 15 500 000 bytes (D138).
  - **R63b:** `/etc/lw-build` of a tailscale image has the line `tailscale=<version>` with the package's version, and the first-start entry of the reset state rule (R7b) has it too (D139).
  - **R63c:** Tailscale runs as a service from the first start on and keeps its state in `/etc/tailscale/` (D135).
  - **R63h:** The output of the running `tailscaled` does not go to syslog; the cleanup step the package's init runs at each start (`tailscaled --cleanup`, about 14 lines, measured: `tests/vm/80-tailscale.sh`, 2026-11-22, 1 run) does (D156).
  - **R63d:** The image's Tailscale service writes one syslog entry with the tag `lw-tailscale` per change of the Tailscale state (not added, added, sign-in ended, not running), in ASCII, without a link or a key (D135).
  - **R63e:** No failure of Tailscale (no internet, a crash, a refused key, a failed sign-in) restarts the router, changes the modem or `wwan`, or delays the link repair or the session watcher; the link repair (R14 to R21) and the session watcher (R49) do not look at Tailscale (D157).
    - *scenario* given a tailscale image whose Tailscale service is killed every minute, when the router runs for an hour with working LTE, then `wwan-recover` logs no offline cycle and the router does not restart
  - **R63f:** A profile with `TS_ENABLED='0'` and a non-empty `TS_AUTHKEY` or `TS_ADVERTISE_LAN='1'` stops the build with the key's name (D150).
  - **R63g:** When the `tailscale` package of a build has a version other than those the tests' stand-in CLI was made from (now 1.104.1), `build.sh` writes a warning that names the version and builds the image (D153).
    - *cmd* `tests/build/run.sh` expects the warning for a package version not on the list and no warning for 1.104.1 (expected)

#### Settings and firewall

- **R64:** At every start, also after a sign-in, Tailscale on the router manages no firewall rules of its own (netfilter mode `off`), takes no DNS settings and no routes from the account, and has the profile's `HOSTNAME` as its name; with `TS_ADVERTISE_LAN='1'` it offers the LAN prefix (R67). These settings hold before anyone signs in and without Telegram keys and `SMS_ADMINS`; a sign-in by hand goes through `lw-tailscale link` (R65e), which keeps them (D135, D136, D154).
  - *cmd* `tests/vm/run-all.sh tailscale` boots a tailscale image of the x86 package with kernel TUN and checks that Tailscale reaches the state that asks for a sign-in with the settings of this rule, that `tailscale0` is in the zone `tailscale` with output rejected, and that no nftables table of Tailscale exists; without internet in the guest it reports `SKIP` (expected)
  - **R64a:** The interface `tailscale0` is in the firewall zone `tailscale` with input accepted, output and forward rejected (answers to connections from the account pass) and MSS clamping on, so the account reaches the router and the router starts no connection into the account (D149). With `TS_ADVERTISE_LAN='1'` the zone forwards to `lan`, and that forwarding is masqueraded, so devices on the LAN see the router's LAN address (D155); there is no other address translation for the zone. Nothing passes from `lan` or `wan` into the zone, and `wg0` with its zone `vpn` stays as the tunnel rules say (R31, R32) (D137).
    - *scenario* given a tailscale image added to an account, when a laptop of the same account opens `https://<HOSTNAME>` and runs `ssh root@<HOSTNAME>`, then LuCI answers and SSH asks for the login as over the LAN; given a device on the LAN, or a shell on the router, when it pings the laptop's Tailscale address, then nothing answers; given `TS_ADVERTISE_LAN='1'` and the route approved, when the laptop opens a LAN device's page, then the device sees the router's LAN address as the source
  - **R64b:** `TS_AUTHKEY` is in the image in one file only, readable by root only.

#### Joining

- **R65:** Once after every flash that does not keep settings and every button reset, when the router is not added, `TS_AUTHKEY` is empty or refused (R66a) and Telegram is reachable, the chat gets the link message, at most 5 min after Telegram is reachable; a refused key gives the refusal message instead, which carries the link (D133, D140, D145).
  - *scenario* given a flash with fresh Tailscale state and Telegram keys, when the router restarts twice before anyone signs in, then the chat has one link message
- **R65a:** `/tailscale` and the button `🔗 New link` of the link message answer within 30 s, while the router is not added, with a link Tailscale gives at that moment; once the router is added, both answer as `/tailscale` does for an added router (R69b), never with a link (D140, D158).
- **R65b:** When the router becomes added, the chat gets the added message, once per sign-in (R72b): the name, the account, how to connect (the app signed in with the same account, `https://<name>`) and the date the sign-in ends, or no date when key expiry is off for the router (D143, D151).
  - **R65c:** The added message has the line about the old entry only when Tailscale gave the router another name than `HOSTNAME`, compared without regard to letter case, and the line about the route only with `TS_ADVERTISE_LAN='1'` and the route not yet approved.
- **R65d:** The link message says that the link is for the person who manages the router, because whoever signs in first adds the router to their account (D148).
- **R65f:** No sign-in link goes to syslog or the persistent log.
- **R65e:** `lw-tailscale link`, run over SSH, prints a link Tailscale gives at that moment, or the state and the name when the router is added, so an owner without the bot can add the router.

#### The auth key

- **R66:** `TS_AUTHKEY` is empty or `tskey-auth-` followed by ASCII letters, digits and `-`; a value starting with `tskey-api-` or `tskey-client-` stops the build with the key's name and the words that an API token or an OAuth client secret is not supported (D141, D145).
- **R66a:** With fresh Tailscale state the router signs in with `TS_AUTHKEY` without a click; the key counts as used once Tailscale answered the sign-in with it, accepted or refused, and a used key is not tried again until the next flash that does not keep settings or button reset; without an answer (no internet) the router tries again when the internet is back (D141, D145).
  - *scenario* given a fresh state, a key and no internet at the first start, when the internet comes back, then the router is added without a link message; given a key Tailscale refuses, then the chat gets the refusal message once, and the next restart tries the key no more
- **R66b:** The router records the key as used before it sends the refusal message, so a power cut between the two gives no second try and no second refusal message.

#### The router's LAN

- **R67:** With `TS_ADVERTISE_LAN='1'` the router offers the network of `LAN_IP` with its mask, written as a network address (`192.168.5.0/24` for `192.168.5.1`) (D146).
  - **R67a:** When Tailscale reports that route approved, the chat gets the route message once per sign-in (R72b, D159).
  - **R67c:** The route the router offers is the IPv4 LAN only, also while the LAN has IPv6 (R74) (D188).
    - *cmd* `tests/firstboot/tailscale-lan.sh` expects only the IPv4 LAN as the offered route with a LAN that has an IPv6 prefix (expected)
  - **R67b:** When the LAN is `192.168.0.0/24` or `192.168.1.0/24` and `TS_ADVERTISE_LAN='1'`, `build.sh` writes a warning that this is the network of many homes and builds anyway (D142).

#### Sign-in expiry

- **R68:** When Tailscale gives the date the router's sign-in ends and the clock is set from NTP (the router has no clock of its own), the chat gets the expiry warning 14 days before that date, once per date (R72b); when key expiry is off for the router, it does not come. When the router first sees a date fewer than 14 days away, the warning comes at once, still once for that date (D143, D151, D160).
- **R68a:** When an added router stops being added (the sign-in ended, or the router was removed in the admin page), the chat gets the expired message with a new link once per such change.
  - **R68b:** A removal in the admin page reaches the router only when Tailscale learns of it, which took over 5 minutes and ended only with a restart of Tailscale (measured: `tests/vm/80-tailscale.sh` live, 2026-11-23, 1 run, package 1.104.1). So when `/tailscale`, its button or the SMS `tailscale` asks for the state of a router that counts as added while Tailscale shows it offline and the router reaches the internet, the router restarts Tailscale at most once per 10 minutes and answers from the state after the restart: a removed router answers with a new link (D161).

#### Commands and SMS

- **R69:** `/status` of a tailscale image has one Tailscale line after the data of the month (R39c), with the name and without the address (D143).
- **R69b:** `/tailscale` of an added router answers with the name, the Tailscale address, the account, the date the sign-in ends (or `never`) and, with `TS_ADVERTISE_LAN='1'`, whether the route is approved.
- **R69d:** While Tailscale is starting or not running, `/tailscale` says which of the two.
- **R69c:** `/help` of a tailscale image has a `🔒 Tailscale` line in the keyboard's order, and its keyboard (R37e) has a tenth button `🔒 Tailscale` that acts like `/tailscale`, with the 10 buttons in 3 rows of 4, 3 and 3 as in the layout (D162).
- **R69a:** The SMS command `tailscale` (R46) in a tailscale image gets within 60 s a reply with a new link when the router is not added, or with the name, the Tailscale address, the account and the date the sign-in ends; when the reply would exceed 160 characters (R46a), the account is left out first; the `help` reply lists `tailscale` in a tailscale image (D148).

#### Configurator and documents

- **R70:** The configurator has a group `Tailscale` after the tunnel with `TS_ENABLED` (default `0`), and only with `1` the keys `TS_ADVERTISE_LAN` (default `0`) and `TS_AUTHKEY` (under "Optional: join without a click"); with `1` it shows the image size and the free flash of the size decision (D138) (D133).
  - *cmd* `tests/configurator/run.sh` checks the group, the warnings, the marks and the Tailscale LAN button (configurator rules, R70 to R70d) (expected)
  - **R70a:** With `TS_ENABLED='1'`, Telegram off (either Telegram key empty, R38a) and `SMS_ADMINS` empty, the configurator warns that the link then comes only over SSH (`lw-tailscale link`, R65e); the warning does not stop the text (R57).
  - **R70c:** A `TS_AUTHKEY` against the key format (R66) is marked at the key; the mark does not stop the text (R57).
  - **R70d:** With `TS_ENABLED='0'` and a non-empty `TS_AUTHKEY` or `TS_ADVERTISE_LAN='1'`, the configurator marks `TS_ENABLED` with the words that the build stops on such a profile (R63f); the mark does not stop the text (R57) (D150).
  - **R70b:** With `TS_ADVERTISE_LAN='1'` and a common home LAN (R67b), the configurator shows the same warning and a button that sets `LAN_IP` to `192.168.<n>.1` with a random `n` from 20 to 250 (D142).
- **R71:** `README.md` has a short section on remote access that names the two ways, WireGuard to the owner's server and Tailscale without one: the keys that turn each on, that both are optional and can be on at once, and for each how to limit what the router reaches (for Tailscale a link to the part of the tailnet access policy (R71b) set apart in bold), with a link to `docs/tailscale.md` (D163). `docs/tailscale.md` has, in this order: (D154).
  - before the build: a Tailscale account, and the app on the laptop with the same account;
  - the keys, the build and the flash;
  - signing in with the link (or SMS `tailscale`, or `lw-tailscale link`), never with a bare `tailscale up`, and turning off key expiry for the router (D154);
  - connecting by name, which needs MagicDNS on in the account (guess; Unverified);
  - the LAN route: approval, `--accept-routes` on Linux, the home-network collision;
  - a reset or a flash without kept settings gives a new link, so remove the router's entry first; after a flash that keeps settings a router not yet added sends no link by itself, so send `/tailscale`;
  - `TS_AUTHKEY`: used once, and how to make it (not reusable, not ephemeral, pre-approved when device approval is on, a tag to avoid expiry);
  - safety: the link is a key, a wrong account is fixed with a button reset;
  - limiting the router in the account, by the tailnet access policy (R71b);
  - the cost in flash and data.
  - **R71b:** The part of `docs/tailscale.md` on limiting the router says plainly that by default every device of an account reaches every other, so a stolen router with root reaches the owner's laptop and phone, and gives the steps to make the account reach the router and the router reach nothing: a tag for the router (`tag:lonewrt`, set in the admin page or by a tagged auth key), and a copy-paste access policy in which the owner's devices reach the router's SSH and LuCI (and with `TS_ADVERTISE_LAN='1'` the router's LAN), the owner's devices still reach each other, and no rule has the router as its source; it says that the router's firewall blocks its own connections into the account too (R64a), that a tag that comes with the auth key turns key expiry off while a tag added later in the admin page does not, and that a router added again after a reset needs its tag again. The policy is checked on a real account before the release (D149).
  - **R71a:** `docs/profile.md` lists the three keys, with `TS_AUTHKEY` among the secrets, and `docs/network.md` names Tailscale's servers.

#### Messages

- **R72:** A Tailscale message the bot sends without a command (the link, refusal, added, route and expired messages and the expiry warning) waits for the internet, survives a router restart and reaches the chat at most once, as an SMS does (R34a), and goes as HTML with the fallbacks of R37g and R34f. An answer to `/tailscale` or its button is a command answer: a network failure loses it, as for every command (R51).
  - *scenario* given the added message due and no internet, when the router restarts and the internet comes back, then the chat gets the added message once
  - **R72a:** A message that carries a link takes the link from Tailscale when it is sent, not when it became due; when Tailscale gives no link at that moment, the message goes without one and says to send `/tailscale` for a link (D152).
    - *scenario* given the link message due, no internet and a router restart, when the internet comes back, then the message carries a link Tailscale gave after the restart; given that Tailscale gives no link then, the message has no link and names `/tailscale`
  - **R72b:** Each one-time message comes once per occasion: the link message once per flash that does not keep settings or button reset (R65), the added message and the route message once per sign-in (R65b, R67a), the expiry warning once per date the sign-in ends (R68) and the expired message once per change from added to not added (R68a). So when the sign-in ended or the router was removed and someone signs it in again, the added message comes again (D151, D159).
    - *scenario* given an added router whose sign-in ends, when the expired message came and the owner signs in with its link, then the chat gets the added message again, and later the expiry warning for the new date
  - **R72c:** The record of which one-time messages went is kept on flash: a router restart and a power cut keep it, a flash that keeps settings keeps it exactly as it keeps `/etc/tailscale/`, and a button reset or a flash that does not keep settings removes it with `/etc/tailscale/` (unlike the bot's state in `/etc/lw-notify`, R37f).
    - *scenario* given an added router that got its added message, when it is flashed with `sysupgrade` keeping settings, then no added message comes again; after a button reset the link message comes once
  - **R72d:** The texts of the joining, expiry and command rules (R65 to R69d) follow the layout below, which binds: the words may differ, the layout and the information may not; `<name>` is the router's current Tailscale name and `<account>` the account it is in. The menu paths of the admin page in it (`⋯ > Disable key expiry`, `⋯ > Remove`, `⋯ > Edit machine name`, `⋯ > Edit route settings`) are those of the UX review, which did not check them (guess, from: ../lonewrt-notes/tailscale/review-ux.md; Unverified).
  - **R72e:** At the first start of the image the one-time messages come after the reset note and greeting (startup messages, R37f).

```
[link message]
🔗 Add this router to Tailscale
Sign in with the link to reach the router from your own devices. No account yet? Signing in makes a free one.
https://login.tailscale.com/a/<code>
Only for the person who manages this router: whoever signs in first gets access.
Not working? Send /tailscale for a new link.        [ 🔗 New link ]

[refusal message]
⚠️ The Tailscale key in the profile did not work (expired or already used).
Add the router with this link instead:
https://login.tailscale.com/a/<code>
Only for the person who manages this router: whoever signs in first gets access.

[added message]
✅ Added to Tailscale as <name>
Account: <account>
On a laptop or phone, install the Tailscale app, sign in with the same account and open https://<name>.
❗ One more step: on login.tailscale.com/admin/machines, in the ⋯ menu of <name>, choose Disable key expiry. Otherwise you must sign in again on <date>.
The name <HOSTNAME> was taken, probably by this router before a reset. Remove the old one there (⋯ > Remove), then rename this one (⋯ > Edit machine name).       (only on another name)
To reach devices on the router's network (<prefix>), approve it there: ⋯ > Edit route settings.       (only with TS_ADVERTISE_LAN, route not approved)

[route message]
✅ Tailscale: devices on <prefix> are reachable now.

[expiry warning]
⚠️ Tailscale sign-in of this router ends <date>
To keep access, on login.tailscale.com/admin/machines choose Disable key expiry in the ⋯ menu of <name>.

[expired message]
🔴 This router is no longer in Tailscale (the sign-in ended, or it was removed)
Sign in again to get access back:
https://login.tailscale.com/a/<code>
Only for the person who manages this router: whoever signs in first gets access.

[/tailscale, added]
🔒 Tailscale: on as <name>
Address: 100.x.y.z
Account: <account>
Sign-in ends: <date>        (or: never)
Router's network <prefix>: reachable        (or: waiting for approval; only with TS_ADVERTISE_LAN)

[/tailscale, not added: the link message]
[/tailscale, starting / not running / image without Tailscale]
⏳ Tailscale is starting, try again in a minute.
⚠️ Tailscale is not running on the router.
🔒 Tailscale is not in this image. Set TS_ENABLED to 1 in the profile and build again.

/status line: 🔒 Tailscale: on as <name> | not added yet (/tailscale) | sign-in ended (/tailscale) | not running
/help line, between Alerts and Reboot as on the keyboard: 🔒 Tailscale – remote access from your own devices

[SMS replies]
Router: Tailscale sign-in link, open soon: https://login.tailscale.com/a/<code>
Router: Tailscale on as lte-router 100.101.102.103, account jan@example.com, sign-in ends 21 May.
Router: Tailscale not running, try again in a few min.
Router commands: status, reboot, tailscale
```

  - *cmd* `tests/run-all.sh notify` checks every message of this layout with a stand-in `tailscale` CLI: fresh state, a restart before sign-in (one link message), a queued link message after a restart (R72a), refused key, a key without internet, added with and without another name, route pending and approved, the expiry warning with and without NTP, expired and removed, added again after a new sign-in (R72b), starting and not running, an image without Tailscale, the SMS replies at 160 characters, no link in syslog (R65f), and the `lw-tailscale` entries (R63d); it also kills the stand-in daemon during a ladder test and expects no change of the ladder (R63e) (expected)

### Notifications (Telegram)

They work only when the profile sets `TG_BOT_TOKEN` and `TG_CHAT_ID`; without them the image runs no part of this section except SMS control (R46d) (D43).
Some operators send no SMS when data runs low and renew a package only with enough credit on the account; the balance rules cover that.

- **R34:** Every new SMS in the modem store reaches the chat `TG_CHAT_ID` as a message with the sender in bold, the modem's time and the full text in a quote, within 30 s of appearing in the store when the router has internet; the exception is balance replies, which follow the balance rule (R36) (D43, D46).
  - **R34a:** An SMS that could not be sent waits and goes out within 30 s of the internet coming back, even after a router restart; every SMS reaches the chat at most once, even after a restart. With notifications configured, `ifup` on `wwan`, `wwan_4`, `wwan_6` or `wwan_6_4` gives the queue an immediate send attempt.
  - **R34b:** Checking the store every 15 s takes the AT lock (R12a) for at most one short command; with a busy lock the pass is skipped instead of waiting.
  - **R34c:** A normal pass writes nothing to syslog or flash; flash is written only for a new SMS or a sent message, and syslog gets at most one entry per change of the sending state (works or does not work), with the tag `lw-notify`.
  - **R34d:** The bot token does not appear in syslog, the signal log, the snapshot or the bot's replies.
  - **R34e:** Latin-1 characters (such as `é`, `ü`) that `sms_tool` returns as single bytes instead of UTF-8 reach the chat as the same characters; Telegram rejects such a raw byte with `HTTP error 400`.
  - **R34f:** A message Telegram rejects (HTTP 400) does not block the queue: it goes once more as plain text (R37g), then once more with the text reduced to ASCII, and when that is rejected too, it leaves the queue with one `lw-notify` syslog entry; the following messages go on. A rejection is not reported as a lost link (R34c) (D85).
  - *scenario* given an SMS with the byte `E9` in its text and a second SMS after it, when the router has internet, then the chat has both, the first with `é`, in the order they arrived
  - **R34g:** A long SMS arrives in parts, and `sms_tool` gives `reference`, `part` and `total` for each. The parts of one message (same sender and `reference`, 2 to 20 parts) go to the chat as one message, joined without a separator in the order of `part`, once all are there. When a part is still missing 10 min after the first part, what is there goes out with the note `(part <n> of <total> missing)`. `/sms` shows and counts the joined messages. SMS commands (R46) in a message of several parts are not run (D67).
  - *scenario* given an SMS in 2 parts, with part 2 before part 1 in the store, when both are in the store, then the chat has one message: the text of part 1 followed by the text of part 2
  - **R34h:** `sms_tool` prints the sender of a message as the network gave it, without escaping `"` and `\`, and a text sender ID may hold both, so one such message makes the whole list invalid JSON. Before the list is read, these two characters are escaped in the sender field (only there, and only when the list cannot be read as it is; a valid list is read unchanged), and the message is forwarded like any other; `/sms` and the cleaning of the store (R26f) read the same repaired list. When `sms_tool` answers with exit code 0 but the list is still unreadable on 3 reads in a row, syslog gets one `lw-notify` entry and the chat one message, `⚠️ One SMS in the modem cannot be read.` with the words that new SMS are not posted and SMS commands do not work until it is deleted, and where to delete it (LuCI: Modem, SMS) (R39); once per occurrence, the next only after the list was readable in between. Until then SMS are not forwarded and SMS commands do not run (D71).
    - *scenario* given a store with a message whose sender is `AB"CD`, when the router reads the list, then the chat has that message with the sender `AB"CD`; given a store entry that is cut off, when 3 reads in a row fail, then syslog and the chat get one report, and after the entry is deleted the SMS that waited come once
  - **R34i:** What `lw-notify` and `lw-sms-clean` create (the queue and the other state on flash and in RAM, the request bodies, the SMS dumps) is readable by root only (files 0600, directories 0700); only root processes (the service, cron, hotplug) use them.
  - *scenario* given a router with internet, when an SMS arrives on the SIM, then within 30 s the chat has a message with its sender and text, and after a router restart this message does not come a second time
  - *scenario* given a router without internet, when 2 SMS arrive, then the router restarts and the internet comes back, then both reach the chat, each once
- **R35:** Once a week, on Monday at 10:00, the router sends the preset's balance query (`RACUN` to 1466 with `29702`) (D44, D47).
- **R36:** A balance reply (from the preset's `BALANCE_REPLY_FROM`, first line fitting `BALANCE_REPLY_PATTERN`; with `29702`: from 1466, `Imate: <amount> do <date>`) counts as one while the router waits for an answer: it sent the balance query (R35, `/balance`) less than 10 min ago. Every matching reply in that window counts and runs the rule below; an answer does not end the window. Any other message that fits is an ordinary SMS (R34): it reaches the chat as it is, without the translation of the diagnostic layout rule (R39b) and the warning, and changes neither the last known balance nor the memory below (D72). The one exception is the first scan after a flash or a reset (no list of handled SMS yet, as in the SMS reuse rule (R46e)), which only records the store and forwards nothing: there a message fitting the pattern sets the last known balance without a query, so `/status` has a balance right after a flash (D72); after a restart a message that came meanwhile is judged like any new one. A balance reply reaches the chat only when it answers a `/balance` command from the last 10 min, or when the amount is lower than `LOW_BALANCE` and the previous amount read was not lower; an amount equal to or above the threshold only clears that memory, with no message. The balance message has the amount in bold with the date until which the credit is valid, the line `⚠️ Top up at least <threshold> <currency>, or the data package will not renew.` when the amount is lower than the threshold, and the operator's own text in a quote that starts with its sender and time (layout below). The answer to `/balance` takes the place of the acknowledgement: that message is edited into the balance message when the reply comes inside the window and before the notice that no answer has come (R37); a late reply, the weekly reply and the first amount below the threshold come as new messages (D86). An empty `LOW_BALANCE` turns off the weekly query (D44).
  - *scenario* given the threshold 7.50, when the weekly replies are 8.00, 0.32, 0.32, 9.00, 0.50 in turn, then only 0.32 (the first) and 0.50 reach the chat, both with the threshold note
  - *scenario* given the threshold 7.50 and no query of the router in the last 10 min, when an SMS from 1466 says `Imate: 99.00 do 01.02.;`, then the chat has it as an ordinary SMS and `/status` still shows the balance read before
  - *scenario* given the threshold 7.50, the weekly query and a forged `Imate: 99.00 do 01.02.;` at 10:00:05, when the real `Imate: 0.31 do 05.01.;` comes at 10:00:20, then the chat has the low-balance warning and `/status` shows 0.31; given the real 0.31 first and the forged 99.00 after it inside the 10 min, then the warning has been sent and `/status` shows 99.00 (accepted, D72)
  - *scenario* given `/balance` and two replies inside the 10 min, 12.40 and then 12.50, when both arrive, then the chat has the balance message for the first and nothing for the second (the answer ends the request of `/balance`), and `/status` shows 12.50
- **R37:** The router answers commands from the chat `TG_CHAT_ID` within 30 s, in English; it ignores messages from other chats without replying, and does not run commands sent before it started (D45, D48, D53, D86).
  - **R37b:** `TG_CHAT_ID` may be a group: every member can use the commands, a command also works with the bot's name appended (`/status@<bot>`), and the bot ignores messages without a command; when Telegram reports that the group moved to a new ID, syslog gets one `lw-notify` entry with the new ID (D48).
  - `/balance`: sends the preset's balance query and passes on the reply by the balance rule (R36); a second `/balance` while a query waits less than 2 min gets a "still waiting" message; when the reply does not come within 2 min, the chat is told that there is no answer yet (a message of its own; a reply after it is a new message too, R36).
  - `/status`: the router state by the connection verdict rule (R39c).
  - `/sms [n]`: the n newest SMS in the store (5 by default, at most 10).
  - `/help` and `/start`: what each button does (the layout below); every unknown command gets the same list.
  - `/alerts`: shows which automatic messages are on; `/alerts 2g off` and `/alerts 2g on` turn the 2G message (R40c) off and on, and the answer confirms the new state (R37c).
  - `/speedtest`: speed test by the speed-test rule (R41).
  - `/wifi`, `/devices`, `/reboot`: by the device list rule (R43), R44 and R45.
  - `/tailscale`: a sign-in link or the state, by the Tailscale command rules (R65a, R69b, R69d); an image without Tailscale answers that Tailscale is not in the image and how to add it, with the text of the Tailscale message layout (R72d).
  - **R37a:** The bot has no command that changes the state of the router or the modem, except sending the balance query, the 2G message switch (R37c), the names of devices (R50, which only the bot reads), a restart confirmed with a button (R45) and starting a Tailscale sign-in (`/tailscale` and its button, R65), which changes nothing of the router's network; `/speedtest` uses data but changes nothing (D45, D53, D54, D60, D133).
    - *scenario* given a tailscale image that is not added, when `/tailscale` and the `🔗 New link` button come, then the chat gets a link, and `uci show network` and `uci show firewall` give what they gave before
  - **R37c:** The 2G message switch survives a router restart, and a button reset or a new flash set it back to `on`; flash is written only on a change (D53).
- **R38:** The notifications run as one procd service that comes up at the start and after a crash of its process; a run against a network that does not answer ends within its time limit and leaves no process (D46).
  - **R38a:** A profile without `TG_BOT_TOKEN` or without `TG_CHAT_ID` gives an image that works as if this section did not exist: no Telegram part runs, cron does not ask for the balance, and the remaining requirements pass unchanged; without `SMS_ADMINS` as well, the service does not start and nothing reads the store every 15 s. When only one of the two Telegram keys is given, the first start writes one `lw-notify` syslog entry that notifications are off (D43).
  - **R38b:** No failure of the notifications (wrong token, Telegram unreachable, script error, killed process) restarts the router, changes the modem mode or `wwan`, or delays other scripts by more than one short AT command (R34b).
  - *scenario* given an image from a profile without Telegram keys and without `SMS_ADMINS`, when the router runs for a day, then there is no `lw-notify` process, the crontab has no `lw-notify balance`, and first start, modem, link repair and log work as in an image without this section
- **R47:** The router picks the **operator preset** by the first 5 digits of the SIM's IMSI (MCC-MNC) whenever the notification service starts; until the modem answers, it reads the IMSI again every minute. A preset is a `KEY='value'` file in the image, read as data and never run. It gives the operator's name, the number and text of the balance query, the pattern of the reply with amount and date, the short codes to label and the currency. The balance requirements (R35, R36, `/balance` in the Telegram command rule (R37), R39a, R39b, the balance lines in `/status` and in the `status` SMS reply) work by the preset. Their texts quote the shipped preset `29702` (Telekom Montenegro): `RACUN` to 1466, reply `Imate: <amount> do <date>`, short codes 1466 and 1477, EUR (D66).
  - **R47a:** Without a preset for the SIM, the router sends no SMS except the replies of the SMS commands rule (R46). `/status` then has the line `💶 Balance: not available for this operator (<MCC-MNC>)`, and `/balance` answers the same. Senders get only the `+` of the diagnostic snapshot rule (R39a), no labels. The weekly query (R35) sends nothing.
    - *scenario* given the generic image with a SIM `26003`, when `/balance` comes, then the bot answers `💶 Balance: not available for this operator (26003)` and the modem sends no SMS
  - **R47b:** `LOW_BALANCE` is a threshold in the preset's currency, and balance messages show the preset's currency. `docs/operators.md` describes the preset format with the example `29702`, so a preset for another operator can be added without code changes.
  - **R47c:** The reply pattern is plain text, a space for any white space (also none), and the placeholders `{amount}` (once), `{day}` with `{month}` (both or neither) and `{any}` (any text); any other brace is an error. A pattern is also rejected when `{any}` is followed by `{amount}`, `{day}` or `{month}` with nothing between them but spaces, digits, dots and commas: `{any}` takes as much text as it can, so it would end inside the number and `12.50` would be read as `50` (D76). A rejected preset is handled as in the operator preset rule (R47). `docs/operators.md` lists the rule with examples.
    - *scenario* given a preset with the pattern `Saldo {any}.{amount}` or `Saldo {any},{amount}`, when the service loads it, then syslog has one line `operator preset <MCC-MNC> rejected (bad reply pattern), treated as no preset` and `/balance` answers as in the preset pattern rule (R47a); with `Saldo{any}:{amount} EUR` the reply `Saldo racuna:112.50 EUR` gives the balance 112.50
  - **R47d:** A profile carries one preset of its own in the keys `OP_MCCMNC`, `OP_NAME`, `OP_CURRENCY`, `OP_BALANCE_QUERY_NUMBER`, `OP_BALANCE_QUERY_TEXT`, `OP_BALANCE_REPLY_FROM`, `OP_BALANCE_REPLY_PATTERN` and `OP_SHORT_CODES`, so that a build which takes nothing but a profile (R52, R53) serves an operator the image has no preset for. With all of them empty the image has only its own presets. With one of them set, all but `OP_SHORT_CODES` must be set, and `build.sh` writes them as the preset `/usr/share/lw/operators/<OP_MCCMNC>`, also in place of a preset of the image with that name. Before it writes, the image's own reader of presets (`operators.sh`) judges the file, so the build refuses what the router would reject (R47, R47c) and nothing else; the stop names the profile key and never a value. The values are not secret (D132).
    - *scenario* given the run fixture, when it is built, then the image has the preset `99977` with the fixture's values next to the unchanged `29702`; with `OP_CURRENCY='usd'` or the pattern `Balance {any} {amount}` the build stops naming that key, and with `OP_NAME` empty it stops with `key OP_NAME is empty but another OP_ key is set`
    - *cmd* `tests/build/10-profile.sh` expects the preset file of the fixture line by line with mode 644, no `SHORT_CODES` line for an empty `OP_SHORT_CODES`, the profile's preset in place of `29702` when `OP_MCCMNC` is `29702`, a stop naming each required key left empty, a stop naming the key without its value for 21 bad values (file name, name, currency, number, text, sender, short codes and 7 patterns), and no file for a profile with the keys empty (expected)
- **R48:** `ROUTER_NAME` from the profile (1 to 20 characters: ASCII letters and digits, space and `-`, starting with a letter or digit) starts every SMS reply: `<ROUTER_NAME>: ...` and `<ROUTER_NAME> commands: ...`. `generic.conf` has `Router`, which the layout below uses (D66).
- **R39:** A person without technical knowledge understands the bot's messages: the first line says what the message is about or whether the internet works; times read `today 11:11`, `yesterday 18:40` or `5 Nov 09:12` in the router's time zone, without seconds; apart from the `Tech:` line in `/status` there are no log lines or `key=value` pairs; the text is in English, and emoji are allowed, because the ASCII rule (R29b) is about logs (D49).
  - **R39a:** A sender made of digits only, with at least 9 digits, gets a `+` in front; senders listed in the preset's short codes get the operator's name in front (`Telekom 1466` with `29702`); other senders are shown unchanged (D49).
  - **R39b:** A balance reply shown as an SMS (in `/sms`, or forwarded as an ordinary message) has below its quote a line that translates the amount and the date until which the credit is valid (`= Balance 0.31 EUR, valid until 5 Jan`; without a date in the preset's pattern, only `= Balance <amount> <currency>`); other SMS go without a translation. A balance message of the balance reply rule (R36) has the same two facts in its first line (D49).
  - **R39c:** `/status` has, in order: the verdict in bold, then (for the cases below) one line about what works or about the repair, the action line (R39d), an empty line, the signal in words with the network type, the number of devices on WiFi, the last known balance `💶 Balance: <amount> <currency> (checked <when>)` with `, <n> days ago` added when the reading is a day or more old by the synced clock (counted in calendar days; `, 1 day ago`), the speed test line (R41b) and the data of the month (R44) when there are any, the start line `⏱ Up <span>, since <when> (<cause>)` (without a synced clock `⏱ Up <span> (<cause>)`), the last outage (R40a), and last a quote with the `Tech:` line: bands, RSRP in dBm, SINR in dB, ping in ms and `VPN handshake <age> ago`; an unknown value is `unknown`, without a unit. The verdict is `🟢 Internet works`, `🟠 Internet works, but slow (2G backup)` with a line about what works, or `🔴 Internet does not work` with a line about the repair. The start cause is `restarted by self-repair` when the last restart was made by the link repair (R16), `restarted from Telegram (<name>)` or `restarted by SMS from <number>` after a restart asked for in the chat (R45) or by SMS (R46b), and `power cut or manual restart` otherwise. A missing reading gives `unknown` in its field instead of dropping the line (D49).
  - **R39d:** The action line is `❗ Needs a person: unplug the router for 10 s` when the link repair reached the limit (R19), otherwise `⚠️ Top up at least <threshold> <currency>, or the data package will not renew.` when the last balance is below the threshold, and `✅ Nothing to do` in all other cases (D49).
  - **R39e:** The signal is `good` with RSRP ≥ -95 dBm and SINR ≥ 10 dB, `poor` with RSRP < -105 dBm or SINR < 0 dB, and `fair` in between (guess; signal thresholds under Unverified) (D49).
  - **R39f:** Text that comes from outside (the text and the sender of an SMS, the first name of the person who taps `Restart`, the server name of a speed test) is shown as it is: a sequence in it that looks like one of the router's time markers is not turned into `today 11:11` (its `@@T` is shown as `@ @T`), and a sender or name that is only an option word for the shell, such as `-n`, is shown.
  - *scenario* given a last signal log line with RSRP -86 dBm and SINR 4.6 dB, a balance of 0.31 EUR read today at 11:05, the threshold 7.50 and no outage, when `/status` comes, then the answer starts with `🟢 Internet works` and the line `⚠️ Top up at least 7.50 EUR, or the data package will not renew.`, has `Signal: fair`, the balance with `checked today 11:05`, and ends with the `Tech:` line in a quote
- **R40:** When a link failure (R16) that lasted longer than one repair cycle ends, the chat gets one message `🟢 Internet back` with the start of the failure, its length and the number of router restarts it took (D50).
  - **R40a:** The last outage (start, length, how it ended) survives a router restart and shows in `/status`; flash is written at most at the start of the outage, at the 2G message (R40c) and at the end of the outage (R34c) (D51).
  - **R40b:** When the clock was not yet set from NTP at the start of the failure, the start and the length have a `~` in front; a router restart without a link failure (a power cut, for example) gives no `Internet back` message (D51).
  - **R40c:** When the router switches to the EDGE fallback (R22), the chat gets one message `🟠 Internet slow: switched to 2G backup` with what works and the start of the failure, within 30 s of Telegram being reachable; switching to EDGE again in the same failure (after a restart from the ladder, for example) gives no second message; when the switch (R37c) is `off`, there is no message (D52).
  - *scenario* given a failure from 14:20 fixed after 12 min with one router restart, when the internet comes back, then the chat gets once `🟢 Internet back after 12 min` with `Down since today 14:20` and `fixed by 1 restart`, and `/status` shows this outage in the line `Last outage`
  - *scenario* given the switch `on` and a failure in which the router switches to EDGE after a restart, then restarts a second time and switches to EDGE again, when LTE comes back, then the chat has one 2G message and one `Internet back`; given `/alerts 2g off` before the failure, then the chat has only `Internet back`

- **R41:** `/speedtest` answers at once with `⏱ Speed test started, result in about 60 s.` and measures download, upload, ping and jitter against the nearest speedtest.net server; the result or a failure message reaches the chat at most 120 s after the command, without a confirmation step (D54, D55).
  - **R41a:** The result has a verdict in bold (`🚀 <b>Speed test: fast</b>`, R37g) and an "Enough for" line by the download speed: from 50 Mbit/s `fast`, from 10 `OK`, from 2 `slow`, below that `very slow`; it also gives the server with its distance and the data used (the `wwan0` counter during the test, with `~`).
  - **R41b:** The line `Previous today` with the result of the previous test is there only when that test ran on the same day; `/status` has a line with the last test (download, upload, ping, time) only when it ran today. The results are kept in RAM and are gone after a restart (D56).
  - **R41c:** At most one test runs at a time; a command during a test gets `⏳ A speed test is already running`; on the EDGE fallback the test does not start and the chat is told that the router is on 2G; a failed test gives a message with the result of a plain ping (whether the internet works).
  - **R41d:** The test does not hold up SMS forwarding or other commands (R34, R37) and ends within its time limit with no processes left (R38); it never starts by itself, only from the command.
  - *scenario* given a test at 11:40 with the result 129/61, when `/speedtest` comes at 17:52 and gives 99/62 with a ping of 16 ms, then the chat has `Speed test started` at once, and within 120 s the result `🚀 Speed test: fast` with the line `Previous today: 129 ⬇️ / 61 ⬆️ (11:40)`; `/status` has `🚀 Speed test: 99 ⬇️ / 62 ⬆️ Mbit/s · ping 16 ms (today 17:52)`; after midnight both lines are gone

- **R42:** When the profile sets `HC_PING_URL`, the router calls this address (healthchecks.io) every 5 min when it has internet; a normal call writes nothing to syslog or flash, and a failed call does nothing except that the signal does not arrive. An empty `HC_PING_URL` turns the calls off. This does not depend on the Telegram keys (D57).
  - **R42a:** The alarm is sent by healthchecks.io, not by the router: period 5 min, grace 25 min, only the "down" event, no repeats and no "up", by a webhook through the bot (`sendMessage` to `TG_CHAT_ID`) with a short text such as `🔴 Router offline for 30 min`. The healthchecks.io setup lives outside the image (D57).
- **R43:** `/wifi` gives the names and passwords of the enabled WiFi networks from the router's current configuration, each name and password in a `<code>` tag so that a tap copies it (D83); the two bands with the same name and password are one entry and no band is named; a further network is a paragraph of its own, and when it shares the password it has `Password: same password`; a network without a password has `No password` (D58). With the means to make a picture, the answer is a photo with a QR code and the same names and passwords as its caption (R51, D90); without them it is this text (D92).
- **R51:** When the router can make the picture, `/wifi` and the `📶 Wi-Fi` button answer with a photo, the QR code of the first network as the device list rule (R43) lists it, and a caption in HTML that makes sense when it is forwarded outside the group: the heading, the names and passwords as in the device list rule (R43) (`Password: none` for a network without a password, `Password: same password` as in the device list rule (R43)), and the last line `Scan the code with the phone camera to join.`, with `... to join the first network.` when there are several networks; no command and no word about guests. The text of the code is `WIFI:T:WPA;S:<name>;P:<password>;;`, with `\`, `;`, `,`, `:` and `"` in the name and the password written with a backslash in front, `T:nopass` and no `P` field for a network without a password, `H:true` before the closing `;` for a hidden name. The picture is made for each request in RAM and deleted after the upload: `qrencode` draws the code as text with a margin of 4 modules, an awk filter makes a grayscale picture of it, 8 to 12 pixels per module (400 to 600 pixels wide for the usual lengths), and `cjpeg` packs it as a JPEG of quality 90 (D91). The upload is a `sendPhoto` as `multipart/form-data` (fields `chat_id`, `parse_mode=HTML`, `caption`, `photo`) built in a 0600 file in RAM, with the token in the URL only and the stderr of `uclient-fetch` never logged (R34d, R34i). A caption of more than 1000 bytes counted with its markup (busybox counts bytes; Telegram's limit is 1024 characters after the markup is read), a picture that cannot be made (a tool missing or failing), or the answer HTTP 400 to the photo gives the text of the device list rule (R43) instead and one `lw-notify` syslog entry in ASCII without a name, a password or the token (D92); a network failure loses the answer like that of every command (D90).
  - *scenario* given a router with the network `Home-Guest` and the password `blue-lamp-4521`, when the owner taps `📶 Wi-Fi`, then the chat gets a photo of a QR code that a phone camera reads as `WIFI:T:WPA;S:Home-Guest;P:blue-lamp-4521;;`, with the caption `Name: Home-Guest`, `Password: blue-lamp-4521` and the line about scanning; given `cjpeg` fails, then the chat gets the text of the device list rule (R43) and syslog has one entry
- **R44:** `/devices` starts with the sum of the month in its heading, `📱 Devices · <n> GB this month (since <start>)` with the start of the traffic accounting rule (R44c), and lists every device with traffic in the current calendar month in two bold groups with their counts: `Connected now (<n>)` and `Earlier this month (<n>)`, each sorted by usage, largest first, with the name the owner gave it (R50), else the name from DHCP, else `Unknown device` with the end of the MAC address; the band is not shown; at most 20 entries in total, the smallest drop off the list but not out of the sum; the router's own traffic (a speed test, for example) is the last entry, `Router itself`, after an empty line; the same sum is the `📊` line in `/status` below the balance; the reply has the button `✏️ Name a device` (R50) (D59, D63).
  - **R44b:** The router remembers the DHCP name of every MAC address with traffic in the current month, also after its lease expired, so a device switched off for the night keeps its name (D63). A name the owner gave (R50) comes before it.
  - **R44a:** The month's counters survive a router restart, losing at most a day on a power cut; flash is written at most once a day and at a stop; the month is taken from the NTP clock, not from the time before the sync (D59).
  - **R44d:** The kernel allows a netlink receive buffer of the size `nlbwmon` asks for (`net.core.rmem_max` at least 524288 bytes), so it starts without the warning about a capped buffer and a burst of connections does not leave a gap in the counters (D166).
  - **R44c:** The start shown with the month's sum (R44, the `📊` line of the connection verdict rule (R39c)) is `1 <month>`, unless the counters started later in the current month: a reset that wipes the settings (a flash without kept state, a factory reset) also wipes the counters, and then the start is the boot after it, as `<when>` (`today 12:53`, `15 Nov 12:53`). The router notes that boot once, at the first pass with a synced clock, in `/etc/lw-notify/data-since`, which the same resets wipe (D93).
  - *scenario* given a router flashed today at 12:53, when `/devices` comes at 13:43, then the heading says `this month (since today 12:53)`; in December the same router says `since 1 Dec`
- **R50:** The owner gives a device a name from the chat. The `/devices` reply has the button `✏️ Name a device`; a tap sends `✏️ Which device do you want to name?` with one button per device of the current month: first the devices that have neither a name of the owner nor a DHCP name, then the others in the order of `/devices`, at most 12 devices, and a last button `Cancel`; two buttons whose labels are 14 characters or less share a row. A tap on a device changes that message to `✏️ Naming <b>name</b>`, without buttons, and sends `✏️ Send the new name for <b>name</b> as a reply to this message.` with the line `Up to 24 characters. Send - to remove a name.`, a reply request (Telegram `force_reply`, with a placeholder) so that the app opens the reply field. A reply to that message from the chat `TG_CHAT_ID` is the name: control characters and line breaks are dropped, runs of spaces become one, spaces at either end go, and the first 24 characters are kept (characters of UTF-8, not bytes; a cut never splits one); `-` removes the name. The answer is `✅ <b>old name</b> is now called <b>new name</b>.` or `✅ Name removed: <b>name</b>.`; a text that is empty after the cleaning gets a short error and the prompt stays open, `-` for a device that has no name of its own says so, and a flash that cannot be written says so and stores nothing. The list and the prompt stand for 10 min and are kept in RAM, a prompt for one name only. A tap on `Cancel` closes the list (its message becomes `✏️ Naming cancelled.` without buttons); a button of a list that is gone, replaced by a newer one or older than 10 min gets the pop-up `This button expired, tap ✏️ Name a device again.` and nothing else happens; a reply to a prompt that ran out, was used or that the router forgot (a restart) gets `This request expired, tap ✏️ Name a device again.`. A reply that is a command (starts with `/`) or the label of a keyboard button is not a name. Everything shown from a name is escaped (R37g). In `/devices`, in the list and in these messages the device is called by its name from the owner, else its DHCP name (R44b), else `Unknown device (…xx:yy)` (D87, D89).
  - *scenario* given the device `Unknown device (…3c:5d)`, when the owner taps `✏️ Name a device`, taps `Unknown device (…3c:5d)` in the list and answers the prompt with `Kate's iPhone`, then the chat gets `✅ Unknown device (…3c:5d) is now called Kate's iPhone.` and `/devices` lists `Kate's iPhone` from then on, also after a restart of the router
  - **R50a:** The names of the device naming rule (R50) are one file on flash, `/etc/lw-notify/devnames`: a line `<MAC>`, a tab and the name for each device, the newest last, at most 64 lines (beyond that the oldest go); a name that is set again moves to the end. The file is written atomically and only when a name changes, so a list, a prompt, a tap and a reply that changes nothing write nothing (R34c). The names survive a restart and a power cut and stay when the device leaves the month's list; a new flash and a button reset empty `/etc/lw-notify` and so remove them (R37f) (D88, D89).
- **R45:** `/reboot` answers with a question with the buttons `🔄 Restart` and `Cancel`; the restart happens only after a tap on `Restart` within 60 s by a member of the chat `TG_CHAT_ID`; after the tap, a cancel or 60 s the message changes its text and loses its buttons; a tap on an expired button gives the Telegram popup `This button expired, send /reboot again` and does nothing (D60).
  - **R45a:** After the start from such a restart the chat gets one message with the name of the person who tapped and the length of the break; a normal restart (power, link repair) gives no such message.
- **R37d:** `/alerts` shows the 2G message switch also as a button that changes the state and the text of the same message; the command `/alerts 2g on|off` keeps working (D60).
- **R37e:** The chat has a persistent bot keyboard with 9 buttons as in the layout below (10 in a tailscale image, R69c); the bot sends it with the answer to `/help` and `/start` and once after the first start of the image, and a button acts like its command (`🟢 Status` like `/status` and so on); the bot skips other text without `/`; the keyboard works with the bot's privacy mode on, because the Telegram app sends a tap as a reply to the bot's message that carries the keyboard, and typed commands work in both modes (D64).
- **R37f:** After the first start of the image, when the state on flash was fresh at that start (no flag of the chat isolation rule (R37e) and nothing else in `/etc/lw-notify` at the first pass of the service), the chat first gets a message of its own with two lines, `🔄 Router was reset to its built-in settings.` (bold) and `Earlier logs and saved SMS are gone.`, and then the greeting of the chat isolation rule (R37e) with the keyboard as a second message, which has no reset lines (D75, D84). The note is not part of the greeting because a tap on the keyboard arrives as a reply to the message that carries the keyboard, and the app shows the start of that message above every tap; with the note in it, each tap would quote `Router was reset`. The note goes out once: after a Telegram outage or a restart of the router before the greeting went out only the greeting is still due when the note had already gone, and the note is still due when it had not; an image flashed with its state kept (`sysupgrade -o`: files in `/etc/lw-notify`, no flag) gets the greeting without the note, while a plain `sysupgrade` without `-n` keeps `/etc/config` but not `/etc/lw-notify` and gets the note; a flash that cannot be written leaves the note out.
  - *scenario* given a router after a button reset, when the service has started and Telegram answers, then the chat gets the message `🔄 Router was reset to its built-in settings.` / `Earlier logs and saved SMS are gone.` and after it the message `🤖 The router bot is ready. ...` with the keyboard, and neither after a later restart of the service or of the router
- **R37g:** Every message and every edit of the bot goes out with `parse_mode=HTML`; the tags in use are `b`, `i`, `code` and `blockquote`. Text that the code did not write itself (the text and sender of an SMS, DHCP names, the names the owner gives devices, the names and passwords of the WiFi networks, the first name of the person who taps `Restart`, the operator's name, the server of a speed test) has `&`, `<` and `>` written as entities, so it shows as it is and cannot change the layout or make Telegram refuse the message; a long reply is cut between messages, never inside a tag or an entity. When Telegram answers a message with HTTP 400, the same text goes once more as plain text (tags removed, entities decoded) and one `lw-notify` syslog entry says that the markup was refused (ASCII, no token and no text of the message); if that is rejected too, R34f goes on, and an edit that is rejected is not repeated as an edit (the balance message then goes as a new message, R36) (D83, D85).
  - *scenario* given an SMS from the sender `<b>Bank&Co` with the text `a <i>b</i> && c`, when it reaches the chat, then the sender and the text show as typed, in bold and in a quote; given that Telegram rejects the HTML of a message, then the chat gets it once as plain text and syslog has one entry
- *scenario* given a TV `LG-TV` that downloaded 18 GB in the evening and is off since 23:00, with its DHCP lease expired, when `/devices` comes in the morning, then `LG-TV · 18.2 GB` is in the group `Earlier this month`, and the sum includes it (R44, R44b)
- *scenario* given a router restarted at 03:00 and 40 GB used since 1 Nov, when `/devices` comes, then the sum is still about 40 GB `since 1 Nov`, and a device without a DHCP name shows as `Unknown device (…6e:94)`
- *scenario* given `/reboot` sent by one member of the group, when another member, Sam, taps `Restart` after 20 s, then the message changes to `🔄 Restarting now (Sam tapped Restart).`, the router restarts, and after the start the chat has once `🟢 Router back after restart (asked by Sam)` with the length of the break; given no tap, then after 60 s the message says `Restart not confirmed, nothing done.` and there is no restart

- **R46:** An SMS from a number in `SMS_ADMINS` (the sender's digits without `+`, a leading `00` or a single leading `0` match when they are at least 8 digits and a number in the list ends with them; for example `05550100123` matches `+15550100123`; a sender with any character other than digits and one leading `+`, a text sender ID such as `X600000001` or a number with a space, is no number and never matches) with the text `status`, `reboot` or `help`, and in a tailscale image `tailscale` (R69a) (any case, spaces around it allowed) gets an SMS reply to the matched number within 60 s of appearing in the store, even when Telegram is unreachable; any other text from such a number gets the `help` reply, except a text that has the shape of a router reply (it starts with `ROUTER_NAME`, or looks like `<name>: internet OK ...`, `<name>: Tailscale ...` or `<name> commands: status, reboot`), which gets no reply, so two routers never answer each other; an SMS from other numbers gets no reply. Each of these SMS also reaches the chat like any SMS (R34) (D61, D65, D68, D69).
  - **R46a:** A reply has at most 160 ASCII characters (one GSM-7 SMS) and the content of the layout below; `status` gives the internet state, since when the failure lasts, the repair step with its limit, the cause in words, the 2G state, the signal in words and the last balance with its date; parts that do not fit are left out whole, the cause first (D61, D68).
  - **R46b:** `reboot` first sends its reply, then restarts the router within 30 s; after the start the chat gets a message as in the SMS reply rule (R45a), with the number the command came from. Only a `reboot` restarts that appeared in the store after the service started (what was in the store at the start is old) and whose SMS centre time stamp is at most `SMS_REBOOT_MAX_AGE_MIN` minutes old by the router's clock; when the clock is from NTP, it is also at most as many minutes in the future (a larger difference means the clocks disagree) and a time stamp that cannot be read is not fresh. Without NTP the clock is the one the router had at the start, never ahead of the real time, so only the age is checked: a copy delivered late is refused, and a fresh one is accepted when the centre stamps in the router's time zone. The stamp is read as router-local time, because `sms_tool` drops the zone field, so an admin number whose SMS centre stamps in another time zone cannot restart the router by SMS (a stamp behind the router's zone reads as old and is refused, with NTP or without; one ahead of it is refused only with NTP); `status` and `help` have no age check and are not affected; at most `SMS_REBOOT_MAX_PER_DAY` restarts by SMS per day (the counter survives a restart); an old `reboot`, or one over the limit, gets a reply with the reason, within the reply limit of the SMS toggle rule (R46c), and restarts nothing (D61, D65).
  - **R46c:** The router sends at most 10 replies per day in total (the counter survives a restart; flash is written only for a reply); after the limit commands get no reply, while `reboot` still works; a failed send (an empty account, for example) gives one `lw-notify` syslog entry and is not repeated (D61).
  - **R46e:** At the first pass after a flash or a reset (no list of handled SMS yet) the commands found in the store are not run and get no reply, because after a reset old SMS may lie there, and replies would use up the limit of the SMS toggle rule (R46c) (D65).
  - **R46d:** SMS control also works in an image without Telegram keys when the profile sets `SMS_ADMINS`; an empty `SMS_ADMINS` turns it off (R38a then covers both: without Telegram keys and without `SMS_ADMINS` the service does not start) (D61).
  - *scenario* given a failure from 14:20, step 3 of 6, class `hang`, when `status` comes at 14:52 from `+15550100123`, then within 60 s that number gets a reply starting with `Router: internet DOWN since 14:20 (32 min). Self-repair step 3 of 6, cause: modem not responding. 2G backup: off.`, and once the internet is back this SMS is in the chat too
  - *scenario* given 10 replies sent in the last day, when `reboot` comes from a number in the list, then the router restarts without an SMS reply

Message layout (the words may differ, the layout and the information may not). The messages are sent as HTML (R37g): the layout shows what the chat displays; a `>` at the start of a line is a quote, and names and passwords of WiFi networks are copyable:

```
🟢 Internet works
⚠️ Top up at least 7.50 EUR, or the data package will not renew.

📶 Signal: fair (4G+)
📱 Wi-Fi: 3 devices connected
💶 Balance: 0.31 EUR (checked 9 Nov 10:00, 6 days ago)       (checked today 11:05 when the reading is from today)
🚀 Speed test: 99 ⬇️ / 62 ⬆️ Mbit/s · ping 16 ms (today 17:52)
📊 Data this month: 42 GB (since 1 Nov)
⏱ Up 3 d 15 h, since 11 Nov 16:49 (power cut or manual restart)       (without a synced clock: ⏱ Up 3 d 15 h (power cut ...))

> Tech: B3+B1 · RSRP -86 dBm · SINR 4.6 dB · ping 50 ms · VPN handshake 2 min ago

Messages and email OK, video and calls no.

(/alerts 2g off turns this message off)

/status shows the details.

The internet itself works (ping 30 ms), try again later.

✅ New SMS
✅ Low balance
✅ Internet back
✅ 2G backup

Only the 2G message can be turned off.
[ 2G backup: turn off ]

🔴 Router offline for 30 min        (healthchecks.io webhook, R42a)

📶 Wi-Fi          (R43: the text, which is the answer when no picture can be made)
Name: OpenWrt-LTE
Password: xxxxxxxx

Name: OpenWrt-LTE-5G
Password: same password

[photo: the QR code of OpenWrt-LTE]  (R51: the same names and passwords as the caption)
📶 Wi-Fi
Name: OpenWrt-LTE
Password: xxxxxxxx

Name: OpenWrt-LTE-5G
Password: same password

Scan the code with the phone camera to join the first network.

📱 Devices · 42 GB this month (since 1 Nov)

Connected now (3)
• iPhone · 12.4 GB
• Galaxy-S21 · 3.1 GB

Earlier this month (2)
```
