### Builder decides

- The Tailscale commands the image's service and `lw-tailscale` run (how they read the state, ask for a link, sign in with the key and set the settings), within the interface facts of the Tailscale section and its rules.
- Where on flash the record of one-time messages and of a used key lives, within the record rule (R72c) and the key rule (R66a).
- Where the auth key file lives, within the key file rule (R64b).
- Where the text of the `qmi.sh` edits lives in the repository and how the anchors are written, within the QMI derivation rule (R12d) and outside `src/files/`, so the uqmi check of the modem-access rule (R12c) still holds.
- The form of the hook that adds the family check to watchcat's round and of the option that selects `wwan_soft`, within the watchcat and first-start rules (R14 to R14f, R7d).

- The tag and the words of the family state lines, within the family loss rule (R77b) and the ASCII rule (R29b).
- The tools that give the `<vm>` WAN peer its DNS64 and NAT64 for point 12 of Acceptance, within the VM bench decision (D187).

## Risks

- The stock `464xlat` protocol script and helper are not pinned (from: `pinned-packages.sh`, code baseline in the header). A package update may change the helper arguments, PID file, anycast marker or routing rules used to identify and clean up this translation (R76d, R76i). Next check: compare those contracts and run the lifecycle check after an update.
- The planned-teardown PID check does not cover stock actions initiated directly by netifd or PID reuse between the check and the signal. If the stock PID file vanishes before the helper PID was recorded, cleanup cannot recover that PID (from: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/C2-review.md, Limits of verification, 2026-11-25; R76i).
- A replacement waits for a known old helper. A late stock nat46 device deletion can still race reuse of the same name, and interruption between stock address or rule changes and recording them can leave leftovers for the next cleanup (from: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/C2-clat-verify-report.md, Rework, 2026-11-25; R76i, R76j).
- A kernel-only IPv6 route loss without an interface event, reload trigger, watcher signal or pending replay can leave the old default advertised until the next qualifying event. An unreadable first snapshot with no pending record also needs another event to reconcile (from: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/L1-review.md, round 2, 2026-11-25; R74f).
- The first withdrawal PIO and client receipt of the final RA are best effort. A client that misses the final RA can retain the last advertised lifetimes. The first RA can still carry a positive router lifetime when another default exists (from: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/L1-review.md and ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/L1-lan-ipv6-loss-report.md, Rework, 2026-11-25; R74c).
- A stopped odhcpd with pending work can receive a bounded reload attempt roughly every 30 s from the watcher and once per non-dry-run recovery cycle. It stays stopped. A healthy dual-stack watcher pass also adds one netifd read for the identity check (from: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/L1-review.md, round 2, 2026-11-25; R74g, R49a).
- The silent-PLAT case in `tests/vm/96-clat-verify.sh` can miss an expected DNS64 query. The cause is unverified (from: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/handoff.md; DNS64 recheck, R76a; Acceptance 12).
- `tests/vm/run-all.sh` builds the shared image before the scenario and outside the scenario's optional lock. Concurrent runs in one VM directory can interfere with the build. Separate `LWVM_WORK` directories isolate the mutable build and guest state (from: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/L1-review.md, round 2, 2026-11-25).
- A concurrent writer can add a host route with exactly the recorded selector, which the interrupted DNS lookup's cleanup cannot distinguish from its own route (from: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/C2-review.md, Limits of verification, 2026-11-25; R76g, O7).

- A bad image on the MF289F: recovery is only over UART with the case opened (`docs/development.md`, "Recovery"), so every image passes `sysupgrade -T` before a flash.
- Frequent interface restarts can fill the persistent log and shorten the retained history (log limits, R29, R29c).
- Debug logging adds recurring signal, heartbeat and modem entries. A modem repeatedly returning unusable replies can rotate the log much more often than a healthy one (debug log, R29c).
- QMI clients on the modem: the watcher's calls now run on netifd's own IPv4 client (R49b), but a start of the interface takes and releases more clients than before (R73e), and a call killed by its timeout may leave one allocated; D5 records what killed calls did to the client IDs before. Nobody has counted the clients on the router after a week of reconnects (Unverified).
- A used-up package: the image cannot fix it, it only recognises it and stops restarting sooner; the warning comes from the heartbeat on the server side.
- Flashing over the tunnel: a mistake in the tunnel or band fields of a profile cuts the router off until someone is on site, so an image with changed tunnel fields is flashed only with someone at the router.
- The operator changes bands or priorities: the band mask is in the profile, and the signal log shows the change.
- The bot token in a private image: whoever gets the image file or the router can write as the bot and read commands; the bot has only reading commands (R37a), and the token is changed in @BotFather and with a new build. The token is also visible in the arguments of the `uclient-fetch` process during a request, that is to every process on the router, but not in the logs. The address of the healthchecks.io check (`HC_PING_URL`) is visible the same way, in the arguments of the `uclient-fetch` process every 5 min; whoever reads it can send false signals that the router is alive, which hides an outage, and can do nothing else with it.
- Every profile secret stays readable in the image's read-only part for whoever holds the router or the image: the tunnel key, the SSH host keys, the WiFi password, the root password hash, the APN password, the bot token, the heartbeat address and the Tailscale auth key (its file rule, R64b, limits who on the running router reads it, not who holds the router or the image). First boot deletes `zz-lw-config` only from the writable layer. The router stands among strangers, so the limit is set on the WireGuard server: the router's peer gets only its own tunnel address and may not open connections into the owner's network.
- Another modem firmware: the SMS store size, the `ZSNT` codes and the receive store falling back to `SR` were measured on `BD_TMOPLMF289F1V1.0.0B03`; on another firmware they must be measured again, and the requirements win over the modem's behaviour. The same holds for the dropped network scan (R12d, D82): the generic image drops it for every firmware of the MF289F, and on another one the scan after a mode change may matter.
- The build needs the package feeds of the ImageBuilder (network) for `uqmi`, before `make image` and in the VM bench (R12d); the `uqmi` package of the feed can change at an OpenWrt point release, and then the build stops if its `qmi.sh` lost the scan line or changed, instead of shipping a stale copy.
- A session that drops again after every `ifup` can cause successive watcher escalations. Each start permits a new incident, and the restore-rate brake does not count escalations (watcher escalation and restore-rate rules, R49d, R49e).

- Unanswered IPv6 through the router counts as traffic (R77e): only the router's own ICMPv6 is taken off, so ICMPv6 errors the network sends back to LAN devices, and their unanswered connection attempts, pass for traffic; a dead path behind a busy LAN can therefore hold watchcat's restart and the ladder, and make the IPv6 result `answer` (from: second review of slice F2, ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/F2-rereview.md, R1). This affects sessions using IPv6. Next step: a counter of nftables on IPv6 replies (`ct direction reply`) of the session's device, without ICMPv6, as a stricter measure.
- Flow offloading would hide the LAN's forwarded IPv6 from the counters of the IPv6 traffic measure (R77e), so the measure would see only the router's own traffic, give quiet or unknown, and leave the decision to the probes as without the measure; the owner's router has no flowtable and `flow_offloading` unset (measured: its nft ruleset and firewall settings, 2026-11-24). Next check: `flow_offloading` stays off in the documents and in LuCI.

- Other state of the image is still in the world-writable `/tmp`, of the same kind R14h moved (unverified under path interference): `lw-apn.fails` (with its lock), `lw-nlbwmon-restarted`, `lw-at.lock`, `lw-modem-mode.lock`, `lw-notify/`, `lw-tailscale/` and `lw-signal.log` (from: the main session's read of the owner's 0.6.0 router, via ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/F1-review.md). Where root writes there with `>` or `mkdir -p` and then trusts what it reads, a local user could plant a file or a link; `lw-apn.fails` and `lw-nlbwmon-restarted` hold decisions and matter most. Next step: move them under `/var/run` with the check of the RAM state rule (R14h).
- Repeated netifd starts after registration or call failure can rotate the persistent log rapidly and repeatedly invoke Auto-APN (QMI setup and Auto-APN rules, R12d, R13b; modem start evidence, D82).
- The artifact of a run holds a private image, which holds the profile's secrets: whoever can read the user's repository can download it while GitHub keeps it, 90 days unless the workflow sets less (from: GitHub docs, "Downloading workflow artifacts" and "Store and share data with workflow artifacts", read 2026-11-16), so its collaborators are people the person would give the router's passwords; the workflow keeps it 7 days (R53g).
- The rootfs tests need `bwrap`, and Ubuntu 24.04 blocks unprivileged user namespaces through AppArmor by default (from: Launchpad bug 2046477; actions/runner-images issue 10443), so a change of GitHub's runner image can stop the tests in the workflow from one run to the next, and then no person gets an image until the workflow is fixed (R53d).
- A test that makes LTE unavailable with a band mask needs a band the modem cannot camp on: on 2026-11-11 a mask of B7 alone did not do it (the modem registered on the weak B7 cell), unlike on 2026-11-06, while B32 alone did (D82).
- The Tailscale sign-in link is a key: whoever signs in with it first adds the router to their account, where every device of that account reaches LuCI and SSH (both still ask for the login). The link goes only to the chat of `TG_CHAT_ID`, to SMS admins and over SSH; a router in the wrong account goes back to a fresh state with a button reset.
- An image close to the root size limit leaves little room for extra packages and persistent data. Next check: `df /overlay` after flashing the full image (root size limit, R63a; small tools, D167).
- Tailscale's use of mobile data at rest is not measured, and on a prepaid SIM it counts. Next check: the `wwan` byte counters over 24 h with Tailscale running and 24 h with it stopped.
- A signed-in Tailscale in kernel mode may need more memory than the router has to spare: only a user-space run, never signed in, was measured (the project's notes outside this repository, `../lonewrt-notes/tailscale/plan.md`, 2026-11-22, 1 run). Next check: the resident size of `tailscaled` after a day on the owner's router.
- A tailscale image depends on Tailscale's servers for every sign-in and for finding its peers.
- Whoever has root on the router (a stolen router through failsafe or the serial port) can copy `/etc/tailscale/` and join the owner's account as the router; the account's default access rules let every device reach every other (from: Tailscale docs, "Access control", `tailscale.com/kb/1018`, read 2026-11-22), so that reaches the owner's other devices. The router's firewall cannot stop that, because root changes it; only the account's access policy of `docs/tailscale.md` (R71b) does, and the owner has to set it.

## Acceptance

The owner or a reviewer runs acceptance. Fixture: an MF289F with a working SIM, wired to a laptop; for the generic image, an MF289F with a SIM of another operator.
Needed: the work folder with the ImageBuilder and packages, `unsquashfs`, `openssl`, `ssh` and `scp` over the cable.
Each point is a separate reason to reject:

1. **Build:** a private profile and `generic.conf` both build; the image manifest has every package of the "Included" table (a row marked "only with `TS_ENABLED='1'`" only in a tailscale image, R2) and none of the "Left out" table; the rootfs of the private image has the image's files and the profile's values; the rootfs of the generic image meets the no-secrets rules (R4, R4a); a broken profile and a broken script stop the build (R3, R6); the image's `/lib/netifd/proto/qmi.sh` is the `uqmi` package's file with the edits of the QMI derivation rule (R12d), and a package file with another sha256 or an anchor not there as often as expected stops the build (R12d, R12e, R12f), so does a changed stock `/etc/init.d/odhcpd` (R12h), and so does any of the four pin variables set outside `<tests>` (R12g); the manifest has `464xlat` and `kmod-nat46` (R76e); git ignores the private profile and image (R5); a profile with `SSH_KEYS` gives the image those keys and the folder's, each once, and a malformed entry stops the build (R61); `generic-passwords.txt` has the passwords and hints, and a second build does not change them (R33, R33a); `<vm>` passes the first start with the first-start table and the `lw-firstboot` entry, a start without the AT port, a power cut during the first start, a reset through `firstboot`, cron, and the persistent log with rotation (R7, R7b, R8, R8a, R29); `<tests>` passes, including the modem and SMS scripts (R11 to R13d, R26 to R26e) and `<ladder>` with the results of the ladder, class and EDGE rules in every scenario: hang, used-up package with and without DNS, SIM with PIN, dead LTE with working EDGE, start in passive mode, EDGE without network after the limit, dead lock, busy AT lock, with state and entries in the format of the state rules (R16, R18, R18c, R18d, R19, R20a, R21a, R21d, R21e, R22, R22a, R23a, R25); `<tests>` also passes the watcher tests `tests/linkwatch/` (lost session with and without APN options, retries, "No effect", an unregistered modem, escalation after 20 s, the sessions of the native IPv4 rule (R49a), no action on an empty, garbage or hung uqmi, the brake, debug lines, the snapshot and the heartbeat) and the log level tests (R29c, R49 to R49g), and `<vm>` shows `lw-linkwatch` running idle and silent (R49); `sysupgrade -T` on the router passes (R1, R2).
2. **After flashing a private image, within 5 min:** `ip -4 addr` on `wwan0` has an address; `wg show wg0` has a handshake younger than 3 min; both WiFi networks are up; `AT+ZSNT?` gives `6,0,0`; `uci get network.wwan.timeout` gives `30` and `grep -c -e --network-scan /lib/netifd/proto/qmi.sh` gives `0`; `AT+ZNLOCKBAND?` gives the mask of the profile's bands; `AT+CPMS?` has `ME` three times; syslog has `lw-modem-mode` with `modem OK` (entries about setting the state before it are allowed at the first start); the crontab matches the first-start table; watchcat runs; one `lw-linkwatch run` process runs and `uci get lonewrt.log.level` gives the profile's level (R29c, R49); LuCI answers over HTTPS, `http://<router>/` redirects to it and the modemdata page shows a reading; `/tmp/lw-signal.log` has a new line; `/root/openwrt.log` grows and has an `lw-firstboot` entry with the content of `/etc/lw-build` (R7, R7b, R10, R11, R11a, R12, R13, R14, R28, R29, R29c, R30, R30a, R31, R49).
3. **Button reset with someone at the router:** for an image whose profile folder holds the host keys, the router comes back with the same SSH host key, point 2 passes again, and this time syslog has no entries about setting the mode or bands (R7, R11a); the first message in the chat starts with the two reset lines (R37f).
4. **Dry runs and the lock on the router:** `DRYRUN=1 wwan-recover` changes no state; `DRYRUN=1 MAX=0 KEEP=0 lw-sms-clean` prints every index and deletes nothing; an AT command with a busy lock ends within its limit without an orphan; a snapshot with a busy lock ends within 30 s (R12b, R20a, R21b, R26a, R26d).
5. **Generic image on a router with another operator's SIM:** LTE comes up on that SIM, and syslog shows whether with the empty APN or from the table; the WiFi has the name and password from `generic-passwords.txt`; `wg0` is disabled, and once enabled in LuCI it comes up; a change made in LuCI survives a restart (R4, R7a, R9, R13, R32).
6. **Notifications on a router with a private image:** `<tests>` passes the notification tests with a stand-in Telegram API (R34 to R38, including no internet, a restart, a low threshold, a foreign chat); an SMS from the owner's phone reaches the chat within 30 s; `/status`, `/sms 3` and `/balance` get answers; after a router restart that SMS does not come a second time; `logread` and `/root/openwrt.log` do not contain the token; the generic image does not run the `lw-notify` service (R34, R34a, R34d, R36, R37, R38). `<tests>` checks the messages against the layout, including `/status` without a signal reading, at the repair limit and on EDGE, `Internet back` after a failure with a restart and without NTP, and no such message after a power cut, the 2G message once per failure and its `/alerts` switch, an SMS with Latin-1 bytes and a message rejected by Telegram (R34e, R34f), `/speedtest` with a stand-in client: result, previous from today and from yesterday, a second test during one, EDGE, failure, a hung client and an SMS forwarded during a test (R41 to R41d); on the router `/speedtest` gives a result within 120 s; `<tests>` also checks the `HC_PING_URL` call and its absence, `/wifi`, `/devices` with the `nlbwmon` database after a restart and with a device not connected after its lease expired (R44b), the `/reboot` buttons (tap, cancel, 60 s, expired button, foreign chat) and the message after such a restart (R42 to R45a); on the router the healthchecks.io alarm arrives after 30 min with the antenna unplugged or the SIM removed, and `/reboot` with the button restarts the router; `<tests>` checks the SMS commands (number in the list and outside it, letter case, the limit of 10, `reboot` after the limit, a failed send, no Telegram keys), a number in national form, a `reboot` that is too old, one lying in the store at the start and one over the daily limit (SMS control and diagnostics, R46 to R46e); `<tests>` checks the preset `29702` and a SIM without a preset (no SMS, the `not available` line), labels and currency from the preset and `ROUTER_NAME` in SMS replies (R47 to R48); on the router `status` from the owner's phone gets a reply within 60 s; on the router `/status` has the layout above, and an SMS from the owner's phone shows its sender with `+` (R39 to R40b).

For points 7 to 9 the owner also needs a second GitHub account, a browser with its network log, a coding agent (Claude Code) and the network, and the project needs a release with the configurator and `SSH_KEYS`, the public template repository and the configurator on GitHub Pages, each pushed on the owner's order; the owner runs these points. Point 7 uses two profiles: the run fixture for the checks of the log and the artifact, and a profile of the owner's router with both passwords for the flash.

7. **Build without Linux, on the owner's second GitHub account:** a private user's repository with `LW_PROFILE` from the configurator gives a passing run with the image and the manifest in the artifact and nothing else, the files named by the run's name field and the tag's commit in the summary, and a run with the version `9.9.9` stops naming it (R52, R53, R53e, R53h, R53j); a profile with an empty `WIFI_KEY` stops the run before the build (R53i); the user's repository has no new commit (R53c); a run without the secret and one with `LAN_IP` removed stop with their message and no artifact (R53a); the log, summary and annotations hold no secret value (R53b); a run of a test tag whose one test prints `SKIP` stops (R53d); the summary gives the date the artifact goes, and after 7 days it is gone (R53g); a second repository, made public, stops an unmarked run before the build with no artifact, and a run marked as a test builds the run fixture, not its `LW_PROFILE`, with the warning (R54); a user's repository made from the template before a release that changes `ci/github-run.sh` runs the new steps (R53k); the summary links to the hints of the card in `README.md` (R55a); the image flashed in LuCI as `README.md` describes passes point 2 (R55).
8. **Configurator:** opened from disk with the browser's network log open, the page makes no request after the file itself (R58); the unchanged form gives the values of `generic.conf` and `build.sh --render-only` accepts its text, and a filled form gives the run fixture key by key (R56, R56a, R56b); the page from GitHub Pages behaves the same, shows the newest release's version, and a reload empties the form (R56c, R56e); a profile of `v0.1.0`, which lacks `SSH_KEYS`, loads with that key marked as new, a made-up line with a key `generic.conf` does not have is named as left out, and the changelog of the release that adds `SSH_KEYS` names it (R56d, R60); an empty `LAN_IP` and a `'` are marked at their key, and every value of `fake-full.conf` passes (R57); an empty `WIFI_KEY` gets the note about GitHub and the text is still made (R57a); the key test fails for a key added to `generic.conf` (R56); following the Telegram steps gives a working token and chat ID (R58a); picking a zone fills `TIMEZONE` and a loaded unlisted zone shows it (R56f); setting `WG_ENABLED` to `1`, an APN, both Telegram keys and `SMS_ADMINS` shows their dependent fields, and a root password hides the hash field (R56g); two keys on two lines give one `;`-separated `SSH_KEYS` (R56h).
9. **Agent skill, with someone at the router:** a fresh agent session with only the repository and the skill goes from a profile file saved by the configurator to the report of the agent build report rule (R59a), once on the person's Linux machine and once in the user's repository; it does not flash after "build it", and after "flash it" it shows the backup, the sha256 check and `sysupgrade -T` before `sysupgrade -n`; its session transcript holds no secret value of the profile, and asked for the WiFi password it names the file (R59 to R59c); over the tunnel it refuses an image with a changed `WG_*` or `WWAN_*` key until told that someone is at the router (R59e); on the GitHub way it makes the repository and sets `LW_PROFILE` with no value of the file in its transcript (R59g); the image flashed by the agent passes point 2; `README.md` and `AGENTS.md` link to the skill, and `AGENTS.md` names it as the exception to its private data rule (R59d, R59f).

For point 10 the owner also needs a Tailscale account with MagicDNS on, its app on a phone with Telegram and on a laptop that can leave the router's LAN for a phone hotspot, and a device on the router's LAN; the owner's profile has `TS_AUTHKEY` empty and the owner's number in `SMS_ADMINS`; the owner gives the word for each flash.

10. **Tailscale on the owner's router, with someone at the router:** an image of the owner's profile with `TS_ENABLED='1'` builds, its manifest has `tailscale` and `kmod-tun`, and `/etc/lw-build` has the `tailscale=` line (R63, R63b); after the first start `df /overlay` shows at least 3 MiB free (D138); within 5 min of Telegram being reachable after the flash the chat has one link message, and a `reboot` before signing in does not repeat it (R65, R72b); `/tailscale` gives a link (R65a), and `lw-tailscale link` over SSH prints one (R65e); signing in from the phone gives the added message with the name and the account (R65b); with the laptop off the router's LAN, on a phone hotspot, it opens LuCI at `https://<HOSTNAME>` and logs in with `ssh root@<HOSTNAME>`, and a LAN device does not reach the laptop's Tailscale address (R64a); `nft list tables` has no Tailscale table and `logread` no line of tailscaled (R63h, R64); the access policy of the tailnet access policy (R71b), set in the owner's account, still lets the laptop reach the router, and a shell on the router pings neither the laptop nor the phone (R64a, R71b); `/status`, `/tailscale` and the SMS `tailscale` answer by the layout (R69, R69b, R69a); removing the router in the admin page gives the expired message with a link, and signing in with it gives the added message again (R68a, R72a, R72b); after a button reset, without removing the old entry, the chat gets a new link once, and after signing in again the added message names the old entry (R65, R65c); point 2 still passes. Optional, with a second flash with `TS_ADVERTISE_LAN='1'` and the route approved: the laptop on the hotspot opens the page of a device on the LAN (R64a, R67, R67a).

Points 11 and 12 are run by the reviewer of the work or by the owner, never by its builder alone. They need, beyond point 1, the cached `uqmi` package in the work folder, from which the tests derive the real handler (without it they fail, they do not pass), and KVM for point 12. Each point names the hardware it covers; what none covers is in Unverified.

11. **IP families on stand-ins (hardware: none):** `<tests>` runs the derived `qmi.sh` under the image's busybox against the stateful `uqmi` stand-in, in auto and both forced modes, for the operator models of the order rule (both granted, only IPv4 as on the owner's SIM, only IPv6, `IPV4V6` refused with `IP` granted, only `IPV6` granted, all refused), and checks the session, the published calls, the child interfaces and the failed starts (R73 to R73e, R73j), the injected faults of the QMI call lifecycle rule (R73e) and the cleanup of the QMI cleanup rule (R73m), the profile writes, their rollback and the autoconnect flag with a readable, a disabled, an empty-APN and an unreadable profile, a change of the login alone and a write that does not stick (R73f, R73g, R73n, R73o), and the deadline with a modem that stops answering before and after a granted call (R73h); it checks the family checks with a stand-in `ping` that answers by family, device and address: IPv4-only, dual stack, IPv6-only with and without the translation, one family lost, an unnamed device, and an answer through the tunnel (R14, R14d, R18c, R28, R77 to R77e); the watcher with session fixtures and the family result file of watchcat's hook (R14g, R49a to R49i); the image's odhcpd init (R74g); the kept 0.6.1 configuration and its migrations (R7c, R7d, R7e, R74b, R75a); and the ladder, EDGE, APN, notification, Tailscale and watcher suites pass, including a due EDGE restart under IPv6 traffic (R23, R77d).
    The stand-in CLAT suite covers DNS64 validation and route ownership, deduplication and a session that ends during setup (`tests/clat/r76a-dns64-prefix.sh` and the rest of `tests/clat/`, R76a to R76c, R76g). `tests/clat/r76c-session-end.sh`, `r76c-down-contended.sh`, `r76c-ownership.sh` and `r76c-session-identity.sh` in that same directory exercise session end, unreadable status, owned resources, deferral under contention and a new session with repeated handles (R76f, R76i to R76k).
    `tests/clat/r76c-plat-and-renumbering.sh` and `tests/clat/r76c-renumber-safety.sh` check the DNS64 re-check gate, full /64 set, replacement before teardown, session and path validation after removal, stock PID-file protection, and retry of interrupted route and rule cleanup (R76a, R76c, R76g, R76i, R76j).
    L1's `tests/lan6/r74-session.sh` and `tests/lan6/r74f-odhcpd-wiring.sh` cover the withdrawal phases, staged LuCI isolation, unreadable status and route timeouts, failed first, final and return signals, an empty procd instance, interruption before or after the configuration rename, and replay after interruption between signals (R74b, R74c, R74e to R74g).
    `tests/linkwatch/r74f-ipv6-call.sh` and `tests/linkwatch/r49i-ipv6-lost.sh` cover IPv6 status, settings and flag races, unreadable replies, the negotiation mark, removal of the control node, one loss and return, and no IPv4 action. The same watcher tests and `tests/ladder/lan6-pending.sh` cover bounded replay and the ladder's dry-run exclusion (R49a, R49b, R49f, R49h, R49i, R74f, R74g).
    In `tests/lan6/r74-ipv4-cost.sh`, a steady IPv4-only `ifup wwan` runs the stock command multiset and sends no signal (the test counts the external commands captured by its wrappers; on the reviewed snapshot it printed 24 per path, including 6 UCI calls, and `ok`; R74b, R74g).
    On native IPv4 and dual stack the hotplug fork count equals the baseline `0dff13c` (`tests/hostnet/clat-hotplug-forks.sh`); the IPv4 ladder trace test returned `ok` comparing to `tests/ladder/ipv4-trace.golden` from `e504f61` (`tests/ladder/ipv4-trace.sh`) (measured: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/C1-review.md, round 3, 2026-11-24; R76b, D210).
    Clean flash and reset produce `ipv4v6`; keep-settings cases preserve the saved `ipv4`, a saved Auto choice or the absence of a value (`tests/firstboot/generic.sh`, `tests/firstboot/r7e-r75a.sh`, `tests/vm/10-firstboot.sh`, `tests/vm/40-reset.sh`; first-start and default rules, R7c, R73k).
12. **IP families in the VM:** the reviewer uses `<vm>` with real netifd, DHCPv6, odhcpd, dnsmasq, fw4, watchcat and nat46. The test protocol replays the modem state.
    - **CLAT end to end:** with the DNS64 and PLAT peer in `tests/vm/95-clat.sh`, the router and LAN client reach an IPv4 literal and an IPv4-only name over TCP, the resolver returns the operator prefix and the device is `464-wwan_6_4` in the cellular firewall zone (R76, R76a, R76d, R76e, R76h). The discovery query is counted on the cellular device and never on the LAN device, both with the normal routes and with an unscoped LAN default. A foreign host route remains in place and no temporary host route remains after a normal lookup (R76g). On the fresh setups checked for readiness, at least one of eight pings with a 2 s wait per ping replies (from: the `PING_OK` helper in `tests/vm/clat-lib.sh`, code baseline in the header).
    - **CLAT lifecycle:** in `tests/vm/95-clat.sh`, discovery succeeds on child-up when DNS64 returns during setup and on a later recovery cycle when it returns after setup. After a dead helper or repeated setups there is one owned translation with one set of rules, and `wwan` uptime continues through local recovery. A new session uses its own prefix, a standalone `ifdown` removes the translation, and a return to native IPv4 removes the old translation. IPv4-only and dual-stack sessions with no old translation start no check, including on dual-stack DHCPv6 child-up (R76b, R76c, R76f, R76i to R76k, R77).
    - **Translation recovery and coexistence:** `tests/vm/96-clat-verify.sh` exercises Tailscale routing rules alongside CLAT, a silent PLAT with a live helper, PLAT prefix movement, `ifdown` without a subsequent `ifup`, and a cellular /64 change. The same PLAT prefix leaves the current translation in place with one notice, a changed prefix restores IPv4 during an eligible cycle, and a child update restores IPv4 with an address from the new /64 without a session restart. The old address's rule and host route are gone, and CLAT cleanup preserves Tailscale's rule set (R76a, R76c, R76f, R76i).
    - **WireGuard over translation:** `tests/vm/96-clat-verify.sh` drops fragments at `wg0`. Its pass checks cover full-size tunnel traffic on a larger link, the full-size failure on the smaller link, and a smaller MTU that fits when set at both ends (tunnel and CLAT rules, R31, R76; live MTU question, O10).
    - **LAN IPv6 withdrawal and return:** in `tests/vm/96-lan6.sh` a live LAN peer captures withdrawal followed by a final RA, 1 to 5 s apart, with and without a second IPv6 default. The final RA carries router lifetime zero and no PIO; the old prefix in the first RA has preferred lifetime zero for netifd-deprecated addresses and at most 60 s for the watcher-only loss. The LAN is quiet in response to further solicitations and resumes advertisements when the assignment and route are usable again.
      The scenario also drives the real watcher against a QMI stand-in: unreadable settings preserve the LAN; a confirmed IPv6 loss withdraws it without an IPv4 action; the in-session return restores service. The VM counts QMI reads across a window and the scripted fixtures verify their per-pass order (R49a, R49b, R49f, R49h, R49i, R74b, R74c, R74f, R74g; from: `tests/vm/96-lan6.sh`, code baseline in the header).
    - **Other IP family results (expected):** a routed /64 and a delegated prefix each give the LAN native IPv6; a day of IPv4 alone gives no recurring odhcpd warning; WAN TCP, UDP port 500 and ESP do not reach the LAN client, while Packet Too Big and echo requests do. A kept 0.6.1 configuration gives no LAN IPv6 in forced IPv4 and gets LAN IPv6 with the same firewall protections after switching to Auto (R74 to R75a).
    - **Link repair and accounting results (expected):** when probe echoes are dropped and LAN TCP still flows over IPv6, the LAN retains its advertisements and watchcat and the ladder issue no silence-based restart. A detected withdrawal of the cellular route follows R74c and the event rule (R74f); another uplink gives no cellular probe answer. The separate watchcat entry retains its own behaviour, LAN events add no flash writes or pending UCI changes, and per-MAC native and translated traffic counts survive reload without duplicates (R14b, R14d, R14f, R44, R74c, R74e, R77, R77d).
13. **IP families on the owner's router (hardware: the owner's MF289F and IPv4-only SIM; the owner flashes):** with wired access, the previous image and the known IPv4 settings at hand, five timed starts in auto and five in forced IPv4, in turn, meet the start time rule by its statistic, and the report holds all ten times against D82 and the profile times apart, each auto start with one refused IPv6 call (R73i); the IPv4 lease and DNS work, the refusal gives no failed start and no Auto-APN line, any client retained beside a live call is cleared by the next start, the LAN has no global IPv6 and no router advertisement, and odhcpd gives no recurring warning over a day (R73b, R73e, R73m, R74b); watchcat over several full periods and the ladder past its grace restart nothing and give no EDGE (R14, R77); a session lost with `AT+CGATT=0` then `AT+CGATT=1` is restored in place as before (R49, R49b, R49c); after `reboot` LTE, the APN and the type of profile 1 are as before, and 20 `ifup wwan` in a row give no QMI client error in the log (R73f, R73m); WireGuard, Tailscale, the bot, the heartbeat and the signal log work overnight (R28, R77); a flash from 0.6.1 that keeps settings keeps `pdptype` `ipv4`, and forced IPv4 set in LuCI gives the start of the older image (R7c, R73); point 2 still passes. A button reset and the rollback test run only in a window the owner names.
    Compare an IPv6-refusing SIM against the surviving-family rule: Auto keeps the native IPv4 session, creates no IPv6 child and starts no CLAT (call order and refusal, R73a, R73b; translation gate, R76b).

A private image is accepted after points 1 to 4 and 6, the generic image after points 1 and 5, the ways to build after points 7 to 9, and a Tailscale image also after point 10. For an image released with Auto as default, the separate release rule applies (R73k); whether the hardware timing comparison also gates it is open (O12).

### Unverified

- CLAT in LuCI (R76h): the VM works without a CLAT option, but the browser's network pages have not been checked for any required CLAT interaction. Next check: start from default LuCI settings with the IPv6-only peer and confirm translated IPv4 works without enabling a CLAT option.
- CLAT runtime directory (R14h, R76i, R76j): the root ownership, no-link and 0700 protections of the two directories in the RAM state rule (R14h) are not established for `/var/run/lw-clat`; the current writers use `mkdir -p` (from: `qmi-edits/functions.sh` and `src/files/usr/sbin/lw-clat-check`, code baseline in the header). Next check: check the directory and its records when the path is a link or already has the wrong owner or mode, and determine whether to apply the same guards.
- Loss of just the IPv6 call while native IPv4 remains healthy on the MF289F (R49b, R49i, R74f): the modem's `--get-data-status` and `--get-current-settings` replies for an in-modem loss and for unreadable or ambiguous conditions have not been observed on hardware. Whether the same client returns without a netifd event, and how the LAN follows a changed prefix rather than retaining the old assignment, are also unverified. Next check: with a dual-stack SIM, capture both QMI replies and netifd's assignment across in-modem loss, an unreadable reply and a return with a changed prefix, while native IPv4 stays up.
- LuCI flash of the GitHub build artifact (GitHub build instructions, R55): a locally built image was flashed, but the artifact has no end-to-end flash evidence. Next check: download a generic artifact and complete backup, validation, flash and first boot from the documented LuCI workflow.
- The agent's flash from Windows (R59b): Windows ships `ssh` and `scp` (guess), but whether its `scp` copies to the router's dropbear, which has no SFTP server, is not checked (guess: it needs `scp -O`). Next check: one copy of an image from a Windows machine.
- Key types of `SSH_KEYS` (R61): the router's dropbear has taken `ssh-ed25519` logins since the first image; `ssh-rsa` and `ecdsa-sha2-nistp256` are taken to work with the dropbear of 25.12.6 (guess). Next check: one login with a key of each type in `<vm>`.
- Keyboard taps with privacy mode on (R37e, D64): seen working in Telegram Desktop; the Android app's source sends taps the same way; Telegram for iOS and Telegram Web are not checked, and a tap made while the user is replying to another message goes to that message instead, so the bot does not receive it. Next check: one tap from each app in use.
- Start without the modem and an interrupted first start on the hardware (R8, R8a): `<vm>` checks them on x86_64, not on the router's NAND flash. Next check: a restart with the SIM removed, with someone at the router.
- Repeated first-start actions (first-boot interruption rule, R8a): the VM erases its incomplete overlay, so the test does not exercise replay over retained partial state. Next check: replay the first-start scripts on a stand-in overlay with a subset of their writes already applied.
- A power cut after a button reset (R8a, R9): on one router the first power cut about 7 min after a completed start after a reset erased the overlay, while later ones in normal operation did not; `<vm>` with an ext4 overlay does not reproduce it, and a repeat on the router (reset, 7 min, power cut) did not either. The only difference seen: the reset came about 6 min after `sysupgrade -n`. Next check: save `dmesg` and `logread` at the next case.
- The modem after a power cut (R8): once, after a short power cut, the modem did not show up on USB, and another power cut helped; whether a restart from the ladder brings it back is unknown. Next check: `ls /sys/bus/usb/devices/2-1` at the next case, then `reboot`.
- The counter across a restart and the state write (R16a, R21, R21c): `<ladder>` does not restart the host or interrupt a write. Next check: a scenario with an interrupted write, and the first ladder restart on the router.
- LTE probe from EDGE, mode confirmation and the end of EDGE at a restart (R23, R24): `<ladder>` replaces `lw-fallback` with a stub. Next check: a test for `lw-fallback` with stand-ins for `ifup`, `uci` and the mode read.
- Class `account` on a real network (R18): the operator DNS answering with a used-up package has been seen, but the rule has not run with a used-up package. Next check: the class entry in the log the next time a package runs out.
- Class `sim` on a real modem (R18, R18d): the answer of `AT+CPIN?` has been seen only for a missing SIM (`SIM not inserted`), not for a SIM with a PIN. Next check: a SIM with the PIN turned on, with someone at the router. The same check covers `WWAN_PIN` (R13e): what the handler does with a PIN has been read in its code and seen in the log only for a SIM without one (`PIN verification is disabled`), never run on a SIM with a PIN.
- A profile's operator preset on a real network (R47d): the build checks the file with the router's reader and `<tests>` runs the balance flow of another operator with the stand-in modem, but no operator other than `29702` has answered a real query. Next check: `/balance` on a router built with `OP_*` keys for a SIM of another operator.
- APN fallback (R13b): on the test network an empty APN works, so nobody has seen the switch to the table. Next check: a SIM of an operator that rejects an empty APN, or a profile with a wrong APN on purpose.
- Error paths of a real modem (R11b, R13c, R26c, R26e): `<tests>` fakes a silent port with a stand-in, and a real modem hang may look different. Next check: the log entries at the next hang.
- A kernel identical to the official one (R1): `sysupgrade -T` does not prove it. Next check: compare the kernel checksum of the image with the official 25.12.6 image.
- Firewall directions of the tunnel (R31a): nobody has checked traffic from the LAN into the tunnel. Next check: a ping from a LAN device to the server's tunnel address must fail.
- Rotation of the persistent log on the router (R29): `<vm>` checks it, but nobody has looked at the `.old` file on a router. Next check: the size of `/root/openwrt.log*` after a week.
- The SMS centre's time zone for the owner's numbers (R46b, D65): the stamp is read as router-local time, and it was only observed with the phone and the router in the same zone. Next check: send an SMS from the owner's phone at home and abroad and compare the `timestamp` in `sms_tool recv` with `date`.
- Cost of the balance query (R35, R37): one query with the shipped preset did not change the balance, and no price list for it was found. Next check: the balance before and after the first weekly query.
- The `Internet back` message on a real failure (R40, R40b): `<tests>` fakes the repair state and the clock. Next check: the message in the chat after the next failure longer than 5 min.
- Signal thresholds (R39e): taken from typical LTE values, not from a site. Next check: the RSRP and SINR distribution from a week of signal log.
- The restore of the data session (R49c, D80) was measured after a detach made with `AT+CGATT=0` and `AT+CGATT=1`, not yet after a detach sent by the network. Next check: the next real detach, with `LOG_LEVEL='debug'`: the `lw-linkwatch` line with its outcome, the steps and the AT snapshot in `/root/openwrt.log`.
- The wait between USR2 and USR1 (R49c): 25 looks at the address are about 0.2 s on the router (one `ip -4 -o addr show` takes about 8 ms, measured), and that udhcpc finishes the release and netifd removes the address within them is not measured; if not, the 1 s wait covers it. Next check: with `LOG_LEVEL='debug'` the step `address of wwan0 released after N looks` or `still on wwan0 after 25 looks, waiting 1 s` shows which case the router is in.
- Lease renewal on a static or non-default device (lease renewal rule, R49c): the hardware check covers the default device path. Next check: compare the device identified in the watcher log with the renewal or static-address result when netifd publishes a different device.
- QMI clients left behind (R49a, R73e, Risks): calls of the watcher and of a start ended by their timeout. Next check: after a week with the watcher, `ifup wwan` still brings the interface up, and the netifd entries of the log have no QMI client allocation errors.
- The brake (R49e) and the escalation (R49d) on a real network: the tests use stand-ins for uqmi, `ip` and the signals to `udhcpc`. Next check: the lines in the log the next time a data plan runs out and the next time a restore does not work.
- Watchcat in passive mode (interface restart rule, R14): the watcher has not been observed acting independently after the ladder reaches its limit. Next check: keep the link down after the limit and verify the watchcat entries and interface restart.
- EDGE with LTE absent under a weak antenna, without the scan (R22, D82): one run with a good antenna was measured (interface up after 84 s, six failed starts, D82). Next check: the same `lw-fallback edge` with the antenna weakened and LTE made unavailable by a band mask of B32 alone (this writes modem NVRAM, so only with the owner's word), then the bands put back.
- A reply to the prompt for a name with the bot in privacy mode (R50, D87): `<tests>` delivers the reply with `reply_to_message` as the Bot API documents it, and `force_reply` is the documented way for a bot in privacy mode to get an answer in a group, but nobody has run it with the real bot. Next check: name a device from the chat in Telegram for Android and for Desktop, and read the answer of the bot.
- A link that is tapped late still works, also from Telegram's own browser (R65, R65a): how long a link stays valid and whether an older link dies when a new one is asked for are not documented and not tried. Next check: a link tapped after 1 h, after 24 h and after a router restart, from Telegram on iOS and Android with a Google, Microsoft and GitHub login, and an old link after a new one.
- Tailscale's status fields (the interface facts of the Tailscale section; R63d, R65b, R65c, R67a, R68, R68a, R69b, R69d): the field names, and `NeedsLogin` after an expiry or a removal, come from the reviews, not from the package 1.104.1, and the other name after a reset is assumed to differ from `HOSTNAME`; none was seen. Next check: the status of 1.104.1 in `<vm>` before and after a sign-in to a test account and after the router is removed there; point 10.
- Tailscale timing bounds on the real daemon (link, delivery and command timings, R65, R65a, R69a): the scripted timings use a stand-in CLI. Next check: time link creation, its bot delivery and the command reply from a fresh real daemon.
- Tailscale outbound isolation (firewall rule, R64a): the tests do not verify that the router cannot initiate connections to tailnet devices while it remains reachable from them on real hardware. Next check: exercise both directions against a signed-in peer.
- Tailscale netfilter and fw4 (firewall rules, R64, R64a): VM coverage does not verify this pairing on the target router. Next check: inspect the target nftables tables after sign-in and test the allowed and blocked directions.
- The settings after a sign-in on the real daemon (R64): `<vm>` checks them before a sign-in, and point 10 checks only the firewall part. Next check: at point 10, after the sign-in, `/etc/resolv.conf` and `ip route show table all` hold nothing from the account.
- Signing in with an auth key on the real daemon (R66a, R66b): only the stand-in CLI gives the answers for a key accepted, refused and without internet. Next check: a flash of the owner's router with a one-off key, then a button reset with the same key in the image.
- A LAN device answering through the route (R64a, R67, R67a, D155): offering the route, seeing it approved, the masquerading and a device that accepts only its own subnet are not tried. Next check: the optional step of point 10, with a Windows PC on a public network profile as the device.
- The expiry warning on the real daemon (R68): the stand-in gives the end date, and a real one 14 days ahead needs a router signed in for months. Next check: a router signed in to a test account with a shortened key expiry, which also shows the late warning (D160).
- The admin page's menu paths in the messages and MagicDNS (R72d, R65b, R71): the paths come from the UX review, which did not check them, and that `https://<name>` needs MagicDNS on is taken as known. Next check: the four menu paths and the MagicDNS setting in the admin page of a test account, and `https://<name>` with MagicDNS off.
- A tagged router is outside `autogroup:member`, so the policy of the tailnet access policy (R71b) gives it no reach (D149): read in Tailscale's docs (a device has tags or a user, not both), and seen on the owner's router on 2026-11-23 (its connection to the laptop rejected). Next check: with the tag applied, a shell on the router pings neither the laptop nor the phone (point 10).
- Old `tailscale` packages leaving the feed (R63b, D139): taken from the review, not seen. Next check: the feed's package list at the next `tailscale` update in 25.12.

- Tailscale setup instructions (documentation rules, R71, R71a): the policies have a check, but a new user has not followed the complete join and connection flow. Next check: observe a first-time user reaching both LuCI and SSH from the documented instructions.
- The IPv6 settings on the MF289F (R73j): whether a dual-stack modem report is usable, and what the link supplies when DHCPv6 is used, are unverified. Next check: a dual-stack SIM in Auto and an IPv6-only SIM through the DHCPv6 child, both at the debug level.
- Dual stack on the MF289F (R73a step 1, R49h, R49i, R74 to R74c): two calls at once, an IPv4 restore that leaves IPv6 up, and the LAN's IPv6 from a real /64 are tried on stand-ins and in `<vm>` only. Next check: a dual-stack SIM, with a forced detach and a LAN client.
- netifd's `uptime` of `wwan` as the birth of a session (R14g, R49d, R49h): netifd counts it from the change to up, a later update of the data does not reset it, and `/proc/uptime` shares its base on a router that never sleeps (from: slice 2b review, ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/P2b-review.md, decision 3); not checked on the router. Next check: two `ifstatus wwan` reads 5 s apart give the same `/proc/uptime` minus `uptime`, and an `ifup` gives a new one.
- Two calls kept apart on the MF289F (R49c, R49h, R73e): whether a stop on the IPv4 client (by handle, or with `0xffffffff` and the autoconnect flag uqmi sends with it) leaves the call on the IPv6 client up is not measured; until it is, the wildcard stop is not used while another call may exist. Next check: a dual-stack SIM, a forced detach, IPv6 traffic that keeps flowing through the stop, and a reconnect with the flags of the autoconnect rule (R73g).
- A SIM that refuses the dual-stack request as a whole (R73a steps 2 and 3): only the stand-in modem gives this answer. Next check: such a SIM, if one turns up.
- Translated IPv4 on the router (R76, R77): that a ping bound to `wwan0` fails on an IPv6-only session and one through the translation device works is taken from the routing, not seen. Next check: an IPv6-only SIM (for example of Orange or Bouygues in France), with pings through both devices.
- WireGuard over a real CLAT path (tunnel and CLAT rules, R31, R76; MTU question, O10): the VM tests unfragmented traffic at different MTUs, but the live path size and behaviour of the operator are unknown. Next check: measure bidirectional unfragmented traffic, Packet Too Big handling and NAT64 UDP mapping lifetime against WireGuard keepalive with an IPv6-only operator and unchanged image defaults.
- Tailscale beside CLAT on a live operator (R75, R76d, R76i): the VM compares coexisting rules and cleanup without signing in to a tailnet (from: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/C2-review-round1.md, 2026-11-25). Next check: use a real IPv6-only session with Tailscale signed in and verify both translated and tailnet traffic through one CLAT rebuild.
- PLAT failover on a live operator (R76a, R76c): the VM covers a silent and moved PLAT. Live failover with an unchanged prefix and the effect of the operator resolver's DNS64 TTL and cache are unknown. Next check: capture the translated probes and successive DNS64 answers across a real failover.
- Renumbering with CLAT on the MF289F (R76c): the test protocol and DHCPv6 client produce the update in the VM. A real QMI session has not shown whether netifd emits that update, how often a /64 changes in place, or whether the helper selects its address outside the reported /64 set. Next check: record real `wwan_6` events, the validated prefix set, the CLAT address and IPv4 traffic across renumbering.
- Operator DNS64 prefix length and `nslookup` (R76a, O6): the prefix actually supplied, the command response and its routing have not been checked on a live IPv6-only operator. Next check: capture the AAAA lookup and result on that SIM.
- PLAT routing of the CLAT address (R76, O6): return traffic to the extra CLAT address is uncovered on a live operator. Next check: capture the returns on the same SIM, separately from traffic to the router's own IPv6 address.
- Translation readiness on the MF289F (CLAT reachability, R76; operator question, O6): the time from interface-up to first reply is unknown on hardware (VM evidence: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/C1-clat-report.md, Rework 2). Next check: repeat fresh setups at rest and under load on an IPv6-only SIM and record time to the first reply.
- Repair lock and traffic sample paths (R21a, R77e, O8): behaviour under interference at `/tmp/wwan-recover.lock` and `/tmp/wwan-recover.run` is uncovered. Next check: exercise lock takeover and sample rejection with unexpected file types and owners before moving both paths.
- Temporary DNS64 `/128` after an interruption on the MF289F (R76g, O7): the recovery record and matching cleanup were exercised with stand-ins (from: ../lonewrt-notes/ipv6/C2-review.md, 2026-11-25). Next check: interrupt an IPv6-only lookup after route installation, change the gateway on the same device and verify later cleanup and the next lookup.
- DNS64 routing with a second uplink, UID or mark policy, or a concurrent replacement of the same host route (R76g, O7). Next check: trace the actual lookup and LAN packets during those cases on the bench. Try an operator advertising a link-local DNS server to confirm the refusal and record what fallback is available (R76a, O6).
- A second CLAT setting up while this translation is removed (R76i, O9). Next check: pause the second setup after rule installation and before its marker, then verify that its rules and the shared local lookup survive.
- The router's services over IPv6 or NAT64 (R31, R34, R42, R63, R41, R77): Telegram, healthchecks.io, Tailscale's servers, the WireGuard server and speedtest.net are reached by name, so they should work over IPv6 or the translation (guess). Next check: the same SIM, with a message, a heartbeat, a Tailscale connection, a WireGuard handshake and a speed test.
- Return traffic to the LAN's addresses (R74): that the operator routes the whole /64 to the router, so a LAN device's address gets its answers, is the premise of RFC 7278, not seen. Next check: a dual-stack or IPv6-only SIM and a LAN client reaching an IPv6 service.
- The type of profile 1 after a cut-off start (R73f): the stand-in models the modem's memory; what the modem does after a power cut with another stored type, and whether such a type blocks registration (guess, expert review finding 4), are not seen. Next check: a power cut during a start, in a window the owner names.

- IPv6 accounting in `/devices` (device counters, R44): per-MAC counts across prefix changes and counter reload are unverified. Next check: generate known native and translated traffic from a LAN client, change its prefix and compare the reported counters after a reload.
